### M2-W061 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(vi), the structure. A $\Spinc$ structure is given by lifts to $\Spinc(12)$ of the gluing data of the tangent bundle in local frames \cite[p.~41]{Nicolaescu}, and $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}$ by the lifts $\xi(h_{\alpha\beta})$ of the gluing data $h_{\alpha\beta}$ in frames that are unitary for $\widetilde{J}$ \cite[Proposition~1.3.10 and pp.~44--45]{Nicolaescu}. Take frames made of a unitary frame along the fibre, the frame $\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$, and a unitary frame of the base; they are unitary for $\widetilde{J}$, and in them $h_{\alpha\beta}=1\oplus u_{\alpha\beta}$, with $1$ on the directions of time and of space and $u_{\alpha\beta}$ unitary on the others. For two spaces $V_{1},V_{2}$ at right angles the products $xy$ of $x\in\mathrm{Spin}(V_{1})$ and $y\in\mathrm{Spin}(V_{2})$ give a homomorphism from $\Spinc(V_{1})\times\Spinc(V_{2})$ to $\Spinc(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})$, written $(x,y)\mapsto x\cdot y$, that lies over the sum of the two rotations, and $\xi(\omega_{1}\oplus\omega_{2})=\xi(\omega_{1})\cdot\xi(\omega_{2})$. So the gluing data of $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}$ are $1\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\beta})$. For $\widetilde{J}'$ the unitary frames are the same with $(\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3})a_{\alpha}$ in the place of $\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$, where $a_{\alpha}$ is a rotation of the directions of time and of space that depends only on the point of $T\times S^{3}$; the gluing data are $a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\beta}\oplus u_{\alpha\beta}$, and those of $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}'}$ are $\xi(a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\beta})\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\beta})$. Let $\hat a_{\alpha}$ be a lift of $a_{\alpha}$ to $\mathrm{Spin}(4)$ \cite[Proposition~1.33]{Hatcher}. Then $\xi(a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\beta})=\hat a_{\alpha}^{-1}\hat a_{\beta}\,z_{\alpha\beta}$ with $z_{\alpha\beta}$ in the circle that lies over the identity rotation, and the $z_{\alpha\beta}$ are the gluing data of a line that is pulled back from $T\times S^{3}$. The second cohomology group of $T\times S^{3}=S^{1}\times S^{3}$ is zero by the K\"unneth formula \cite[Theorem~3.15]{Hatcher}, so that this line is trivial \cite[Remark~1.1.3]{Nicolaescu}: $z_{\alpha\beta}=\zeta_{\alpha}^{-1}\zeta_{\beta}$ for functions $\zeta_{\alpha}$ with values in the circle. With $b_{\alpha}=\hat a_{\alpha}\zeta_{\alpha}$, a lift of $a_{\alpha}$ to $\Spinc(4)$, the gluing data of $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}'}$ are $(b_{\alpha}^{-1}b_{\beta})\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\beta})=(b_{\alpha}\cdot1)^{-1}\bigl(1\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\beta})\bigr)(b_{\beta}\cdot1)$: they are the gluing data of $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}$, carried from the one system of frames to the other by lifts of the changes of frame. The two are one $\Spinc$ structure: $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}'}=\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}$.
```
- **Place:** `states 500`; the seventh step of the proof of Proposition `prop:whole`
- **Step:** S04d
- **Rests on:** M2-W053; M2-W020 and M2-W014 (the groups of the circle and of $S^{3}$); Verified References V-M2-2, V-M2-4, V-M2-7, V-M2-9, V-M2-10
- **Mark:** Verified references, READ at source: V-M2-10 (Nicolaescu: the Clifford algebra and its relations, p.27; $\mathrm{Spin}(V)$, $\rho_{x}(v)=xvx^{-1}$, its kernel $\{\pm1\}$, a covering map, p.35; $\Spinc(V)=(\mathrm{Spin}(V)\times S^{1})/\Z_{2}$, p.38; the definition of $\xi$ in the "Idea of Proof" of Proposition 1.3.10, p.39; a spin$^{c}$ structure as lifts of the gluing data, p.41; the structure induced by an almost complex structure, pp.44–45), V-M2-9 (Remark 1.1.3), V-M2-7 (the lifting criterion), V-M2-2 and V-M2-4 (the groups of $S^{1}$ and $S^{3}$ and the Künneth formula). CHECKED: certificate `L4_S1S3_degree_two` (the ranks in degree two add up to $0$); script part (7). Hand steps, in full. (a) The frames: $\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$ is one frame over the whole of $\widetilde{M}$, so the gluing data are $1$ on these directions; with $J_{0}\partial_{s}=e_{1}$, $J_{0}e_{2}=e_{3}$ it is unitary for $J_{0}$ in the lengths in which it has length one and is at right angles. (b) The product: a unit vector of $V_{1}$ is a unit vector of $V_{1}\oplus V_{2}$, so an even product of unit vectors of $V_{1}$ lies in $\mathrm{Spin}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})$ (p.35); vectors $u\in V_{1}$, $v\in V_{2}$ have $uv=-vu$ by the relations (p.27), so an even product of vectors of $V_{1}$ commutes with every vector of $V_{2}$ and with every product of them; hence $(x,y)\mapsto xy$ is a homomorphism, and $\rho_{xy}$ is $\rho_{x}$ on $V_{1}$ and $\rho_{y}$ on $V_{2}$; with the circles multiplied, $[x,z]\cdot[y,z']=[xy,zz']$ is well defined on $\Spinc$ (p.38) and a homomorphism. (c) $\xi$ of a sum: for paths $\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}$ of unitary maps from $1$ to $\omega_{1},\omega_{2}$, the path $\gamma_{1}\oplus\gamma_{2}$ goes from $1$ to $\omega_{1}\oplus\omega_{2}$, the product of the lifts of $\gamma_{1}$ and $\gamma_{2}$ starts at $1$ and lies over it, so is its lift, and the product of the lifts of the two determinants is the lift of the determinant of the sum; so $\xi(\omega_{1}\oplus\omega_{2})=\xi(\omega_{1})\cdot\xi(\omega_{2})$ by the definition of p.39, and $\xi(1)=1$. (d) The frames for $\widetilde{J}'$: over a contractible open set $W$ of $T\times S^{3}$, $(\partial_{s},J_{0}'\partial_{s})$ spans a plane carried to itself by $J_{0}'$, its orthogonal complement is an oriented plane bundle over $W$, trivial there, with a unit section $f$, and $(\partial_{s},J_{0}'\partial_{s},f,J_{0}'f)$ is a frame of length one at right angles, unitary for $J_{0}'$ and of the handedness of $J_{0}$; it is $(\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3})a$ for a rotation $a$ depending on the point of $W$; a cover of $\widetilde{M}$ by contractible open sets $U_{\alpha}$, each lying over one such $W$, is taken, and $a_{\alpha}$ is $a$ read at the point of $T\times S^{3}$ below; the changes of frame $a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\beta}$ are unitary. (e) $W$ is contractible, so path-connected with trivial fundamental group, and $a$ lifts through the covering $\mathrm{Spin}(4)\to SO(4)$ (V-M2-7; p.35). (f) $\xi(a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\beta})$ and $\hat a_{\alpha}^{-1}\hat a_{\beta}$ both lie over $a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\beta}$, so their quotient lies over the identity; the elements of $\Spinc(4)$ over the identity are the $[\pm1,z]$, a circle whose elements commute with everything; both families satisfy the cocycle condition ($\xi$ is a homomorphism; the second is a quotient of functions), hence so do the $z_{\alpha\beta}$, which are read from $T\times S^{3}$. (g) $H^{2}(S^{1}\times S^{3};\Z)$: by Theorem 3.15 it is the sum of $H^{i}(S^{1})\otimes H^{2-i}(S^{3})$, $i=0,1,2$, each zero by the groups of M2-W014 and M2-W020; the line with the gluing data $z_{\alpha\beta}$ on the compact $T\times S^{3}$ has class zero, so it is trivial (V-M2-9); a section $(t_{\alpha})$ of it with no zero gives $\zeta_{\alpha}=t_{\alpha}/\lvert t_{\alpha}\rvert$ with $z_{\alpha\beta}=\zeta_{\alpha}^{-1}\zeta_{\beta}$ (or its inverse, by the convention for gluing data; the argument is the same). (h) $(b_{\alpha}^{-1}b_{\beta})\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\beta})=(b_{\alpha}\cdot1)^{-1}(1\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\beta}))(b_{\beta}\cdot1)$, the product being a homomorphism of the product group. **Identification steps** (CLAUDE.md §5; the note to V-M2-10): (1) the source writes a spin$^{c}$ structure in one system of local frames and does not say when two families of lifts in two systems of frames are one structure; it is taken here that gluing data carried to another system of frames by lifts of the changes of frame give the same structure; (2) the source gives $\xi$ in an "Idea of Proof" and leaves to the reader that it is well defined; (3) the cover of $\widetilde{M}$ and the frames are taken so that the pieces of $\widetilde{J}$ other than $J_{0}'$ have the same unitary frames for both structures; (4) the family of complex structures carried round time is the same in $\widetilde{J}$ and $\widetilde{J}'$

### M2-W062 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(vi), the spinor bundle. The manifold $T\times S^{3}$ has an almost complex structure, $J_{0}$, hence a $\Spinc$ structure, and exactly one, its lines being trivial \cite[Proposition~1.3.15 and Remark~1.1.3]{Nicolaescu}. The structures that $J_{0}$ and $J_{0}'$ induce on $T\times S^{3}$ are therefore the same, and their spinor bundles, the $(0,*)$-forms of $J_{0}$ and of $J_{0}'$ on $T\times S^{3}$ \cite[p.~77]{Nicolaescu}, are isomorphic. The $(0,*)$-forms of $\widetilde{J}$ and of $\widetilde{J}'$ on $\widetilde{M}$ are the tensor products of the pull-backs of these with the $(0,*)$-forms of the fibre and of the base, which are the same for both; the pull-back of an isomorphism between the two bundles on $T\times S^{3}$, tensored with the identity on the other factors and on $N$, carries the one spinor bundle to the other and covers the identity of $\widetilde{M}$. It carries lift to lift: the fold covers the identity of $T\times S^{3}$, so that $\iota^{*}$ is the identity on the forms pulled back from $T\times S^{3}$ and commutes with a map pulled back from there. This is (vi).
```

### M2-W064 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
Write $R=S^{3}\times\CPtwo\times\Stwo$, so that $\widetilde{M}=Y\times R$, and $p_{Y}$, $p_{R}$ for the two projections. On a compact manifold two lines with the same first Chern class are isomorphic, and the first Chern class of a tensor product is the sum \cite[Remark~1.1.3]{Nicolaescu}. The cohomology groups of $\CPtwo$, of $\Stwo$ and of $S^{3}$ with integer coefficients are finitely generated and free (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:basespace}), and so, by the K\"unneth formula \cite[Theorem~3.15]{Hatcher}, are those of $R$, which are $\Z$ in degree $0$, zero in degree $1$ and $\Z\oplus\Z$ in degree $2$, one summand from $\CPtwo$ and one from $\Stwo$ (the same proof). The cohomology group of $Y$ in degree $0$ is $\Z$, spanned by $1$, $Y$ being path-connected \cite[Proposition~2.7, Theorem~3.2 and p.~207]{Hatcher}. By the K\"unneth formula \cite[Theorem~3.15 and p.~214]{Hatcher} every class of degree two on $\widetilde{M}$ is therefore $p_{Y}^{*}a+p_{R}^{*}b$ for one class $a$ on $Y$ and one class $b$ on $R$, and $a$ and $b$ are its restrictions to $Y\times\{r\}$ and to $\{y\}\times R$. So a line $N$ on $\widetilde{M}$ is isomorphic to $p_{Y}^{*}N_{Y}\otimes p_{R}^{*}N_{R}$, $N_{Y}$ and $N_{R}$ being its restrictions to $Y\times\{r\}$ and to $\{y\}\times R$. If the integers of $N$ on $\CPtwo$ and on $\Stwo$ are zero, then $N_{R}$ is trivial and $N$ is isomorphic to $p_{Y}^{*}N_{Y}$; and $p_{Y}^{*}N_{Y}$ and $p_{Y}^{*}N'_{Y}$ are isomorphic only if $N_{Y}$ and $N'_{Y}$ are.
```

- **Mark:** Verified references, READ at source: V-M2-9 (Nicolaescu, Remark 1.1.3: for compact $M$ "an isomorphism of groups" from the lines to the second cohomology group), V-M2-12 (Hatcher, Proposition 2.7, Theorem 3.2, the unit of the cup product, the cross product), V-M2-2 (Theorem 3.15), V-M2-4. Hand steps, in full: $Y$, $R$ and $\widetilde{M}$ are smooth manifolds, the joining of $Y$ being by a linear map of the torus and $\widetilde{M}$ a product, and they are compact ($E$ and the spheres are compact, $\CPtwo$ is compact, and $Y$ is the image of the compact $E\times[0,3L]$); the groups of $R$: by the hand steps of M2-W020 the groups of $\CPtwo$, $\Stwo$ and $S^{3}$ are free and finitely generated, so by Theorem 3.15, applied twice, those of $R$ are, with rank $1$ in degree $0$, $0$ in degree $1$ and $2$ in degree $2$; $Y$ is path-connected, $E$ and $\R$ being so, so $H_{0}(Y)\approx\Z$ (Proposition 2.7), and by Theorem 3.2 at $n=0$ the group $H^{0}(Y;\Z)$ is $\mathrm{Hom}(H_{0}(Y),\Z)=\Z$, the group $H_{-1}$ being zero; it is spanned by the class $1$, which takes the value $1$ on each point (p.207); the same for $R$; Theorem 3.15 with $R$ as the factor whose groups are free: in degree two the cross product is an isomorphism from $H^{2}(Y)\otimes H^{0}(R)\,\oplus\,H^{1}(Y)\otimes H^{1}(R)\,\oplus\,H^{0}(Y)\otimes H^{2}(R)$, the middle term is zero, and $a\times1=p_{Y}^{*}a$, $1\times b=p_{R}^{*}b$ (p.214, and $1$ the unit); the inclusion $i$ of $Y\times\{r\}$ has $p_{Y}i$ the identity and $p_{R}i$ a constant map, through which a class of degree two pulls back to zero, so $i^{*}(p_{Y}^{*}a+p_{R}^{*}b)=a$, and likewise for $\{y\}\times R$; the classes of $N$ and of $p_{Y}^{*}N_{Y}\otimes p_{R}^{*}N_{R}$ are then equal, and the lines isomorphic; the integers of $N$ on $\CPtwo$ and on $\Stwo$ are the classes of its restrictions to $\CPtwo$ and to $\Stwo$ at one point of the other factors, and they are the two integers of $b$ in $\Z\oplus\Z$ (M2-W020: one summand from $\CPtwo$ and one from $\Stwo$); if both are zero then $b=0$ and $N_{R}$ is trivial; restriction to $Y\times\{r\}$ carries $p_{Y}^{*}N_{Y}$ to $N_{Y}$. The hypothesis of Theorem 3.15 that the factors are CW complexes is not restated: for $R$ the cell structures are those of M2-W020's mark; for $Y$ no cell structure is given here, and the statement is used on the book's own sentence that the hypothesis "will be shown to be unnecessary in §4.1" (the note to V-M2-2), whose proof was not read: **an identification step**. Changed from M2-W057 (fresh reader, P208): the statement said of the groups of $R$ in degrees $0$ and $1$, and of their being free, "(the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:basespace})", where that proof (M2-W020) has the groups of the three factors and the group of $R$ in degree two only; the sentence now says which is from that proof and which is by the Künneth formula. The mark names smoothness, the CW hypothesis for $Y$, and no longer says "as in M2-W025". Nothing else changed

### M2-W065 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(ii). The determinant line of $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}$ is $K^{-1}$, the top exterior power of the tangent bundle of $\widetilde{M}$ taken with $\widetilde{J}$ \cite[pp.~44--45]{Nicolaescu}; it is the tensor product of the top exterior powers of the four pieces of $\widetilde{J}$. Its integer on $\CPtwo$ is three and on $\Stwo$ two (M1, Proposition~7.3 and Section~7.4), the other pieces being constant along them; on a fibre $E$ it is zero, the canonical line of $E$ being trivial (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}), and with it its dual, the tangent line, and the other pieces being constant along $E$. Every $\Spinc$ structure on $\widetilde{M}$ is $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes N$ for a line $N$ that is unique up to isomorphism, and its determinant line is $K^{-1}\otimes N^{2}$ \cite[p.~42 and Proposition~1.3.15]{Nicolaescu}, with the integers $3+2n_{1}$, $2+2n_{2}$ and $2n_{3}$, where $n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}$ are the integers of $N$ on $\CPtwo$, on $\Stwo$ and on the fibre. These are the integers three, two and six of M1 exactly when $(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})=(0,0,3)$ (\texttt{L4\_integers}). By the step above such a line is the pull-back of a line on $Y$ whose restriction to the fibre has degree three, lines on $Y$ that are not isomorphic having pull-backs that are not, and by (i) there are sixteen of them. This is (ii).
```

### M2-W066 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii) and (iv). Let $N=p_{Y}^{*}N_{Y}$. A lift of the fold to $N_{Y}$ (Definition~\ref{def:time}) pulls back to a lift of the fold to $N$ (Definition~\ref{def:unfolded}) with the same signs, and a lift of the fold to $N$ restricts, over $Y\times\{r\}$, which the fold carries to itself, to a lift of the fold to $N_{Y}$ with the same signs. By Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}(iii) and~(iv) a line of degree three on $E$ has a lift of the fold with the signs $+1,+1,+1,-1$, the lift of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}(iv); by Proposition~\ref{prop:time}(i) there is a line on $Y$ with a lift of the fold that restricts to it, with those signs on the corner circles; its restriction to the fibre has degree three, so that it is one of the sixteen lines of (i); and by Proposition~\ref{prop:time}(ii) any two lines on $Y$ with such lifts are isomorphic. This is (iii). For (iv): the $(0,1)$-forms of $\widetilde{J}$ are the direct sum of those along the fibre and those of time, of space and of the base, and the exterior algebra of a direct sum $A\oplus B$ is the tensor product of the exterior algebras of $A$ and of $B$, by $a\otimes b\mapsto a\wedge b$. So $\Lambda^{0,*}_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes N$ is the tensor product of the $(0,*)$-forms along the fibre with values in $N$, which is the pull-back of the spinor bundle of the fibre carried round time (Definition~\ref{def:spinortime}), and the $(0,*)$-forms of time, of space and of the base. The fold of $\widetilde{M}$ multiplies the directions along the fibre by $-1$ and is the identity on the others, so that $\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda$ is the lift of the fold of Definition~\ref{def:spinortime} on the first factor and the identity on the second.
```

### M2-W067 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(vi), the structure. A $\Spinc$ structure is given by lifts to $\Spinc(12)$ of the gluing data of the tangent bundle in local frames \cite[p.~41]{Nicolaescu}, and $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}$ by the lifts $\xi(h_{\alpha\alpha'})$ of the gluing data $h_{\alpha\alpha'}$ in frames that are unitary for $\widetilde{J}$ \cite[Proposition~1.3.10 and pp.~44--45]{Nicolaescu}. Take frames made of a unitary frame along the fibre, the frame $\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$, and a unitary frame of the base; they are unitary for $\widetilde{J}$, and in them $h_{\alpha\alpha'}=1\oplus u_{\alpha\alpha'}$, with $1$ on the directions of time and of space and $u_{\alpha\alpha'}$ unitary on the others. For two spaces $V_{1},V_{2}$ at right angles the products $xy$ of $x\in\mathrm{Spin}(V_{1})$ and $y\in\mathrm{Spin}(V_{2})$ give a homomorphism from $\Spinc(V_{1})\times\Spinc(V_{2})$ to $\Spinc(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})$, written $(x,y)\mapsto x\cdot y$, that lies over the sum of the two rotations, and $\xi(g_{1}\oplus g_{2})=\xi(g_{1})\cdot\xi(g_{2})$ for unitary maps $g_{1}$ of $V_{1}$ and $g_{2}$ of $V_{2}$. So the gluing data of $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}$ are $1\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\alpha'})$. For $\widetilde{J}'$ the unitary frames are the same with $(\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3})a_{\alpha}$ in the place of $\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$, where $a_{\alpha}$ is a rotation of the directions of time and of space that depends only on the point of $T\times S^{3}$; the gluing data are $a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\alpha'}\oplus u_{\alpha\alpha'}$, and those of $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}'}$ are $\xi(a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\alpha'})\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\alpha'})$. Let $\hat a_{\alpha}$ be a lift of $a_{\alpha}$ to $\mathrm{Spin}(4)$ \cite[Proposition~1.33]{Hatcher}. Then $\xi(a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\alpha'})=\hat a_{\alpha}^{-1}\hat a_{\alpha'}\,z_{\alpha\alpha'}$ with $z_{\alpha\alpha'}$ in the circle that lies over the identity rotation, and the $z_{\alpha\alpha'}$ are the gluing data of a line that is pulled back from $T\times S^{3}$. The second cohomology group of $T\times S^{3}=S^{1}\times S^{3}$ is zero by the K\"unneth formula \cite[Theorem~3.15]{Hatcher}, so that this line is trivial \cite[Remark~1.1.3]{Nicolaescu}: $z_{\alpha\alpha'}=\zeta_{\alpha}^{-1}\zeta_{\alpha'}$ for functions $\zeta_{\alpha}$ with values in the circle. With $b_{\alpha}=\hat a_{\alpha}\zeta_{\alpha}$, a lift of $a_{\alpha}$ to $\Spinc(4)$, the gluing data of $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}'}$ are $(b_{\alpha}^{-1}b_{\alpha'})\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\alpha'})=(b_{\alpha}\cdot1)^{-1}\bigl(1\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\alpha'})\bigr)(b_{\alpha'}\cdot1)$: they are the gluing data of $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}$, carried from the one system of frames to the other by lifts of the changes of frame. The two are one $\Spinc$ structure: $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}'}=\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}$.
```
- **Place:** `states 500`; the seventh step of the proof of Proposition `prop:whole`, in the place of M2-W061, which it replaces

- **Rests on:** M2-W053; M2-W020 and M2-W014 (the groups of the circle and of $S^{3}$); Verified References V-M2-2, V-M2-4, V-M2-7, V-M2-9, V-M2-10

CHECKED: certificate `L4_S1S3_degree_two` (the ranks in degree two add up to $0$); script `papers/M2/checks/S04d_whole.py`, part (8). Hand steps, in full. (a) The frames: $\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$ is one frame over the whole of $\widetilde{M}$, so the gluing data are $1$ on these directions; with $J_{0}\partial_{s}=e_{1}$, $J_{0}e_{2}=e_{3}$ it is unitary for $J_{0}$ in the lengths in which it has length one and is at right angles. (b) The product: a unit vector of $V_{1}$ is a unit vector of $V_{1}\oplus V_{2}$, so an even product of unit vectors of $V_{1}$ lies in $\mathrm{Spin}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})$ (p.35); vectors $u\in V_{1}$, $v\in V_{2}$ have $uv=-vu$ by the relations (p.27), so an even product of vectors of $V_{1}$ commutes with every vector of $V_{2}$ and with every product of them; hence $(x,y)\mapsto xy$ is a homomorphism, and $\rho_{xy}$ is $\rho_{x}$ on $V_{1}$ and $\rho_{y}$ on $V_{2}$; with the circles multiplied, $[x,z]\cdot[y,z']=[xy,zz']$ is well defined on $\Spinc$ (p.38) and a homomorphism. (c) $\xi$ of a sum: for paths $\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}$ of unitary maps from $1$ to $g_{1},g_{2}$, the path $\gamma_{1}\oplus\gamma_{2}$ goes from $1$ to $g_{1}\oplus g_{2}$, the product of the lifts of $\gamma_{1}$ and $\gamma_{2}$ starts at $1$ and lies over it, so is its lift, and the product of the lifts of the two determinants is the lift of the determinant of the sum; so $\xi(g_{1}\oplus g_{2})=\xi(g_{1})\cdot\xi(g_{2})$ by the definition of p.39, and $\xi(1)=1$. (d) The frames for $\widetilde{J}'$: over a contractible open set $W$ of $T\times S^{3}$, $(\partial_{s},J_{0}'\partial_{s})$ spans a plane carried to itself by $J_{0}'$, its orthogonal complement is an oriented plane bundle over $W$, trivial there, with a unit section $f$, and $(\partial_{s},J_{0}'\partial_{s},f,J_{0}'f)$ is a frame of length one at right angles, unitary for $J_{0}'$ and of the handedness of $J_{0}$; it is $(\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3})a$ for a rotation $a$ depending on the point of $W$; a cover of $\widetilde{M}$ by contractible open sets $U_{\alpha}$, each lying over one such $W$, is taken, and $a_{\alpha}$ is $a$ read at the point of $T\times S^{3}$ below; the changes of frame $a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\alpha'}$ are unitary. (e) $W$ is contractible, so path-connected with trivial fundamental group, and locally path-connected, being an open subset of a manifold, and $a$ lifts through the covering $\mathrm{Spin}(4)\to SO(4)$ (V-M2-7; p.35). (f) $\xi(a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\alpha'})$ and $\hat a_{\alpha}^{-1}\hat a_{\alpha'}$ both lie over $a_{\alpha}^{-1}a_{\alpha'}$, so their quotient lies over the identity; the elements of $\Spinc(4)$ over the identity are the $[\pm1,z]$, a circle whose elements commute with everything; both families satisfy the cocycle condition ($\xi$ is a homomorphism; the second is a quotient of functions), hence so do the $z_{\alpha\alpha'}$, which are read from $T\times S^{3}$. (g) $H^{2}(S^{1}\times S^{3};\Z)$: by Theorem 3.15 it is the sum of $H^{i}(S^{1})\otimes H^{2-i}(S^{3})$, $i=0,1,2$, each zero by the groups of M2-W014 and M2-W020; the line with the gluing data $z_{\alpha\alpha'}$ on the compact $T\times S^{3}$ has class zero, so it is trivial (V-M2-9); a section $(t_{\alpha})$ of it with no zero gives $\zeta_{\alpha}=t_{\alpha}/\lvert t_{\alpha}\rvert$ with $z_{\alpha\alpha'}=\zeta_{\alpha}^{-1}\zeta_{\alpha'}$ (or its inverse, by the convention for gluing data; the argument is the same). (h) $(b_{\alpha}^{-1}b_{\alpha'})\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\alpha'})=(b_{\alpha}\cdot1)^{-1}(1\cdot\xi(u_{\alpha\alpha'}))(b_{\alpha'}\cdot1)$, the product being a homomorphism of the product group. **Identification steps** (CLAUDE.md §5; the note to V-M2-10): (1) the source writes a spin$^{c}$ structure in one system of local frames and does not say when two families of lifts in two systems of frames are one structure; it is taken here that gluing data carried to another system of frames by lifts of the changes of frame give the same structure; (2) the source gives $\xi$ in an "Idea of Proof" and leaves to the reader that it is well defined; (3) the cover of $\widetilde{M}$ and the frames are taken so that the pieces of $\widetilde{J}$ other than $J_{0}'$ have the same unitary frames for both structures; (4) the family of complex structures carried round time is the same in $\widetilde{J}$ and $\widetilde{J}'$. Changed from M2-W061 (fresh reader, P208): the second index of the cover is $\alpha'$ where M2-W061 had the source's $\beta$, which in this proof is the function of M2-W055 and M2-W056; the unitary maps are $g_{1},g_{2}$ where it had the source's $\omega_{1},\omega_{2}$, M1 using that letter in $\theta_{\omega}$; the mark names the hypothesis "locally path-connected" of the lifting criterion and points to part (8) of the script, part (7), which it named, checking nothing of $S^{1}\times S^{3}$. Nothing else changed

### M2-W069 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the step of the unfolded geometry]\label{def:step}
Let $\widetilde{M}$ be the unfolded geometry (Definition~\ref{def:unfolded}), and let $\widetilde{J}$ be the almost complex structure of the unfolded geometry (Definition~\ref{def:spinorwhole}) whose family of complex structures carried round time is the one that the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, give, with the coordinates $z_{s}=e^{-\kappa s}y+ie^{\kappa s}x$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}). The \emph{step of the unfolded geometry} is the map
\[
\tau\colon\widetilde{M}\to\widetilde{M},\qquad \tau(p,s,r)=(M_{0}p,\;s-L,\;r),
\]
for $p\in E$, $s\in\R$ and $r\in S^{3}\times\CPtwo\times\Stwo$: on the fibre it is the step $M_{0}$ of the drive, the linear map of its matrix (M1, Section~4.1); it carries the fibre at the time $s$ to the fibre at the time $s-L$, $L$ being the length of one step; on space and on the base it is the identity. It is the join of M1 read as a map. In M1, Proposition~5.8, the lengths are measured with ``the fibre at $s+L$ being joined to the fibre at $s$ by the step'', and $\tau$ carries the point $(p,s+L)$ to the point $(M_{0}p,s)$, to which that join joins it; in $\widetilde{M}$, whose time circle is three steps long (M1, Corollary~7.8), the two are different points. That one step of the drive acts on the whole geometry by the map $\tau$ is a reading and is not derived: it is the fifth choice of this paper. Its ground is M1's own join, and not a clause of M1, Definition~3.2. For complex line bundles $N$ and $N'$ on $\widetilde{M}$, a \emph{lift of the step} from $N$ to $N'$ is a continuous map $\nu\colon N\to N'$ that carries the fibre of $N$ over each point $m$ of $\widetilde{M}$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre of $N'$ over $\tau(m)$. The \emph{lift of the step} that $\nu$ defines, from the spinor bundle of the unfolded geometry for $\widetilde{J}$ and $N$ to that for $\widetilde{J}$ and $N'$, is $(\tau^{-1})^{*}\otimes\nu$, where $(\tau^{-1})^{*}$ is the pull-back of the $(0,*)$-forms by the inverse of $\tau$; it carries the $(0,*)$-forms at $m$ to $(0,*)$-forms at $\tau(m)$, since $\tau$ keeps $\widetilde{J}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}(i)). That one step of the drive acts on a spinor in this form is part of the same choice: it is the form of the third and fourth choices (Definitions~\ref{def:lift} and~\ref{def:spinorwhole}) taken for the step. Clause~(b) of M1, Definition~3.2, is the ground of this part: the step acts on a spinor through the step itself and through $\nu$, and through nothing else.
\end{definition}
```

### M2-W070 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the fold and the step on states]\label{prop:step}
Let $\widetilde{M}$, $\widetilde{J}$ and $\tau$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:step}. For $k=2,3,4$ let $N_{k}$, with its lift of the fold $\lambda_{k}$, be the pull-back to $\widetilde{M}$ of a line on $Y$ with a lift of the fold whose restriction to the fibre over $s=0$ has degree three and whose sign is $-1$ on the corner circle of $P_{k}$ and $+1$ on the other three (Proposition~\ref{prop:time}(i)), and let $S_{k}$ be the spinor bundle of the unfolded geometry for $\widetilde{J}$ and $N_{k}$, with its lift of the fold $\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda_{k}$; $S_{4}$ with its lift of the fold is the spinor bundle of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(iii) and~(iv). (i) The map $\tau$ is well defined; applied three times it is the identity, and neither $\tau$ nor $\tau$ applied twice leaves a point in place; it commutes with the fold; it carries the corner circle of $P_{i}$ onto that of $M_{0}P_{i}$, that of $P_{1}$ to itself and those of $P_{2},P_{3},P_{4}$ round the cycle $P_{2}\to P_{3}\to P_{4}\to P_{2}$ (M1, Proposition~4.4); it keeps the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, and of the maps $(p,s,r)\mapsto(M_{0}p,s+c,r)$, $c$ real, it is the only one that does; and it keeps $\widetilde{J}$. (ii) The fold acts on the sections of $S_{4}$ by $\psi\mapsto(\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda_{4})\circ\psi\circ\iota$. A state of the geometry (Definition~\ref{def:state}), read through the lift of the fold as the second choice of this paper has it, is a section of $S_{4}$ that this action leaves unchanged: on a state the fold acts as the identity. (iii) There are lifts of the step $\nu_{1}$ from $N_{4}$ to $N_{2}$, $\nu_{2}$ from $N_{2}$ to $N_{3}$ and $\nu_{3}$ from $N_{3}$ to $N_{4}$ that carry lift of the fold to lift of the fold: $\nu_{1}\lambda_{4}=\lambda_{2}\nu_{1}$, $\nu_{2}\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{3}\nu_{2}$ and $\nu_{3}\lambda_{3}=\lambda_{4}\nu_{3}$. Two such lifts between the same two lines differ by multiplication by a continuous function on $\widetilde{M}$ with no zero that the fold leaves unchanged, and every such multiple of one is another. (iv) For $k=2,3,4$ there is no lift of the step from $N_{k}$ to itself that carries $\lambda_{k}$ to $\lambda_{k}$ or to $-\lambda_{k}$. The lines $N_{2}$, $N_{3}$ and $N_{4}$ are pairwise not isomorphic, so that the $\Spinc$ structures $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes N_{2}$, $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes N_{3}$ and $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes N_{4}$ are three different ones among the sixteen of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(ii). One step of the drive therefore carries a state to no state: for a state $\psi$, the section $((\tau^{-1})^{*}\otimes\nu_{1})\circ\psi\circ\tau^{-1}$ is a section of $S_{2}$, which the lift of the fold of $S_{2}$ leaves unchanged; the sign $-1$ of the lift of the fold has moved from the corner circle of $P_{4}$ to that of $P_{2}$ (M1, the proof of Corollary~7.8: ``One step moves the odd corner from $P_{4}$ to $P_{2}$, a second from $P_{2}$ to $P_{3}$, a third from $P_{3}$ to $P_{4}$''). (v) The composite $\nu_{3}\nu_{2}\nu_{1}$ is multiplication by a continuous function $g$ on $\widetilde{M}$ with no zero that the fold leaves unchanged, and three steps carry a state $\psi$ to the state $g\psi$. For every continuous function $f$ on $\widetilde{M}$ with no zero that the fold leaves unchanged the lifts can be taken so that $g=f$; in particular they can be taken so that $g=1$, and so that $g=-1$: the spinor bundles with their lifts of the fold do not decide between the two. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `states 540`; after M2-W069
- **Step:** S05
- **Rests on:** M2-W069; M2-W071, M2-W080, M2-W073 to M2-W076 and M2-W081 (its proof; M2-W080 and M2-W081 in place of M2-W072 and M2-W077, retired; flagged at P213: the statement is unchanged); M2-W005 (`def:state`), M2-W016, M2-W023(iii) (a lift of the fold with given signs on a line of degree three), M2-W033, M2-W039, M2-W041 (`prop:time`), M2-W052, M2-W053, M2-W054 (`prop:whole`); M1 v7.3, Proposition 4.4 (`prop:threecycle`), Theorem 4.2 (`thm:drive`), Proposition 5.8 (`prop:A2`), Corollary 7.8 (`cor:timecircle`) with its proof; Verified References V-M2-5, V-M2-7, V-M2-8; Minimality M2-5
- **Mark:** CHECKED (the proof, M2-W071, M2-W080, M2-W073 to M2-W076 and M2-W081; certificate `MEFProof/Paper_M2/M2_L5_step.lean`, seventeen theorems, which certify the integer arithmetic only; script `papers/M2/checks/S05_step.py`, parts (1) to (8)); confirmed by read-back (P211). The quotation READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.956, cut by script (M1's sentence goes on with a reference to Proposition 4.4, which is not quoted). Not shown here: anything for another family of complex structures carried round time (M2-W068, M2-W079); how the fibre algebra or the element $-w$ itself acts on a spinor (card S10; M2-W078 takes only its cube); the flow of the drive, and what it is for a state to be at rest (card S11); how a state is differentiated (card S14). In (v) the function $g$ depends on the lifts taken and is no invariant of the bundles, as the statement says. **Additions to M1, not contradictions; no proof of M1 changes:** M1 says of the structure of a twist that one step does not carry it to itself and that the corner signs return after three steps (Definition 2.1(ii), l.156; Lemma 7.5(c), l.846; Corollary 7.8); (iii) and (iv) say it of the spinor bundle with its lift of the fold over the whole geometry, and (iv) adds that the $\Spinc$ structure itself is moved among three of the sixteen. M2-W054's "single out one $\Spinc$ structure of the whole geometry" stands as it is: it is the one for the naming under which the odd corner is $P_{4}$ (M2-W023(iv)); M1 l.949 has "The three patterns are one pattern at three successive steps, and to name one of them is to say which step is called the first." M1 takes the corner signs of a structure "up to the overall sign" (l.838, a hit of the red-flag scan); (iv) speaks of $\lambda_{k}$ and of $-\lambda_{k}$ both. **The statement names Proposition `prop:time`(i) alone as the ground of $N_{k}$; that a lift of the fold with the sign $-1$ at $P_{k}$ and $+1$ at the other three corners exists on a line of degree three on $E$ is M2-W023(iii), which the statement does not name (fresh reader, P213): corrected wording put to the PI, and confirmed (P214): retired and replaced by M2-W082**

### M2-W071 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i). Two pairs $(p,s)$ and $(p',s')$ are one point of $Y$ exactly when $s'=s-3Lk$ and $p'=M_{0}^{3k}p$ for an integer $k$ (Definition~\ref{def:time}). The matrix $M_{0}$ commutes with $M_{0}^{3}$, so that the images $(M_{0}p,s+2L)$ and $(M_{0}^{4}p,s-L)$ of the pairs $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ are one point: $\tau$ is well defined on $Y$, and so on $\widetilde{M}$. Applied three times it carries $(p,s)$ to $(M_{0}^{3}p,s-3L)$, which is the point $(p,s)$; and $(M_{0}p,s-L)$ and $(M_{0}^{2}p,s-2L)$ are not the point $(p,s)$, $L$ and $2L$ not being multiples of $3L$. Since $M_{0}$ is linear, $M_{0}(-p)=-M_{0}p$, and $\tau$ commutes with the fold. It carries $(P_{i},s)$ to $(M_{0}P_{i},s-L)$, and $M_{0}$ leaves $P_{1}$ in place and carries $P_{2}$ to $P_{3}$, $P_{3}$ to $P_{4}$ and $P_{4}$ to $P_{2}$ (M1, Proposition~4.4; certificates \texttt{L5\_corners}, \texttt{L5\_cycle}). With $x$ and $y$ the coordinates of M1, Proposition~5.8, along $v_{1}$ and $v_{2}$, $M_{0}$ multiplies $x$ by $\phi^{2}$ and $y$ by $\phi^{-2}$; the lengths $\mathrm{d}s^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$ at the image of a point under $(p,s)\mapsto(M_{0}p,s+c)$, read at the point, are $\mathrm{d}s^{2}+e^{2\kappa(s+c)}\phi^{4}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa(s+c)}\phi^{-4}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$, and since $e^{\kappa L}=\phi^{2}$ they are the lengths at the point exactly when $c=-L$. In the same way $z_{s-L}\circ M_{0}=e^{-\kappa(s-L)}\phi^{-2}y+ie^{\kappa(s-L)}\phi^{2}x=z_{s}$: $M_{0}$ is holomorphic from the fibre at $s$ to the fibre at $s-L$, and $\tau$ keeps the complex structures along the fibre. It carries $\partial_{s}$ to $\partial_{s}$ and is the identity on $S^{3}$, on $\CPtwo$ and on $\Stwo$, so that it keeps the other pieces of $\widetilde{J}$: it keeps $\widetilde{J}$.
```

### M2-W076 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iv), the three lines. Let $\lambda_{Y}$ and $\lambda'_{Y}$ be lifts of the fold to one line on $Y$. As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}, $\lambda'_{Y}=\gamma\lambda_{Y}$ on each fibre, with $\gamma\colon Y\to\C\setminus\{0\}$ continuous and $\gamma(\iota(y))\,\gamma(y)=1$. Read on $E\times\R$, $\gamma(\cdot,s)=e^{2\pi i(a\theta_{\psi}+b\theta_{\omega})}e^{\eta_{s}}$ with integers $a,b$ that do not depend on $s$, as in the second step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}, and $\gamma(p,s+3L)=\gamma(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$, $\gamma$ being a function on $Y$; the integers of $p\mapsto\gamma(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ are those of the row $(a,b)M_{0}^{3}$, so that $(a,b)(M_{0}^{3}-I)=(0,0)$, and since $M_{0}^{3}-I$ has determinant $-16$, $a=b=0$ (certificates \texttt{L5\_det}, \texttt{L5\_no\_winding}). The values of $\gamma$ at the four corners are then one and the same number, $+1$ or $-1$ (the sixth step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}): the signs of $\lambda'_{Y}$ on the four corner circles are those of $\lambda_{Y}$, or all four are opposite. For $k\neq k'$ the signs of $\lambda_{k'}$ are neither those of $\lambda_{k}$ nor their opposites (\texttt{L5\_patterns}); an isomorphism of the line of $N_{k}$ on $Y$ onto that of $N_{k'}$ would give the first a lift of the fold with the signs of $\lambda_{k'}$, so there is none, and by the third step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole} $N_{k}$ and $N_{k'}$ are not isomorphic. The lines act freely on the $\Spinc$ structures \cite[Proposition~1.3.15]{Nicolaescu}, so the structures $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes N_{k}$, $k=2,3,4$, are three different ones; the restrictions of their lines to the fibre have degree three, so they are among the sixteen of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(ii).
```

### M2-W077 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iv), the last sentence, and (v). For a section $\psi$ of $S_{4}$, the map $m\mapsto((\tau^{-1})^{*}\otimes\nu_{1})(\psi(\tau^{-1}(m)))$ is a section of $S_{2}$; the pull-backs by the fold and by $\tau^{-1}$ commute, $\tau$ commuting with the fold, and $\nu_{1}\lambda_{4}=\lambda_{2}\nu_{1}$, so that if the lift of the fold of $S_{4}$ leaves $\psi$ unchanged, the lift of the fold of $S_{2}$ leaves this section unchanged. The composite $\nu_{3}\nu_{2}\nu_{1}$ is continuous and carries the fibre of $N_{4}$ over $m$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre over $\tau^{3}(m)=m$, so it is multiplication by a continuous $g\colon\widetilde{M}\to\C\setminus\{0\}$; it carries $\lambda_{4}$ to $\lambda_{4}$, so that $g\circ\iota=g$. The composite of the three lifts of the step to the spinor bundles is the pull-back by $\tau^{-1}$ three times over, which is the pull-back by the identity, tensored with $\nu_{3}\nu_{2}\nu_{1}$: it is multiplication by $g$ on $S_{4}$, and three steps carry $\psi$ to $g\psi$, which the lift of the fold leaves unchanged if it leaves $\psi$ unchanged. For a continuous $f\colon\widetilde{M}\to\C\setminus\{0\}$ with $f\circ\iota=f$, the map $v\mapsto f(\tau(m))\,g(\tau(m))^{-1}\,\nu_{3}(v)$, for $v$ over $m$, is again a lift of the step from $N_{3}$ to $N_{4}$ that carries $\lambda_{3}$ to $\lambda_{4}$, by (iii); with it in the place of $\nu_{3}$ the composite is multiplication by $f$. The constants $1$ and $-1$ are such functions (certificate \texttt{L5\_six\_steps}, for the powers of $-1$).
```

### M2-W078 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
M1, Corollary~7.8, has: ``On spinors the drive is then the element $-w$, of cube $+1$, and not $w$, of cube $-1$, for which six steps would be needed: clauses (b) and~(c) of Definition~3.2 select it.'' By Proposition~\ref{prop:step}(v) the spinor bundles with their lifts of the fold do not say whether three steps carry a state to itself or to its negative. The element of M1 is read here as saying which: the lifts of the step are taken so that three steps carry every state to itself, $g=1$. With $g=-1$, which is read as the case of $w$, three steps would carry a state to its negative and six would be needed to carry it to itself. This is a reading and is not derived: it is the sixth choice of this paper. Its ground is the selection of M1, Corollary~7.8, by clauses (b) and~(c) of M1, Definition~3.2, carried from the element to the states. In M1 the element is a unit quaternion; how the fibre algebra acts on the spinor bundle of the unfolded geometry is not said in this section, and the reading takes from the element its cube and nothing else. The proof of M1, Corollary~7.8, has: ``A structure defined on the whole geometry, carried once round the time circle, returns to itself; that the lifted fold is such a structure is the hypothesis of the statement.'' That sentence speaks of a structure, the lifted fold, and not of a state. Of the lifted fold it holds here: $\lambda_{4}$ is a lift of the fold on the whole of $\widetilde{M}$, and three steps, which carry every point once round the time circle and back to itself, carry $\lambda_{4}$ to itself (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}(iii)). Of a state the sentence says nothing by itself; with the sixth choice, a state carried by three steps once round the time circle returns to itself.
```
- **Place:** `states 620`; after the proof of Proposition `prop:step` (M2-W077)
- **Step:** S05
- **Rests on:** M2-W082(iii) and (v) (in place of M2-W082, retired; flagged at P214: the statement is unchanged), M2-W069; M1 v7.3, Corollary 7.8 (`cor:timecircle`) with its proof (ll.952, 962, 974), Section 5.5 (the worked case on the order-three automorphism, l.659), Definition 3.2 clauses (b) and (c); Minimality M2-6
- **Mark:** DECLARED (a reading and a numbered choice, the sixth of M2; confirmed by the PI by read-back, P211). READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.952 (the first quotation, cut by script, M1's cross-reference `\ref{def:minimal}` printed as its number, 3.2); l.962 (the second quotation, the whole line); l.974 ("With $w$, three steps return the corner signs with the overall sign reversed, since $w^{3}=-1$, and six steps are needed; with $-w$ three suffice, since $(-w)^{3}=+1$.", the ground of "the case of $w$"); l.659 ("Which of the two elements the drive is on spinors is settled in Corollary~\ref{cor:timecircle}: it is $-w$."). This is the place at which "this result" of the PI's words (`papers/M2/DAG.md` §2; P181) is first cited. **An identification step** (CLAUDE.md §5): M1's element is a unit quaternion given to the drive as a symmetry of the torus (ll.986 to 1002); the entry reads its cube, $+1$ or $-1$, as the function $g$ of M2-W082(v) being $1$ or $-1$. That M2 has not said how the fibre algebra acts on the spinor bundle of the unfolded geometry: the write-up log was searched for "fibre algebra" and "quaternion"; the entries that have the words are M2-W003, M2-W005 and M2-W053, and none says it (the mark of M2-W053: "Not shown and not claimed: … anything of the Clifford multiplication"). **What the choice does and does not do** (the PI told before confirming): by M2-W082(v), "For every continuous function $f$ on $\widetilde{M}$ with no zero that the fold leaves unchanged the lifts can be taken so that $g=f$", so the choice is a convention on how the lifts of the step are taken and constrains no bundle; M1's selection of $-w$ already stands behind the time circle of three steps that M2 uses since M2-W039 (M1, Corollary 7.8). CHECKED: certificate `L5_w_cubed`, `L5_six_steps`; script part (7). Not shown and not claimed: that the element $-w$ itself acts on a spinor, or how (card S10). The PI's musing on this choice (P211) is recorded there as a lead and enters no paper. **A second identification step** (fresh reader, P213): M1's "the lifted fold" (l.962; M1, Corollary 7.7, l.932, has "the lift of $\iota$ to the spinors of the fibre") is read as the lift of the fold $\lambda_{4}$ of M2-W082, with $\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda_{4}$ on the spinor bundle. Since P214 the proposition is M2-W082, which replaced M2-W070 for one corrected phrase; the pointers of this mark are to it

A state is therefore a section $\psi$ of $S_{4}$ with $(\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda_{4})\circ\psi\circ\iota=\psi$.

### M2-W082 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the fold and the step on states]\label{prop:step}
Let $\widetilde{M}$, $\widetilde{J}$ and $\tau$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:step}. For $k=2,3,4$ let $N_{k}$, with its lift of the fold $\lambda_{k}$, be the pull-back to $\widetilde{M}$ of a line on $Y$ with a lift of the fold whose restriction to the fibre over $s=0$ has degree three and whose sign is $-1$ on the corner circle of $P_{k}$ and $+1$ on the other three (Propositions~\ref{prop:pillowcase}(iii) and~\ref{prop:time}(i)), and let $S_{k}$ be the spinor bundle of the unfolded geometry for $\widetilde{J}$ and $N_{k}$, with its lift of the fold $\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda_{k}$; $S_{4}$ with its lift of the fold is the spinor bundle of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(iii) and~(iv). (i) The map $\tau$ is well defined; applied three times it is the identity, and neither $\tau$ nor $\tau$ applied twice leaves a point in place; it commutes with the fold; it carries the corner circle of $P_{i}$ onto that of $M_{0}P_{i}$, that of $P_{1}$ to itself and those of $P_{2},P_{3},P_{4}$ round the cycle $P_{2}\to P_{3}\to P_{4}\to P_{2}$ (M1, Proposition~4.4); it keeps the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, and of the maps $(p,s,r)\mapsto(M_{0}p,s+c,r)$, $c$ real, it is the only one that does; and it keeps $\widetilde{J}$. (ii) The fold acts on the sections of $S_{4}$ by $\psi\mapsto(\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda_{4})\circ\psi\circ\iota$. A state of the geometry (Definition~\ref{def:state}), read through the lift of the fold as the second choice of this paper has it, is a section of $S_{4}$ that this action leaves unchanged: on a state the fold acts as the identity. (iii) There are lifts of the step $\nu_{1}$ from $N_{4}$ to $N_{2}$, $\nu_{2}$ from $N_{2}$ to $N_{3}$ and $\nu_{3}$ from $N_{3}$ to $N_{4}$ that carry lift of the fold to lift of the fold: $\nu_{1}\lambda_{4}=\lambda_{2}\nu_{1}$, $\nu_{2}\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{3}\nu_{2}$ and $\nu_{3}\lambda_{3}=\lambda_{4}\nu_{3}$. Two such lifts between the same two lines differ by multiplication by a continuous function on $\widetilde{M}$ with no zero that the fold leaves unchanged, and every such multiple of one is another. (iv) For $k=2,3,4$ there is no lift of the step from $N_{k}$ to itself that carries $\lambda_{k}$ to $\lambda_{k}$ or to $-\lambda_{k}$. The lines $N_{2}$, $N_{3}$ and $N_{4}$ are pairwise not isomorphic, so that the $\Spinc$ structures $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes N_{2}$, $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes N_{3}$ and $\sigma_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes N_{4}$ are three different ones among the sixteen of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(ii). One step of the drive therefore carries a state to no state: for a state $\psi$, the section $((\tau^{-1})^{*}\otimes\nu_{1})\circ\psi\circ\tau^{-1}$ is a section of $S_{2}$, which the lift of the fold of $S_{2}$ leaves unchanged; the sign $-1$ of the lift of the fold has moved from the corner circle of $P_{4}$ to that of $P_{2}$ (M1, the proof of Corollary~7.8: ``One step moves the odd corner from $P_{4}$ to $P_{2}$, a second from $P_{2}$ to $P_{3}$, a third from $P_{3}$ to $P_{4}$''). (v) The composite $\nu_{3}\nu_{2}\nu_{1}$ is multiplication by a continuous function $g$ on $\widetilde{M}$ with no zero that the fold leaves unchanged, and three steps carry a state $\psi$ to the state $g\psi$. For every continuous function $f$ on $\widetilde{M}$ with no zero that the fold leaves unchanged the lifts can be taken so that $g=f$; in particular they can be taken so that $g=1$, and so that $g=-1$: the spinor bundles with their lifts of the fold do not decide between the two. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `states 540`; after M2-W069, in the place of M2-W070, which it replaces
- **Step:** S05 (a corrected wording after the fresh reader's check, P213; confirmed by the PI, P214)
- **Rests on:** M2-W069; M2-W071, M2-W080, M2-W073 to M2-W076 and M2-W081 (its proof; M2-W080 and M2-W081 in place of M2-W072 and M2-W077, retired; flagged at P213: the statement is unchanged); M2-W005 (`def:state`), M2-W016, M2-W023(iii) (a lift of the fold with given signs on a line of degree three), M2-W033, M2-W039, M2-W041 (`prop:time`), M2-W052, M2-W053, M2-W054 (`prop:whole`); M1 v7.3, Proposition 4.4 (`prop:threecycle`), Theorem 4.2 (`thm:drive`), Proposition 5.8 (`prop:A2`), Corollary 7.8 (`cor:timecircle`) with its proof; Verified References V-M2-5, V-M2-7, V-M2-8; Minimality M2-5
- **Mark:** CHECKED (the proof, M2-W071, M2-W080, M2-W073 to M2-W076 and M2-W081; certificate `MEFProof/Paper_M2/M2_L5_step.lean`, seventeen theorems, which certify the integer arithmetic only; script `papers/M2/checks/S05_step.py`, parts (1) to (8)); confirmed by read-back (P211). The quotation READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.956, cut by script (M1's sentence goes on with a reference to Proposition 4.4, which is not quoted). Not shown here: anything for another family of complex structures carried round time (M2-W068, M2-W079); how the fibre algebra or the element $-w$ itself acts on a spinor (card S10; M2-W078 takes only its cube); the flow of the drive, and what it is for a state to be at rest (card S11); how a state is differentiated (card S14). In (v) the function $g$ depends on the lifts taken and is no invariant of the bundles, as the statement says. **Additions to M1, not contradictions; no proof of M1 changes:** M1 says of the structure of a twist that one step does not carry it to itself and that the corner signs return after three steps (Definition 2.1(ii), l.156; Lemma 7.5(c), l.846; Corollary 7.8); (iii) and (iv) say it of the spinor bundle with its lift of the fold over the whole geometry, and (iv) adds that the $\Spinc$ structure itself is moved among three of the sixteen. M2-W054's "single out one $\Spinc$ structure of the whole geometry" stands as it is: it is the one for the naming under which the odd corner is $P_{4}$ (M2-W023(iv)); M1 l.949 has "The three patterns are one pattern at three successive steps, and to name one of them is to say which step is called the first." No entry changes. **The weakest step** is the existence of the lifts (M2-W074): it is on the step audit's list of weak steps (P212), and a second derivation, made blind, agrees with it (P213). M1 takes the corner signs of a structure "up to the overall sign" (l.838, a hit of the red-flag scan); (iv) speaks of $\lambda_{k}$ and of $-\lambda_{k}$ both. Changed from M2-W070 (P214): "(Propositions~\ref{prop:pillowcase}(iii) and~\ref{prop:time}(i))" for "(Proposition~\ref{prop:time}(i))" as the ground of the lines $N_{k}$, the existence of a lift of the fold with those signs on a line of degree three on $E$ being M2-W023(iii) (fresh reader, P213); no other word is changed. The history of the statement (read back and confirmed at P211) is in M2-W070
- **Tag:** [D]: proved from cited statements (V-M2-5, V-M2-7, V-M2-8) and from results of M1 and M2 tagged [D], among them M2's Propositions `prop:time` and `prop:whole`; it rests on the fifth choice (M2-W069) and, for (ii), on the first, second and fourth
- **Failure test:** a point that $\tau$ or $\tau$ applied twice leaves in place, or that $\tau$ applied three times moves; a lift of the step from $N_{k}$ to itself that carries $\lambda_{k}$ to $\lambda_{k}$ or to $-\lambda_{k}$; two of the lines $N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}$ that are isomorphic; no lift of the step from $N_{4}$ to $N_{2}$ that carries $\lambda_{4}$ to $\lambda_{2}$; an invariant of the spinor bundles with their lifts of the fold that tells $g=1$ from $g=-1$; or an error in a cited statement (V-M2-5, V-M2-7, V-M2-8), in an identification step named in the marks, or in M2-W041 or M2-W054.

### M2-W085 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the unknown and what is given]\label{def:unknown}
The \emph{unknown} of the equation is a state of the geometry (Definition~\ref{def:state}), read through the lift of the fold: a section $\psi$ of $S_{4}$ that the lift of the fold leaves unchanged (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}(ii)). \emph{Given} are: the geometry of M1, Theorem~8.1, which is $\widetilde{M}$ folded (Definition~\ref{def:unfolded}), with everything that M1 fixes on it and with the continuous data that M1 does not derive held fixed as M1 holds them; the spinor bundle $S_{4}$ with its lift of the fold (Propositions~\ref{prop:whole} and~\ref{prop:step}); and the step $\tau$ with its lifts, taken as the sixth choice of this paper has them (Definition~\ref{def:step}). That the unknown is a state, and that nothing of the geometry is unknown, is a choice and is not derived: it is the seventh choice of this paper. Its ground is the text of M1 quoted above, and not a clause of M1, Definition~3.2; that no further object is brought in has clause~(b) of that definition as its ground.
\end{definition}
```
- **Place:** `conditions 030`; after M2-W084
- **Step:** S06
- **Rests on:** M2-W088 and M2-W089 (in place of M2-W083 and M2-W084, retired; flagged at P218: the statement is unchanged); M2-W005 (`def:state`), M2-W016 and M2-W033 (the second choice and its lift), M2-W052 (`def:unfolded`), M2-W054 (`prop:whole`), M2-W069 (`def:step`), M2-W078 (the sixth choice), M2-W082 (`prop:step`); M1 v7.3, Theorem 8.1, Definition 3.2 clause (b); the confirmed read-back of `papers/M2/DAG.md` §1; Minimality M2-7
- **Mark:** DECLARED (a reading and a numbered choice, the seventh of M2; confirmed by the PI by read-back, P216). No clause of M1, Definition 3.2, names the unknown of an equation (rule 6 of the plan): it was put to the PI with the architect's recommendation and its three alternatives (Minimality M2-7), and confirmed. READ: the ground that the statement prints is the text of M1, the lines quoted in M2-W083 and M2-W084; the confirmed read-back of what M2 is to derive, which is the record of the task and not a text the paper cites, agrees with it and was shown to the PI with it: `papers/M2/DAG.md` §1 ("M2 starts from the six axioms and that shape with everything M1 fixed on it"; "a differential equation on the twelve-dimensional geometry of M1 Theorem 8.1. What it is an equation for, and its form, are outputs."); clause (b) at l.275, in the gloss of M2-W005 ("the definition brings in no object that M1 does not have"); M2-W082(ii) as logged ("A state of the geometry (Definition~\ref{def:state}), read through the lift of the fold as the second choice of this paper has it, is a section of $S_{4}$ that this action leaves unchanged"). M1's failure tests looked at (rule 7 of the plan; `papers/M2/M1_failure_tests.md`, M1-W025, M1-W027, M1-W028): none is needed by the choice; the third option of Minimality M2-7 (a state together with data of the geometry), if in it the unknown carried the growth, would need what the first alternative of M1-W025 bars. In the statement the parenthesis "(Definition~\ref{def:step})" names where the step $\tau$ and its lifts are defined; the sixth choice is M2-W078, which has no label (fresh reader, P217; the statement is left as confirmed). **Identification steps** (CLAUDE.md §5): (1) the choice itself, that the "equation" of the PI's words and M1's "field equation" has a state as its unknown; (2) "what is given" is taken for the one family of complex structures that the lengths of M1 give (M2-W069), another family staying declared (M2-W068); (3) "with everything that M1 fixes on it" leaves the direction of the warp as M1 leaves it, not fixed (l.127). **Told to the PI before confirming, and confirmed with the choice:** with a state as the only unknown, the law of the warp of M1 l.127 cannot be a part of the equation that determines the warp (card S09); the direction of the warp would be decided by which direction lets the conditions be met (card S13); the equation could fix the declared input of M1 only through whether solutions exist (card S23). M1's outlook (l.1165) names a coupling and variables that this definition does not have; it is not a premise (P181). The PI's musing on what the equation is for (P216) is recorded there as a lead and enters no paper; the PI's two answers on it are in P216. Not shown and not claimed: that no other unknown could serve; that the equation has a solution
- **Tag:** none: a definition; the choice in it is declared
- **Failure test:** a later step of M2 that needs the equation to determine a datum of the geometry (a length, the warp, a structure); or that needs an unknown that is not a state of the geometry; or that needs an object that neither M1 nor the entries of this paper have; or card S09 finding that the law of the warp (M1, Section 1.4) is a part of the equation.
- **Confirmed:** Decision Log P216, 19 November 2026 (the read-back of S06; the PI: "i confirm option 1 the consequences and the 5 sentences are printed.  proceed")

### M2-W086 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the family of equations]\label{def:family}
An \emph{equation for a state} is a condition on the unknown $\psi$ that holds at every point of the geometry and that is made, at each point, from $\psi$, from its derivatives there and from what is given (Definition~\ref{def:unknown}). The equation of this paper is sought among the equations for a state. A \emph{solution} of such an equation is a state for which the condition holds.
\end{definition}
```
- **Place:** `conditions 040`; after M2-W085
- **Step:** S06
- **Rests on:** M2-W085; the confirmed read-back of `papers/M2/DAG.md` §1 ("a differential equation on the twelve-dimensional geometry of M1 Theorem 8.1")
- **Mark:** A definition, confirmed by read-back (P216); no numbered choice: that the equation is a differential equation on the geometry is the read-back of what M2 is to derive, confirmed at P181, and that it is one for a state is the seventh choice (M2-W085). The definition is bare, as M1, Definition 2.1, is for the shapes, and the PI was told so before confirming: it does not say what a derivative of a state is, nor how smooth a state is, nor in which bundle the condition takes its values, nor what "made from" is, exactly; which operations of differentiation the given determine is the question of card S14, and what the clauses of M1, Definition 3.2, say of an equation is that of card S12. "at each point, from $\psi$, from its derivatives there": the condition at a point is made from the state at that point; a condition at a point that used the state at another point (for example at its image under the step $\tau$) is not in the family as it is worded. Not shown and not claimed: that the family is not empty of equations with solutions
- **Tag:** none: a definition
- **Failure test:** a later step of M2 that needs a condition which is not made, at each point, from the state and its derivatives there and from what is given: one that uses the state at another point, or an object that is not given; or card S14 finding that the given determine no operation of differentiation on a state.
- **Confirmed:** Decision Log P216, 19 November 2026 (the read-back of S06; the PI: "i confirm option 1 the consequences and the 5 sentences are printed.  proceed")
- **Retires / retired by:** none

### M2-W090 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
M1 reads the second axiom as a condition on a geometry: ``A geometry satisfies A2 if: (i) at least one product in~(2) is a warped product: the lengths measured on one piece depend on where one is in another; (ii) along the warping direction $s$ the lengths in one direction of the warped piece are multiplied by $w=e^{\kappa s}$ and those in another by $e^{-\kappa s}$, for a constant $\kappa\neq0$, so that $\mathrm{d}w/\mathrm{d}s=\kappa\,w$ and both signs of $\kappa$ occur; (iii) there is a symmetry of the shape that swaps $\kappa$ for $-\kappa$, and applied twice it is an involution $\iota$ of the warped piece.'' (M1, Definition~2.4). M1 shows that its geometry meets the condition, with lengths on time together with the fibre: ``The geometry therefore satisfies A2.'' (M1, Proposition~5.8).
```
- **Place:** `conditions 300`; the first entry of S09 (the numbers 060 to 290 are left for cards S07 and S08, which are left open and come before it in the paper)
- **Step:** S09
- **Rests on:** M1 v7.3, Definition 2.4 (`def:A2`, l.190), Proposition 5.8 (`prop:A2`, l.704)
- **Mark:** READ (`canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex`, l.190: the first quotation is the whole statement of Definition 2.4, checked by script to be a substring of the line, with M1's cross-reference `\eqref{eq:family}` printed as its number, (2); l.704: "Let lengths on $T$ together with the fibre be measured by $\mathrm{d}s^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$, the fibre at $s+L$ being joined to the fibre at $s$ by the step." and the second quotation, its last sentence, both checked by script). "M1 reads the second axiom as a condition on a geometry" is in the words of l.190, "A geometry satisfies A2 if". Proposition 5.8 is tagged [D] in M1. Not said here: anything of the display's $\times_{w}$ (M2-W093). **The lead-in "M1 shows that its geometry meets the condition" says more than l.704 (fresh reader, P231):** Proposition 5.8 begins "Let lengths on $T$ together with the fibre be measured by", so that the lengths are laid down and not derived, it is tagged [D], and M1 prints no proof under it; its words are "The geometry therefore satisfies A2." ### M2-W092 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
M1 has one law of growth: ``\,``As a function of itself'' is the law of natural growth: a quantity $w$ whose rate of change is proportional to its own size, $\mathrm{d}w/\mathrm{d}s=\kappa w$, and so $w=w_{0}e^{\kappa s}$.'' (M1, Section~2.3); it is the law of clause~(ii) of M1, Definition~2.4, and for the lengths on time together with the fibre M1 has it with $e^{\kappa L}=\phi^{2}$ (M1, Proposition~5.8). The quantity $w$ is a factor by which lengths are multiplied, and whether the law holds does not depend on the state $\psi$: in an equation for a state (Definition~\ref{def:family}) it could only be a condition that every state meets or that none does. It is a statement about what is given, and not a part of the equation.
```
- **Place:** `conditions 320`; after M2-W091
- **Step:** S09
- **Rests on:** M2-W098 (in place of M2-W090, retired; flagged at P232: the statement is unchanged); M2-W085, M2-W086 (`def:family`); M1 v7.3, Section 2.3 (l.187), Definition 2.4(ii) (l.190), Proposition 5.8 (l.704)
- **Mark:** READ (`canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex`, l.187: the quotation is the first sentence of the line, checked by script; l.190, clause (ii): "multiplied by $w=e^{\kappa s}$ … so that $\mathrm{d}w/\mathrm{d}s=\kappa\,w$"; l.704: "The lengths agree across the join if and only if $e^{\kappa L}=\phi^{2}$."). "M1 has one law of growth" is a statement about the corpus (Learnings L34): M1 was searched for "law", singular and plural, and every line shown to the PI before the read-back: ll.127, 169 ("the growth law will be a tie between pieces"), 187, 424 ("the Fibonacci law", another sense) and 1237 (the glossary: "the constant rate of the growth law of Definition~\ref{def:A2}"); l.127 has the word and does not say which law it means (M2-W093). M1 was searched for "warp", "warps", "warped" and "warping" as well (ll.127, 187, 190, 267, 1076, 1139, 1235, 1237, 1251: the display's symbol and the sentence on it; the reading of the axiom and Definition 2.4; Definition 3.1; "The warping is a metric datum"; the elimination table; the glossary), and none of these lines has a law of a warp other than that of ll.187 and 190 (added after the fresh reader's check, P231, which found that the mark named one search where the card and the failure test name two). "M1 has it with $e^{\kappa L}=\phi^{2}$" is the entry's wording: M1's words at l.704 are the lengths $e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$, whose factors are $e^{\pm\kappa s}$, and "The lengths agree across the join if and only if $e^{\kappa L}=\phi^{2}$." "it is the law of clause (ii)": the same relation $\mathrm{d}w/\mathrm{d}s=\kappa w$ stands at l.187 and in clause (ii) at l.190, and l.1237 calls it "the growth law of Definition 2.4". Hand step, in full: by clause (ii) $w$ multiplies lengths of the warped piece; the lengths are given (M2-W085); so whether $\mathrm{d}w/\mathrm{d}s=\kappa w$ holds is the same for every state; a condition that is the same for every state is met by every state or by none; M2-W086 has an equation for a state as "a condition on the unknown $\psi$". **An addition to M1, not a contradiction; no proof of M1 changes:** M1 does not say that the law is separate from the equation; its l.127 has "with the field equation", which allows "as a part of the field equation"; that it is not a part rests on the seventh choice (M2-W085), whose mark carries the point for this card, and not on l.127. The failure test of M2-W085 ("card S09 finding that the law of the warp (M1, Section 1.4) is a part of the equation") is not met. **Not shown and not claimed:** that the law of l.127 is this law (open; it waits for S13, P228)
- **Tag:** none
- **Failure test:** a line of M1 v7.3 with another law of growth or of a warp; or a later step of M2 that needs the law $\mathrm{d}w/\mathrm{d}s=\kappa w$ to be a condition on the state.

### M2-W099 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
Proposition~5.8 of M1 measures lengths on time together with the fibre: ``Let lengths on $T$ together with the fibre be measured by $\mathrm{d}s^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$, the fibre at $s+L$ being joined to the fibre at $s$ by the step.'' Here $s$ is the length along $T$, and $x,y$ are the coordinates on the fibre along $v_{1},v_{2}$ (M1, Proposition~5.8). In these lengths the fibre is scaled, by functions on time: beside the lengths at $s=0$, the lengths of the fibre along $v_{1}$ are multiplied by $e^{\kappa s}$ and those along $v_{2}$ by $e^{-\kappa s}$, and lengths along time are not multiplied.
```
- **Place:** `warp 010`; the first entry of the section and of S13
- **Step:** S13
- **Rests on:** M1 v7.3, Proposition 5.8 (`prop:A2`, l.704)
- **Mark:** READ (`canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex`, l.704: the quotation is one whole sentence of the statement of Proposition 5.8, checked by script (`papers/M2/checks/S13_quotes.py`) to be a substring of the line; "Here $s$ is the length along $T$" and "the coordinates on the fibre along $v_{1},v_{2}$" are in the words of the same line, "Let $s$ be the length along $T$" and "let $x,y$ be the coordinates on the fibre $\Ttwo=\R^{2}/\Z^{2}$ along $v_{1},v_{2}$", where $v_{1}$ is "a unit eigenvector of $M_{0}$ with eigenvalue $\phi^{2}$" and "$v_{2}=Sv_{1}$"). CHECKED, a hand step in full: in $\mathrm{d}s^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$ the coefficient of $\mathrm{d}s^{2}$ is $1$, that of $\mathrm{d}x^{2}$ is $e^{2\kappa s}$ and that of $\mathrm{d}y^{2}$ is $e^{-2\kappa s}$, and there is no mixed term; a length is the square root of the form, so that a displacement along $v_{1}$ alone has length $e^{\kappa s}$ times its length at $s=0$, one along $v_{2}$ alone has $e^{-\kappa s}$ times, and one along time alone has the same length at every $s$; the two factors depend on $s$ and on nothing else. "scaled" and "function on" are the words of M1, l.127, where M1 uses them of the product in its display; M1 does not use them of the lengths of Proposition 5.8, and their use here is the hand step above (added after the fresh reader's check, P235). "the fibre" is here the fibre of l.704, "the fibre $\Ttwo=\R^{2}/\Z^{2}$", as in M2-W048 and M2-W071; in M2-W101 it is $\Ttwo/\Ztwo$; M1 uses the word both ways (P235). Proposition 5.8 is tagged [D] in M1, lays these lengths down and has no proof printed under it (M2-W098). ### M2-W105 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the operator $\bar\partial$ of the twist]\label{def:dbar}
Let $J$ be a complex structure of $E$ as in Definition~\ref{def:lift}, with a coordinate $z$ linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$, and let $D=\sum a_{i}P_{i}$ be a divisor on the corners. Away from the corners the constant $1$ is a frame of $\mathcal{O}(D)$, and near $P_{i}$, with $z_{i}=z-P_{i}$, so is $z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$. A section $\sigma$ of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ is \emph{differentiable} when, in each of these frames $e$, it is $\sigma=fe$ with $f$ continuously differentiable; then
\[
\bar\partial\sigma\;=\;\frac{\partial f}{\partial\bar z}\,\mathrm{d}\bar z\otimes e ,
\]
a section of $\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D)$. On the spinor bundle $\Lambda^{0,*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D)$ of the fibre, whose summands are $\mathcal{O}(D)$ and $\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D)$, $\bar\partial$ is this map on the first summand and zero on the second.
\end{definition}
```
- **Place:** `supply 020`; after M2-W104
- **Step:** S14
- **Rests on:** M2-W104; M2-W022 (`def:lift`: $E$, its complex structure, the spinor bundle $\Lambda^{0,*}\otimes N$), M2-W026 and M2-W050 (the frames of $\mathcal{O}(D)$), M2-W051 (a coordinate linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$; the two summands on a surface), M2-W024 (the twist of flux three is the line $N$ of the fibre); M1 v7.3, Definition 7.4 (`def:states`, l.832)
- **Mark:** A definition, confirmed by read-back (P238); no numbered choice: it writes out, in the frames that M2-W050 prints, the derivative that M1's word "holomorphic" (l.834) speaks of, and brings in nothing else. READ: M2-W050 as logged ("Away from the corners the constant $1$ is a frame of $\mathcal{O}(D)$, and near $P_{i}$, with $z_{i}=z-P_{i}$, so is $z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$."); M2-W051 ("On a surface the $(0,*)$-forms are the functions and the $(0,1)$-forms"); M2-W022 ("The spinor bundle of the $\Spinc$ structure that $N$ defines on $E$ is $\Lambda^{0,*}\otimes N$"). Hand steps, in full: $\partial f/\partial\bar z=\tfrac12(\partial f/\partial u+i\,\partial f/\partial v)$ for $z=u+iv$, defined for a continuously differentiable $f$; "zero on the second": on a surface there are no $(0,2)$-forms. That the definition does not depend on the frame is M2-W107. **Not shown and not claimed** (the PI told before confirming, P238): how smooth a state is (M2-W005 does not say; "differentiable" is defined here for sections of the twist only); that the line $N$ of M2-W023, given there up to isomorphism, is $\mathcal{O}(D)$ otherwise than by M2-W024; anything along time, on space or on the base. The symbol $\bar\partial$ and the word "differentiable" are new in the series as names; "operator" stands in M1 only in a title (l.1300)
- **Tag:** none: a definition

### M2-W106 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the fibre at one time]\label{prop:fibre}
Let $E$, $J$, $z$ and $D$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:dbar}. (i) $\bar\partial$ does not depend on the frames or on which coordinate of $J$ is taken: it is made from $J$ and $D$, and no length enters it. (ii) A differentiable section $\sigma$ of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ has $\bar\partial\sigma=0$ exactly when it is a state at rest of M1, Definition~7.4. (iii) $\bar\partial$ commutes with the action on sections of the first and of the second lift of the fold. (iv) Exactly five divisors of flux three on the corners have, for their lift with two states on the pillowcase, the signs $+1,+1,+1,-1$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}(iv): $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$, $2P_{2}+P_{4}$, $2P_{3}+P_{4}$ and $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$. With $\wp$ the Weierstrass function of $E$ and $e_{k}=\wp(P_{k})$, multiplication by $1$, $1/(\wp-e_{4})$, $(\wp-e_{2})/(\wp-e_{4})$, $(\wp-e_{3})/(\wp-e_{4})$ and $\wp'/(\wp-e_{4})^{2}$ respectively carries $\mathcal{O}(D)$ onto $\mathcal{O}(3P_{4})$, differentiable sections onto differentiable sections, $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$, and the lift with two states to the lift with two states. (v) An isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ to itself that covers the identity of $E$ and carries differentiable sections to differentiable sections and $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$ is multiplication by a constant. On the spinor bundle of the fibre with the lift of the fold of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}(iv), therefore, $\bar\partial$ is the same whichever of the five divisors is taken, and the only isomorphisms that keep it are the constants. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `supply 030`; after M2-W105
- **Step:** S14

### M2-W107 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i) and (ii). Where two of the frames meet, $1=z_{i}^{a_{i}}\cdot z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$, and $z_{i}^{a_{i}}$ is holomorphic with no zero there. If $\sigma=f\cdot1=g\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ then $g=z_{i}^{a_{i}}f$, $g$ is continuously differentiable exactly when $f$ is, and $\partial g/\partial\bar z=z_{i}^{a_{i}}\,\partial f/\partial\bar z$: the two frames give the same $\bar\partial\sigma$. Another coordinate of $J$ that is linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$ is $cz$ for a constant $c\neq0$; with it $\partial f/\partial\bar z$ is divided by $\bar c$, $\mathrm{d}\bar z$ is multiplied by $\bar c$, and the frames near the corners are multiplied by constants, so that $\bar\partial$ is unchanged. It is made from $J$, which says which functions are holomorphic, and from $D$, which gives the frames; no length is used. This is (i). A continuously differentiable function $f$ with $\partial f/\partial\bar z=0$ has a complex derivative at each point, and a function with a complex derivative at each point has $\partial f/\partial\bar z=0$. So $\bar\partial\sigma=0$ exactly when $\sigma$ is a holomorphic function $f$ away from the corners and, near $P_{i}$, is $g\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ with $g$ holomorphic, that is when $f$ has at $P_{i}$ a pole of order at most $a_{i}$: a function on $E$ whose poles are bounded by $D$ (M1, Definition~7.4). This is (ii).
```
- **Place:** `supply 040`; the first step of the proof of Proposition `prop:fibre` (M2-W106)
- **Step:** S14
- **Rests on:** M2-W105; M2-W048 (a function linear over the reals and holomorphic is a constant multiple of the coordinate); M1 v7.3, Definition 7.4 (l.834)
- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full: $z_{i}^{a_{i}}$ is a polynomial in $z$, holomorphic, with its only zero at $P_{i}$, which is not in the overlap; $\partial(hf)/\partial\bar z=h\,\partial f/\partial\bar z$ for $h$ holomorphic, since $\partial h/\partial\bar z=0$; $g\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ with $\partial g/\partial\bar z=z_{i}^{a_{i}}\partial f/\partial\bar z$ gives $\partial g/\partial\bar z\,\mathrm{d}\bar z\otimes z_{i}^{-a_{i}}=\partial f/\partial\bar z\,\mathrm{d}\bar z\otimes1$. A coordinate $z'$ of $J$ linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$ is linear over the reals and holomorphic for $J$, so it is $cz$ (the hand step of M2-W048), $c\neq0$ since $z'$ is one-to-one; $\partial/\partial\bar z'=\bar c^{-1}\partial/\partial\bar z$ and $\mathrm{d}\bar z'=\bar c\,\mathrm{d}\bar z$; $z_{i}'^{-a_{i}}=c^{-a_{i}}z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$. For $f$ continuously differentiable at $z_{0}$, $f(z_{0}+k)-f(z_{0})=\frac{\partial f}{\partial z}k+\frac{\partial f}{\partial\bar z}\bar k+o(\lvert k\rvert)$; with $\partial f/\partial\bar z=0$ the quotient by $k$ tends to $\partial f/\partial z$; conversely, if the quotient has a limit, taking $k$ real and $k$ imaginary gives $\partial f/\partial u=-i\,\partial f/\partial v$, that is $\partial f/\partial\bar z=0$. A holomorphic $g$ near $P_{i}$ gives $f=g\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ with a pole of order at most $a_{i}$, and such an $f$ gives $g=z_{i}^{a_{i}}f$ holomorphic near $P_{i}$ once it is known to be continuously differentiable there, which a differentiable section is. READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.834 ("a function $f$ on the covering torus $E$ whose poles are bounded by $D$"). "no length is used": the formula has $J$ (through $z$) and $D$ (through the frames) and nothing else; M2-W048 makes $J$ at each time from the lengths of M1, and two measures of length with the same $J$ give the same $\bar\partial$

- **Failure test:** two of the frames, or two coordinates of $J$ linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$, that give different $\bar\partial\sigma$; or a state at rest of M1, Definition 7.4, with $\bar\partial\neq0$.
