### M2-W108 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
The Weierstrass function. By the coordinate $z$, $E$ is the plane $\C$ modulo the lattice that is the image of $\Z^{2}$; $P_{1}$ is the class of $0$, and $P_{2}$, $P_{3}$, $P_{4}$ are the classes of the half-periods $\omega_{1}$, $\omega_{2}$ and $\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}$, where $2\omega_{1}$ and $2\omega_{2}$ are the values of $z$ at $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$; $\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}$ is congruent to $\omega_{3}=-\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}$. The Weierstrass function $\wp$ of these periods is an even elliptic function whose only singularities are double poles at the points of the lattice, and $\wp'$ is odd \cite[\S\S20.2, 20.21]{WhittakerWatson}. The only singularities of $\wp'$ are triple poles at the points congruent to the origin, and its only zeros are the points congruent to $\omega_{1}$, $\omega_{2}$, $\omega_{3}$; for each of these half-periods $\omega$ the only zeros of $\wp-\wp(\omega)$ are double zeros at the points congruent to $\omega$ \cite[\S20.32]{WhittakerWatson}. An elliptic function has in a cell as many zeros as poles \cite[\S20.13]{WhittakerWatson}, so that the three zeros of $\wp'$ are simple. With $e_{k}=\wp(P_{k})$ for $k=2,3,4$ (M1, Section~7.4, the smallest case) the divisors on $E$ are therefore: of $\wp-e_{k}$, $2P_{k}-2P_{1}$; of $\wp'$, $P_{2}+P_{3}+P_{4}-3P_{1}$.
```
- **Place:** `supply 050`; the second step of the proof of Proposition `prop:fibre`
- **Step:** S14
- **Rests on:** Verified Reference V-M2-13; M2-W035 ($E=\R^{2}/\Z^{2}$ with M1's coordinates and corners); M1 v7.3, Appendix B (the corners by position, l.1217), Section 7.4 (ll.858, 921)
- **Mark:** Verified reference, READ at source (V-M2-13: Whittaker and Watson, 3rd ed. 1920, §§20.13, 20.2, 20.21, 20.32, in the text of a scan; the prose read, the displayed formulas not relied on). READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.1217 (the corners by position), l.921 ("$e_{4}=\wp(P_{4})$"); M1's own sentence at l.858 ("$\wp'$ has a triple pole at $P_{1}$ and a simple zero at each of $P_{2},P_{3},P_{4}$") agrees and is not the ground. CHECKED: script `papers/M2/checks/S14_fibre.py`, part (3), numerically for three lattices ($\wp$ even and $\wp'$ odd; $\wp'(P_{k})=0$; the second derivative of $\wp$ at $P_{k}$ not zero; the three $e_{k}$ different). Hand steps, in full: $z$ is linear and one-to-one from $\R^{2}$ to $\C$, so $2\omega_{1}=z(1,0)$ and $2\omega_{2}=z(0,1)$ are independent over the reals, their ratio is not real, and the lattice is the set of the points $2m\omega_{1}+2n\omega_{2}$ of the source; $P_{2}=(\tfrac12,0)$, $P_{3}=(0,\tfrac12)$, $P_{4}=(\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$ have the coordinates $\omega_{1}$, $\omega_{2}$, $\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}$, and $\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}-\omega_{3}=2\omega_{1}+2\omega_{2}$ is a period; three zeros counted with their orders at three points that are not congruent to one another are three simple zeros; $\wp-e_{k}$ has the poles of $\wp$, one double pole in a cell, at the class of $0$. **Identification steps** (CLAUDE.md §5): the source's functions of a complex variable with two periods are read as functions on $E$, and their zeros and poles in a cell as a divisor on $E$; which of the source's $\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3}$ is which corner is fixed by the two values of $z$ named, and the statement is the same for any naming
- **Tag:** none (a step of a proof)
- **Failure test:** a zero or a pole of $\wp-e_{k}$ or of $\wp'$ other than those named, or of another order; or an error in the cited statements.
- **Confirmed:** checked, not read back, 19 November 2026 (the PI told in plain English in the read-back of S14, first part; Decision Log P238)

### M2-W109 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iv). Of the twenty divisors of flux three on the corners, exactly five have, for the lift with two states on the pillowcase (M1, Lemma~7.5(b)), the signs $+1,+1,+1,-1$: $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$, $2P_{2}+P_{4}$, $2P_{3}+P_{4}$ and $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$ (certificate \texttt{L6\_five}); for $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$ that lift is the second, and for the other four it is the first (\texttt{L6\_which\_lift}; M1, the proof of Proposition~7.6). Let $D=\sum a_{i}P_{i}$ be one of the five and $m$ the function named for it in (iv). By the step above the divisor of $m$ is $D-3P_{4}$ (\texttt{L6\_multipliers}). Away from the corners $m$ is holomorphic with no zero, and near $P_{i}$ it is $z_{i}^{a_{i}-b_{i}}$ times a holomorphic function with no zero, where $b_{4}=3$ and $b_{1}=b_{2}=b_{3}=0$. Multiplication by $m$ therefore carries each frame of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ to a frame of $\mathcal{O}(3P_{4})$ times a holomorphic function with no zero: it is an isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ onto $\mathcal{O}(3P_{4})$ that covers the identity of $E$, it carries differentiable sections onto differentiable sections, and, since $\partial(mf)/\partial\bar z=m\,\partial f/\partial\bar z$, it carries $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$. The first four multipliers are even functions and the fifth is odd (\texttt{L6\_parity}). For $m$ even, $(mf)(-z)=m(z)\,f(-z)$: multiplication by $m$ carries the first lift to the first. For $m$ odd, $(mf)(-z)=m(z)\cdot\bigl(-f(-z)\bigr)$: it carries the second lift of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ to the first lift of $\mathcal{O}(3P_{4})$. In each case the lift with two states is carried to the lift with two states.
```
- **Place:** `supply 060`; the third step of the proof of Proposition `prop:fibre`
- **Step:** S14
- **Rests on:** M2-W105, M2-W107, M2-W108; M2-W026 (the first and the second lift as lifts of the fold; their signs), M2-W032 (the lift with two states over the twenty divisors); M1 v7.3, Lemma 7.5(b) (`lem:states`, l.846), the proof of Proposition 7.6 (ll.908, 912)
- **Mark:** CHECKED: certificate `MEFProof/Paper_M2/M2_L6_fibre.lean`, `L6_twenty`, `L6_five`, `L6_which_lift`, `L6_degree_zero`, `L6_multipliers`, `L6_parity`, which certify the count and the integer arithmetic, the divisors of $\wp-e_{k}$ and of $\wp'$ being written into the file by hand from M2-W108, and the count of M1, Lemma 7.5(b), being taken as given, as in `M2_L2_pillowcase`; script `papers/M2/checks/S14_fibre.py`, parts (1) and (2). READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` ll.908 and 912 (which lift keeps two states, and where the odd corner is, for the three shapes of a divisor of flux three), l.846. Hand steps, in full. The divisors of the five multipliers: of $1$, zero; of $1/(\wp-e_{4})$, $2P_{1}-2P_{4}$; of $(\wp-e_{k})/(\wp-e_{4})$, $2P_{k}-2P_{4}$, $k=2,3$; of $\wp'/(\wp-e_{4})^{2}$, $(P_{2}+P_{3}+P_{4}-3P_{1})-2(2P_{4}-2P_{1})=P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}-3P_{4}$; each is $D-3P_{4}$ for its $D$. A function with the order $a_{i}-b_{i}$ at $P_{i}$ is $z_{i}^{a_{i}-b_{i}}$ times a holomorphic function with no zero near $P_{i}$; for $\sigma=g\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$, $m\sigma=(m\,z_{i}^{b_{i}-a_{i}})\,g\,z_{i}^{-b_{i}}$, and $m\,z_{i}^{b_{i}-a_{i}}$ is holomorphic with no zero; away from the corners $m$ has neither zero nor pole, its divisor lying on the corners; the inverse is multiplication by $1/m$. On the summand $\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D)$ the map is multiplication by $m$ as well. Even and odd: $\wp$ is even, so $\wp-e_{k}$ and the first four multipliers are; $\wp'$ is odd and $(\wp-e_{4})^{2}$ even, so the fifth is odd. The first lift is $f(z)\mapsto f(-z)$ and the second $f(z)\mapsto-f(-z)$ (M1 l.834). **The weakest step of the card:** that multiplication by a function with poles is an isomorphism of the lines rests on the hand step on the frames, which no certificate checks; and the identification step of M2-W108 (zeros and poles in a cell read as a divisor on $E$)

### M2-W110 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii) and (v). For a continuously differentiable function $f$ and $g(z)=f(-z)$, $\frac{\partial g}{\partial\bar z}(z)=-\frac{\partial f}{\partial\bar z}(-z)$, and $\iota^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z=-\mathrm{d}\bar z$ (Definition~\ref{def:lift}). In the frame $1$ the first lift carries the section $f$ to $g$, and the section $u\,\mathrm{d}\bar z\otimes1$ to $-u(-z)\,\mathrm{d}\bar z\otimes1$; so $\bar\partial$ of the image of $f$ is $-\frac{\partial f}{\partial\bar z}(-z)\,\mathrm{d}\bar z\otimes1$, which is the image of $\bar\partial f$. Near a corner the first lift carries the frame $z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ to $(-1)^{a_{i}}z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$, a constant multiple, and the same holds there. The second lift is the first followed by multiplication by $-1$, with which $\bar\partial$ commutes. This is (iii). For (v): an isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ to itself that covers the identity of $E$ is multiplication by a continuous function $\beta$ with no zero (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}). The frames $1$ and $z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ are differentiable sections with $\bar\partial=0$; so are their images $\beta\cdot1$ and $\beta\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$, if the isomorphism carries differentiable sections to differentiable sections and $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$. Then $\beta$ is continuously differentiable with $\partial\beta/\partial\bar z=0$ at every point of $E$; it is an elliptic function with no pole, and so a constant \cite[\S20.12]{WhittakerWatson}.
```
- **Place:** `supply 070`; the last step of the proof of Proposition `prop:fibre`
- **Step:** S14
- **Rests on:** M2-W105, M2-W107; M2-W022 ($\iota^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z=-\mathrm{d}\bar z$; the lift $\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda$), M2-W026 (the first lift on the frames), M2-W034 (an isomorphism covering the identity is multiplication by a continuous function with no zero), M2-W082(ii) (how the fold acts on sections); Verified Reference V-M2-13
- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full, and by script (`papers/M2/checks/S14_fibre.py`, part (4), numerically for one function that is not holomorphic). With $z=u+iv$ and $g(u,v)=f(-u,-v)$, $\partial g/\partial u=-(\partial f/\partial u)(-z)$ and $\partial g/\partial v=-(\partial f/\partial v)(-z)$, so $\partial g/\partial\bar z(z)=-(\partial f/\partial\bar z)(-z)$. The action on sections is $\psi\mapsto(\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda)\circ\psi\circ\iota$: M2-W082(ii) has it for the sections of $S_{4}$ over the unfolded geometry, with $\lambda_{4}$; it is taken here, in the same form, for a lift $\lambda$ of the fold to $\mathcal{O}(D)$ over the fibre, **an identification step** (said after the fresh reader's check, P239). For the first lift and the frame $1$, a section $f\cdot1$ goes to $f(-z)\cdot1$ (the mark of M2-W050: "the first lift carries the frame $1$ over $p$ to the frame $1$ over $\iota(p)$"; the mark of M2-W026: "away from the corners the frame is $1$ and is carried to $1$"), and $u\,\mathrm{d}\bar z\otimes1$ goes to $u(-z)\,\iota^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z\otimes1=-u(-z)\,\mathrm{d}\bar z\otimes1$; with $u=\partial f/\partial\bar z$ this is $\bar\partial g$. Near $P_{i}$ the fold is $z_{i}\mapsto-z_{i}$ and the frame $z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ goes to $(-z_{i})^{-a_{i}}=(-1)^{a_{i}}z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ (M2-W026), so a section $g\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ goes to $(-1)^{a_{i}}g(-z_{i})\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ and the same computation holds with the constant $(-1)^{a_{i}}$. $\bar\partial(-\sigma)=-\bar\partial\sigma$. For (v): READ, M2-W034 ("An isomorphism $\phi$ of $N$ to itself that covers the identity of $E$ is multiplication by a continuous $\beta\colon E\to\C\setminus\{0\}$"); $\bar\partial(1)=0$ and $\bar\partial(z_{i}^{-a_{i}})=0$, the function in the frame being the constant $1$; $\beta\cdot1$ differentiable gives $\beta$ continuously differentiable away from the corners, and $\beta\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ differentiable gives it near $P_{i}$, the function in the frame $z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ being $\beta$; $\bar\partial(\beta e)=\beta\,\bar\partial e=0$ gives $\partial\beta/\partial\bar z=0$; by the hand step of M2-W107, $\beta$ has a complex derivative at every point, and read on $\C$ it has the two periods: an elliptic function with no pole. Verified reference V-M2-13 (§20.12 (IV): "An elliptic function, $f(z)$, with no poles in a cell is merely a constant."), READ at source. The lifts of (iii) are M1's own two; of another lift of M2-W023(iii) the step says nothing (the mark of M2-W106)
- **Tag:** none (a step of a proof)
- **Failure test:** a continuously differentiable function $f$ for which $\bar\partial$ of $f(-z)$ is not the image of $\bar\partial f$ under the first lift; or a function on $E$ that is not constant, has no zero and has a complex derivative at every point.

### M2-W111 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase} gives the line of the fibre up to isomorphisms that cover the identity of $E$, each of which is multiplication by a continuous function with no zero. By Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(v) only the constants among them keep $\bar\partial$. The line as a bundle up to isomorphism therefore determines no operation of differentiation; $\bar\partial$ comes from M1's twist, the line of a divisor, and not from the bundle alone.
```

### M2-W113 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
The Weierstrass function. By the coordinate $z$, $E$ is the plane $\C$ modulo the lattice that is the image of $\Z^{2}$; $P_{1}$ is the class of $0$, and $P_{2}$, $P_{3}$, $P_{4}$ are the classes of the half-periods $\omega_{1}$, $\omega_{2}$ and $\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}$, where $2\omega_{1}$ and $2\omega_{2}$ are the values of $z$ at $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$; $\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}$ is congruent to $\omega_{3}=-\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}$. The Weierstrass function $\wp$ of these periods is an even elliptic function whose only singularities are double poles at the points of the lattice, and $\wp'$ is odd \cite[\S\S20.2, 20.21]{WhittakerWatson}. The only singularities of $\wp'$ are triple poles at the points congruent to the origin, and its only zeros are the points congruent to $\omega_{1}$, $\omega_{2}$, $\omega_{3}$; for each of these half-periods $\omega$ the only zeros of $\wp-\wp(\omega)$ are double zeros at the points congruent to $\omega$ \cite[\S20.32]{WhittakerWatson}. An elliptic function has in a cell as many zeros as poles \cite[\S20.13]{WhittakerWatson}, so that the three zeros of $\wp'$ are simple. With $e_{k}=\wp(P_{k})$ for $k=2,3,4$, as M1, Section~7.4, has it for $k=4$ in its smallest case, the divisors on $E$ are therefore: of $\wp-e_{k}$, $2P_{k}-2P_{1}$; of $\wp'$, $P_{2}+P_{3}+P_{4}-3P_{1}$.
```
- **Place:** `supply 050`; the second step of the proof of Proposition `prop:fibre`, in the place of M2-W108, which it replaces
- **Step:** S14 (a correction after the fresh reader's check, P239)
- **Rests on:** Verified Reference V-M2-13; M2-W035 ($E=\R^{2}/\Z^{2}$ with M1's coordinates and corners); M1 v7.3, Appendix B (the corners by position, l.1217), Section 7.4 (ll.858, 921)
- **Mark:** Verified reference, READ at source (V-M2-13: Whittaker and Watson, 3rd ed. 1920, §§20.13, 20.2, 20.21, 20.32, in the text of a scan; the prose read, the displayed formulas not relied on). READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.1217 (the corners by position), l.921 ("$e_{4}=\wp(P_{4})$"); M1's own sentence at l.858 ("$\wp'$ has a triple pole at $P_{1}$ and a simple zero at each of $P_{2},P_{3},P_{4}$") agrees and is not the ground. CHECKED: script `papers/M2/checks/S14_fibre.py`, part (3), numerically for three lattices ($\wp$ even and $\wp'$ odd; $\wp'(P_{k})=0$; the second derivative of $\wp$ at $P_{k}$ not zero; the three $e_{k}$ different). Hand steps, in full: $z$ is linear and one-to-one from $\R^{2}$ to $\C$, so $2\omega_{1}=z(1,0)$ and $2\omega_{2}=z(0,1)$ are independent over the reals, their ratio is not real, and the lattice is the set of the points $2m\omega_{1}+2n\omega_{2}$ of the source; $P_{2}=(\tfrac12,0)$, $P_{3}=(0,\tfrac12)$, $P_{4}=(\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$ have the coordinates $\omega_{1}$, $\omega_{2}$, $\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}$, and $\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}-\omega_{3}=2\omega_{1}+2\omega_{2}$ is a period; three zeros counted with their orders at three points that are not congruent to one another are three simple zeros; $\wp-e_{k}$ has the poles of $\wp$, one double pole in a cell, at the class of $0$. **Identification steps** (CLAUDE.md §5): the source's functions of a complex variable with two periods are read as functions on $E$, and their zeros and poles in a cell as a divisor on $E$; which of the source's $\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3}$ is which corner is fixed by the two values of $z$ named, and the statement is the same for any naming Changed from M2-W108 (fresh reader, P239): the statement credited $e_{k}=\wp(P_{k})$ for $k=2,3,4$ to M1's smallest case, where M1 has $e_{4}$ only (l.921); the sentence now says so. Nothing else changed. The source's conditions on the periods, read after the check: "any two numbers (real or complex) whose ratio is not purely real" (§20.1, p.429), and the naming of §20.12 (III), that $2\omega_{1}$ is the period for which $\omega_{2}/\omega_{1}$ has a positive imaginary part, which names the periods and does not restrict them

### M2-W114 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iv). Of the twenty divisors of flux three on the corners, exactly five have, for the lift with two states on the pillowcase (M1, Lemma~7.5(b)), the signs $+1,+1,+1,-1$: $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$, $2P_{2}+P_{4}$, $2P_{3}+P_{4}$ and $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$ (certificate \texttt{L6\_five}); for $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$ that lift is the second, and for the other four it is the first (\texttt{L6\_which\_lift}; M1, the proof of Proposition~7.6). Let $D=\sum a_{i}P_{i}$ be one of the five and $m$ the function named for it in (iv). By the step above the divisor of $m$ is $D-3P_{4}$ (the arithmetic of the divisors is certified in \texttt{L6\_multipliers}, the divisors of $\wp-e_{k}$ and of $\wp'$ being written into the certificate from the step above). Away from the corners $m$ is holomorphic with no zero, and near $P_{i}$ it is $z_{i}^{a_{i}-b_{i}}$ times a holomorphic function with no zero, where $b_{4}=3$ and $b_{1}=b_{2}=b_{3}=0$. Multiplication by $m$ therefore carries each frame of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ to a frame of $\mathcal{O}(3P_{4})$ times a holomorphic function with no zero: it is an isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ onto $\mathcal{O}(3P_{4})$ that covers the identity of $E$, it carries differentiable sections onto differentiable sections, and, since $\partial(mf)/\partial\bar z=m\,\partial f/\partial\bar z$, it carries $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$. The first four multipliers are even functions and the fifth is odd, $\wp$ being even and $\wp'$ odd; the multiplier in which $\wp'$ stands is the one for the divisor whose lift with two states is the second (\texttt{L6\_parity}). For $m$ even, $(mf)(-z)=m(z)\,f(-z)$: multiplication by $m$ carries the first lift to the first. For $m$ odd, $(mf)(-z)=m(z)\cdot\bigl(-f(-z)\bigr)$: it carries the second lift of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ to the first lift of $\mathcal{O}(3P_{4})$. In each case the lift with two states is carried to the lift with two states.
```

READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` ll.908 and 912 (which lift keeps two states, and where the odd corner is, for the three shapes of a divisor of flux three), l.846. Hand steps, in full. The divisors of the five multipliers: of $1$, zero; of $1/(\wp-e_{4})$, $2P_{1}-2P_{4}$; of $(\wp-e_{k})/(\wp-e_{4})$, $2P_{k}-2P_{4}$, $k=2,3$; of $\wp'/(\wp-e_{4})^{2}$, $(P_{2}+P_{3}+P_{4}-3P_{1})-2(2P_{4}-2P_{1})=P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}-3P_{4}$; each is $D-3P_{4}$ for its $D$. A function with the order $a_{i}-b_{i}$ at $P_{i}$ is $z_{i}^{a_{i}-b_{i}}$ times a holomorphic function with no zero near $P_{i}$; for $\sigma=g\,z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$, $m\sigma=(m\,z_{i}^{b_{i}-a_{i}})\,g\,z_{i}^{-b_{i}}$, and $m\,z_{i}^{b_{i}-a_{i}}$ is holomorphic with no zero; away from the corners $m$ has neither zero nor pole, its divisor lying on the corners; the inverse is multiplication by $1/m$. On the summand $\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D)$ the map is multiplication by $m$ as well. Even and odd: $\wp$ is even, so $\wp-e_{k}$ and the first four multipliers are; $\wp'$ is odd and $(\wp-e_{4})^{2}$ even, so the fifth is odd. ### M2-W117 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the twist carried round time]\label{def:twisttime}
Let $J_{s}$, with the coordinates $z_{s}=e^{-\kappa s}y+ie^{\kappa s}x$, be the family of complex structures carried round time that the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, give (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}), and let $D=\sum a_{i}P_{i}$ be a divisor on the corners. The \emph{twist carried round time}, for $D$, is the bundle $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ on $Y$ whose fibre over $(p,s)$ is that of the line $\mathcal{O}(D)$ of $(E,J_{s})$ over $p$, with the frames of Definition~\ref{def:dbar} for $J_{s}$: the constant $1$ away from the corner circles and, near the corner circle of $P_{i}$, $z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ with $z_{s,i}=z_{s}-z_{s}(P_{i})$. The fibres over $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ and over $(p,s+3L)$ are joined by the pull-back of functions by $M_{0}^{3}$: a section that is $f$ in the frame $1$ near $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ is $f\circ M_{0}^{3}$ in the frame $1$ near $(p,s+3L)$. Its first and its second lift are, at each time, the first and the second lift of M1, Definition~7.4. A section $\sigma$ of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ is \emph{differentiable along the fibre} when, in each of the frames $e$, it is $\sigma=fe$ with $f$ continuous and with derivatives along the fibre that are continuous in $(p,s)$; then
\[
\bar\partial\sigma\;=\;\frac{\partial f}{\partial\bar z_{s}}\,\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\otimes e ,
\]
which at each time $s$ is the $\bar\partial$ of Definition~\ref{def:dbar} for $J_{s}$.
\end{definition}
```
- **Place:** `supply 110`; after M2-W116
- **Step:** S14b
- **Rests on:** M2-W116; M2-W039 (`def:time`: $Y$, the join by $M_{0}^{3}$, the corner circles), M2-W040 (a family of complex structures carried round time), M2-W048 (the family of the lengths, $z_{s}$), M2-W069 (which takes that family), M2-W105 (`def:dbar`: the frames, "differentiable", $\bar\partial$); M1 v7.3, Definition 7.4 (`def:states`, ll.832, 834), Proposition 5.8 (`prop:A2`, l.704, through M2-W048)
- **Mark:** A definition, confirmed by read-back (P242); no numbered choice: it takes M1's twist (l.832) and the operator of M2-W105 at each time $s$ for the complex structure $J_{s}$ that M2-W048 and M2-W069 already take, and joins the fibres as M2-W039 joins the points and M2-W040 the forms, by $M_{0}^{3}$. READ: M2-W105 as logged ("Away from the corners the constant $1$ is a frame of $\mathcal{O}(D)$, and near $P_{i}$, with $z_{i}=z-P_{i}$, so is $z_{i}^{-a_{i}}$."); M2-W039 as logged ("in which $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ are one point"); `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.832 ("the line $\mathcal{O}(D)$ on $E$ of a divisor $D=\sum a_{i}P_{i}$ on the corners"; no red-flag hit), l.834 (the two lifts; the line has a red-flag hit, "the statements below speak of one lift or the other", in a sentence not used); l.704 is not read here: the lengths come through M2-W048. Hand step, in full: $z_{s,i}=z_{s}-z_{s}(P_{i})$ is, for $p$ near $P_{i}$, $z_{s}(p-P_{i})$, $z_{s}$ being linear; it is the $z_{i}$ of M2-W105 for $J_{s}$. That the join carries frame to frame, so that the bundle is a line bundle on $Y$, is not part of the definition: it is M2-W118(ii), proved in M2-W119. **Not shown and not claimed** (of these the PI was told the first before confirming, P242; the others are said here): how smooth a section is in the direction of time ("differentiable along the fibre" asks only that the derivatives along the fibre be continuous in $(p,s)$); how smooth a state is (M2-W005 does not say); anything on space, on the base or on the unfolded geometry. The symbol $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ and the words "twist carried round time" and "differentiable along the fibre" are new in the series as names

### M2-W118 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the fibre at every time]\label{prop:fibretime}
Let $J_{s}$, $z_{s}$, $D$ and $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:twisttime}. (i) At each time $s$, $J_{s}$ is the only complex structure on the tangent planes of the fibre that keeps the lengths $e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$ of M1, Proposition~5.8, on the fibre at $s$ and the handedness of $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$. (ii) $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ is a complex line bundle on $Y$: the join carries the frames at the time $s$ to the frames at the time $s+3L$. Its first and its second lift are lifts of the fold (Definition~\ref{def:time}), with the signs $(-1)^{a_{i}}$ and $-(-1)^{a_{i}}$ on the corner circle of $P_{i}$. The join carries sections differentiable along the fibre to sections differentiable along the fibre and $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$, the $(0,1)$-forms being joined as in Definition~\ref{def:spinortime}; $\bar\partial$ commutes with the action on sections of the first and of the second lift; and for $\sigma$ differentiable along the fibre, $\bar\partial\sigma=0$ exactly when, at each time $s$, $\sigma$ is a state at rest of M1, Definition~7.4, for $J_{s}$. (iii) For $D$ one of the five divisors of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iv), $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ with its lift with two states on the pillowcase is carried to the line of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(iii) with its lift of the fold by an isomorphism that covers the identity of $Y$ and carries lift to lift. (iv) Let $\tau_{Y}$ be the map $(p,s)\mapsto(M_{0}p,s-L)$ of $Y$, and $D'=\sum a'_{i}P_{i}$ with $a'_{i}$ the integer of $D$ at $M_{0}P_{i}$. The pull-back of functions by $M_{0}^{-1}$, which carries a section that is $f$ in the frame $1$ near $(p,s)$ to the section that is $f\circ M_{0}^{-1}$ in the frame $1$ near $(M_{0}p,s-L)$, is a continuous map $\nu_{D}$ that carries the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D')$ over each point $y$ of $Y$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ over $\tau_{Y}(y)$. It carries the first lift to the first and the second to the second, sections differentiable along the fibre onto sections differentiable along the fibre, and, the $(0,1)$-forms being carried by the pull-back by $\tau_{Y}^{-1}$, $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$. (v) A continuous map that carries the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D')$ over each $y$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ over $\tau_{Y}(y)$, and carries sections differentiable along the fibre to such sections and $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$, is $\nu_{D}$ multiplied by a continuous function with no zero that depends on the time alone. The maps $\nu_{3P_{2}}$, $\nu_{3P_{3}}$ and $\nu_{3P_{4}}$ carry $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$ to $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{2})$, $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{2})$ to $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{3})$ and $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{3})$ to $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$, and the composite of the three is the identity of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$. (vi) There is no complex structure $J$ of $E$ with a coordinate linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$ for which $M_{0}$ is holomorphic from $(E,J)$ to $(E,J)$; $M_{0}$ is holomorphic from the fibre at $s$ to the fibre at $s-L$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:step}). \rigD
\end{proposition}
```

### M2-W119 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i) and (ii). With $u=e^{-\kappa s}y$ and $v=e^{\kappa s}x$ the lengths of the fibre at $s$ are $\mathrm{d}u^{2}+\mathrm{d}v^{2}$ and $z_{s}=u+iv$. A linear map of the plane that keeps $\mathrm{d}u^{2}+\mathrm{d}v^{2}$ carries $(1,0)$ to a vector $(a,b)$ of length one and $(0,1)$ to $(-b,a)$ or to $(b,-a)$. In the second case its square is the identity; in the first its square carries $(1,0)$ to $(a^{2}-b^{2},2ab)$, which is $(-1,0)$ exactly when $a=0$ and $b=\pm1$. So the maps that keep the lengths and have square $-1$ are two: the one that carries $\partial_{u}$ to $\partial_{v}$, which is $J_{s}$, and its negative, which reverses the handedness; $J_{s}$ keeps the handedness of $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}). This is (i). For (ii): $z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}), and $M_{0}^{3}$ leaves each corner in place (M1, Corollary~7.8), so that near $P_{i}$, $z_{s,i}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L,i}$: the pull-back by $M_{0}^{3}$ carries the frame $1$ at the time $s$ to the frame $1$ at the time $s+3L$, and $z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ to $z_{s+3L,i}^{-a_{i}}$. Where two of the frames meet they differ by the factor $z_{s,i}^{a_{i}}$, which is continuous in $(p,s)$ and has no zero there: $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ is a complex line bundle on $Y$. $M_{0}^{3}$ commutes with the fold, so the first and the second lift, taken at each time, respect the join; at each time they are lifts of the fold with the signs $(-1)^{a_{i}}$ and $-(-1)^{a_{i}}$ at $P_{i}$ (step~(i) of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}), and they are lifts of the fold to $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ with these signs on the corner circles. If $f$ is continuous with derivatives along the fibre that are continuous in $(p,s)$, so is $f\circ M_{0}^{3}$, and since $z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$, $\partial(f\circ M_{0}^{3})/\partial\bar z_{s+3L}=(\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s})\circ M_{0}^{3}$, while the pull-back by $M_{0}^{3}$ carries $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ to $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s+3L}$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}): the join carries $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$. At each time $s$ a section differentiable along the fibre is a differentiable section of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ of $(E,J_{s})$, and the last two clauses of (ii) are Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iii) and~(ii) at each time.
```
- **Place:** `supply 130`; the first step of the proof of Proposition `prop:fibretime` (M2-W118)
- **Step:** S14b
- **Rests on:** M2-W117; M2-W026 (the two lifts as lifts of the fold; their signs), M2-W039, M2-W040 (the forms joined by the pull-back by $M_{0}^{3}$), M2-W048 ($z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$; the handedness; $\delta=1$), M2-W050 ($M_{0}^{3}$ near a corner), M2-W106(ii), (iii), M2-W107 (the change of frame); M1 v7.3, Proposition 5.8 (l.704, through M2-W048 and not read here), Corollary 7.8 (l.952)
- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full, and by script (`papers/M2/checks/S14b_fibre_time.py`, parts (1), (2), (3) and (7), numerically at sample points and times). READ: M2-W048 as logged ("$z_{s}=e^{-\kappa s}y+ie^{\kappa s}x$ keeps the handedness, has $\lvert\mathrm{d}z_{s}\rvert^{2}=e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$, the lengths at $s$, and $z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$"; and "the pull-back by $M_{0}^{3}$ carries $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ at $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ to $\overline{\delta(s)}\,\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s+3L}$ at $(p,s+3L)$", with $\delta=1$); `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.952 ("which leaves every corner in place"); M2-W026 as logged. Hand steps, in full: a linear map that keeps $\mathrm{d}u^{2}+\mathrm{d}v^{2}$ keeps the inner product, so the images of $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$ have length one and are at right angles, and the unit vectors at right angles to $(a,b)$ are $\pm(-b,a)$; the map with columns $(a,b)$, $(b,-a)$ has square with first column $(a^{2}+b^{2},0)=(1,0)$; the map with columns $(a,b)$, $(-b,a)$ has square with first column $(a^{2}-b^{2},2ab)$, and $a^{2}-b^{2}=-1$ with $a^{2}+b^{2}=1$ gives $a=0$, $b=\pm1$; $J_{s}$ is multiplication by $i$ in $z_{s}=u+iv$, which carries $\partial_{u}$ to $\partial_{v}$; if $(w,Jw)$ has the handedness, $(w,-Jw)$ has the other; a complex structure on the tangent planes that keeps the lengths is at each point such a linear map. For $p$ near $P_{i}$, $M_{0}^{3}p-P_{i}\equiv M_{0}^{3}(p-P_{i})$ modulo $\Z^{2}$ (the mark of M2-W050), so $z_{s,i}(M_{0}^{3}p)=z_{s}(M_{0}^{3}(p-P_{i}))=z_{s+3L}(p-P_{i})=z_{s+3L,i}(p)$. $z_{s,i}$ is continuous in $(p,s)$ with its only zero on the corner circle of $P_{i}$. The signs do not depend on $s$, $(-1)^{a_{i}}$ being the same at every time. The chain rule: for $h=f\circ M_{0}^{3}$ and $w=z_{s}$, $h$ is $f$ read in the coordinate $z_{s+3L}=z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}$, so its derivative in $\bar z_{s+3L}$ at $p$ is that of $f$ in $\bar z_{s}$ at $M_{0}^{3}p$. Changing the frame multiplies $f$ by $z_{s,i}^{a_{i}}$, holomorphic at each time and continuous in $(p,s)$ with its derivatives along the fibre, so "differentiable along the fibre" and $\bar\partial$ do not depend on the frame, as in M2-W107 at each time. In this entry $(a,b)$ is a vector of the plane; it is not the integer $a_{i}$ of the divisor. **This step and M2-W125 (M2-W120 before P243) are the weakest of the card:** no certificate checks them. It brings in no reading beyond the identification steps named in the mark of M2-W118
- **Tag:** none (a step of a proof)
- **Failure test:** a third linear map of the plane that keeps the lengths and has square $-1$; a frame at the time $s$ that the pull-back by $M_{0}^{3}$ does not carry to the frame at the time $s+3L$; or a function $f$ for which the derivative of $f\circ M_{0}^{3}$ in $\bar z_{s+3L}$ is not that of $f$ in $\bar z_{s}$ carried back.

### M2-W120 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii) and (iv). Let $D$ be one of the five divisors of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iv). By (ii), $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ with its lift with two states on the pillowcase is a line on $Y$ with a lift of the fold; its restriction to the fibre over $s=0$ is the line $\mathcal{O}(D)$ of $(E,J_{0})$, which has degree three (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}), with a lift whose signs are $+1,+1,+1,-1$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iv)). The line of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(iii) with its lift of the fold is another such line, and by Proposition~\ref{prop:time}(ii) the two are carried one to the other by an isomorphism that covers the identity of $Y$ and carries lift to lift. This is (iii). For (iv): $z_{s-L}\circ M_{0}=z_{s}$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:step}), and $M_{0}$ carries $P_{i}$ to $M_{0}P_{i}$, so that near $P_{i}$ the coordinate $z_{s-L}-z_{s-L}(M_{0}P_{i})$ of the fibre at $s-L$, carried back by $M_{0}$, is $z_{s,i}$. The pull-back by $M_{0}$ therefore carries the frame of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ of $(E,J_{s-L})$ near $M_{0}P_{i}$, the power $-a'_{i}$ of that coordinate, to $z_{s,i}^{-a'_{i}}$, the frame of $\mathcal{O}(D')$ of $(E,J_{s})$ near $P_{i}$, and the frame $1$ to the frame $1$ (the arithmetic of the moved divisor is certified in \texttt{L7\_corners}, \texttt{L7\_moved\_flux} and \texttt{L7\_moved\_signs}). Its inverse $\nu_{D}$, the pull-back by $M_{0}^{-1}$, carries frame to frame, so that it is continuous and carries the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D')$ over $y$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ over $\tau_{Y}(y)$; it respects the join, $M_{0}$ commuting with $M_{0}^{3}$ (\texttt{L7\_cube}). $M_{0}$ commutes with the fold, so $\nu_{D}$ carries the first lift to the first and the second to the second. For $f$ continuous with derivatives along the fibre that are continuous in $(p,s)$, so is $f\circ M_{0}^{-1}$, and $\partial(f\circ M_{0}^{-1})/\partial\bar z_{s-L}=(\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s})\circ M_{0}^{-1}$, while the pull-back by $\tau_{Y}^{-1}$ carries $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ to $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s-L}$: $\nu_{D}$ carries $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$.
```

### M2-W121 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(v). Let $\nu'$ be a map as in (v). For $w\neq0$ in the fibre over $y$, $\nu'(w)=c(y)\,\nu_{D}(w)$ for a number $c(y)\neq0$ that does not depend on $w$, and $c\colon Y\to\C\setminus\{0\}$ is continuous. The frames of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D')$ are differentiable along the fibre with $\bar\partial=0$, and $\nu_{D}$ carries them to the frames $e$ of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$; so $\nu'$ carries them to the sections $b\,e$, with $b=c\circ\tau_{Y}^{-1}$, which are differentiable along the fibre with $\bar\partial(b\,e)=0$. At each time, therefore, $b$ is continuously differentiable on the fibre with $\partial b/\partial\bar z=0$ at every point; as in the last step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}, it is an elliptic function with no pole, and so a constant \cite[\S20.12]{WhittakerWatson}. So $b$, and with it $c$, depends on the time alone. For the second sentence: the divisor $D'$ of $D=3P_{2}$ is $3P_{4}$, that of $3P_{3}$ is $3P_{2}$, and that of $3P_{4}$ is $3P_{3}$ (\texttt{L7\_chain}). The composite of $\nu_{3P_{2}}$, $\nu_{3P_{3}}$ and $\nu_{3P_{4}}$ carries a section that is $f$ in the frame $1$ near $(p,s)$ to the section that is $f\circ M_{0}^{-3}$ in the frame $1$ near $(M_{0}^{3}p,s-3L)$, which is the point $(p,s)$; by the join, a section that is $f\circ M_{0}^{-3}$ near $(M_{0}^{3}p,s-3L)$ is $f\circ M_{0}^{-3}\circ M_{0}^{3}=f$ near $(p,s)$. In the same way it carries each frame near a corner circle to itself. The composite is the identity.
```
- **Place:** `supply 150`; the third step of the proof of Proposition `prop:fibretime`
- **Step:** S14b
- **Rests on:** M2-W117, M2-W118 (the map of its part (v)), M2-W119, M2-W125 (in place of M2-W120, retired; flagged at P243: the statement is unchanged); M2-W039 (the join of the points), M2-W107 (its hand step), M2-W110 (the last step of the proof of Proposition `prop:fibre`); Verified Reference V-M2-13 (§20.12 (IV))
- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full; certificate `L7_chain`, `L7_moved_thrice`, `L7_cube` (lists of integers and integer matrices only); script `papers/M2/checks/S14b_fibre_time.py`, parts (5) and (7). Verified reference V-M2-13 (§20.12 (IV): "An elliptic function, f(z), with no poles in a cell is merely a constant."), READ at source by chat C8b, the row read by this chat. Hand steps, in full: $\nu'$ and $\nu_{D}$ are both linear and one-to-one from the fibre over $y$, a complex line, onto the fibre over $\tau_{Y}(y)$, so they differ by a number $c(y)\neq0$; $c$ is continuous since both maps are and $\nu_{D}$ carries frame to frame; a frame $e'$ has, in itself, the constant function $1$, so it is differentiable along the fibre with $\bar\partial e'=0$; $\nu'(e')$ at the point $\tau_{Y}(y)$ is $c(y)\,e(\tau_{Y}(y))$, that is the section $(c\circ\tau_{Y}^{-1})\,e$; $\nu'$ carries $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$, so $\bar\partial(b\,e)$ is the image of $\bar\partial e'=0$; in both kinds of frame the function of $b\,e$ is $b$, so $b$ has continuous derivatives along the fibre with $\partial b/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$ away from the corners and near them; by the hand step of M2-W107 it has a complex derivative at every point, and read on $\C$ through $z_{s}$ it has the two periods; $c=b\circ\tau_{Y}$, and $\tau_{Y}$ carries the fibre at $s$ to the fibre at $s-L$, so $c$ is constant on each fibre. The three maps: $\nu_{3P_{2}}$ goes from $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$ to $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{2})$, and so on, by `L7_chain`; three pull-backs by $M_{0}^{-1}$ are the pull-back by $M_{0}^{-3}$, from near $(p,s)$ to near $(M_{0}^{3}p,s-3L)$; by M2-W039, $(p,(s-3L)+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s-3L)$ are one point, and by M2-W117 a section that is $g$ near $(M_{0}^{3}p,s-3L)$ is $g\circ M_{0}^{3}$ near $(p,s)$; near a corner circle the three maps carry frame to frame (M2-W125) and the join carries frame to frame (M2-W119), so the composite carries the frame $z_{s,4}^{-3}$ to itself. **Not shown and not claimed:** that the function of the time alone is a constant; anything for maps that do not keep $\bar\partial$

### M2-W122 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(vi). A complex structure $J$ of $E$ with a coordinate $z$ linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$ is, at every point, one and the same linear map of $\R^{2}$ with $J^{2}=-1$, and $M_{0}$, whose differential at every point is the matrix $M_{0}$, is holomorphic from $(E,J)$ to $(E,J)$ exactly when $M_{0}J=JM_{0}$. For a real matrix $J=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}p&q\\r&t\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$, $M_{0}J=JM_{0}$ exactly when $r=q$ and $t=p-q$; the first entry of the square of $\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}p&q\\q&p-q\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$ is $p^{2}+q^{2}$, which is not $-1$ (the arithmetic is certified over the integers in \texttt{L7\_commutant}, \texttt{L7\_square\_entry} and \texttt{L7\_not\_minus\_one}). So there is no such $J$. That $M_{0}$ is holomorphic from the fibre at $s$ to the fibre at $s-L$ is in the first step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:step}.
```

- **Step:** S14b

- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full; certificate `L7_commutant`, `L7_square_entry`, `L7_not_minus_one`, which certify the statement for integer entries only; for real entries it is this hand step; script `papers/M2/checks/S14b_fibre_time.py`, part (4) (integers in a range; real entries at sample values). READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.310 ("for $M\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\Z)$ it is the map $x\mapsto Mx \bmod \Z^{2}$ of $\Ttwo=\R^{2}/\Z^{2}$"); M2-W071 as logged ("$M_{0}$ is holomorphic from the fibre at $s$ to the fibre at $s-L$"). Hand steps, in full: with $z=\alpha\theta_{\psi}+\beta\theta_{\omega}$, $J$ is the real-linear map of $\R^{2}$ that $z$ carries to multiplication by $i$, the same at every point; $M_{0}$ is holomorphic from $(E,J)$ to $(E,J)$ when $z\circ M_{0}$ is a holomorphic function of $z$; $z\circ M_{0}$ is real-linear, so it is $\alpha z+\beta\bar z$ for two numbers $\alpha,\beta$, and it is holomorphic exactly when $\beta=0$, that is exactly when $z\circ M_{0}\circ J=i\,z\circ M_{0}$; since $z\circ J=i\,z$ and $z$ is one-to-one, that is exactly when $M_{0}J=JM_{0}$ (written out after the fresh reader's check, P243: the mark had the sentence "a differentiable map between surfaces with complex structures is holomorphic exactly when its differential commutes with the two structures" with no computation); $M_{0}J=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}2p+r&2q+t\\p+r&q+t\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$ and $JM_{0}=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}2p+q&p+q\\2r+t&r+t\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$; the four equations are $r=q$, $t=p-q$, $p=r+t$ and $r=q$ again, and the third follows from the first two; $\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}p&q\\q&p-q\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)^{2}$ has $p\cdot p+q\cdot q$ in its first place; for real $p,q$, $p^{2}+q^{2}\geq0$. The letter $t$ is used here for an entry of the matrix; it is not the trace of M1, Section 4; the certificate and the script write $s$ for it. **Not shown and not claimed:** the same for a complex structure of $E$ whose coordinate is not linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$
- **Tag:** none (a step of a proof)
- **Failure test:** a real matrix $J$ with $J^{2}=-1$ and $M_{0}J=JM_{0}$.
- **Confirmed:** checked, not read back, 19 November 2026 (the PI told in plain English in the read-back of S14b; Decision Log P242)
- **Retires / retired by:** none

```tex
(iii) and (iv). Let $D$ be one of the five divisors of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iv). By (ii), $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ with its lift with two states on the pillowcase is a line on $Y$ with a lift of the fold; its restriction to the fibre over $s=0$ is the line $\mathcal{O}(D)$ of $(E,J_{0})$, which has degree three (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}), with a lift whose signs are $+1,+1,+1,-1$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iv)). The line of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(iii) with its lift of the fold is another such line, and by Proposition~\ref{prop:time}(ii) the two are carried one to the other by an isomorphism that covers the identity of $Y$ and carries lift to lift. This is (iii). For (iv): $z_{s-L}\circ M_{0}=z_{s}$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:step}), and $M_{0}$ carries $P_{i}$ to $M_{0}P_{i}$, so that near $P_{i}$ the coordinate $z_{s-L}-z_{s-L}(M_{0}P_{i})$ of the fibre at $s-L$, carried back by $M_{0}$, is $z_{s,i}$. The pull-back by $M_{0}$ therefore carries the frame of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ of $(E,J_{s-L})$ near $M_{0}P_{i}$, the power $-a'_{i}$ of that coordinate, to $z_{s,i}^{-a'_{i}}$, the frame of $\mathcal{O}(D')$ of $(E,J_{s})$ near $P_{i}$, and the frame $1$ to the frame $1$ (how $M_{0}$ moves the corners is certified in \texttt{L7\_corners}, and, for the twenty divisors of flux three, that the moved divisor has flux three in \texttt{L7\_moved\_flux}). Its inverse $\nu_{D}$, the pull-back by $M_{0}^{-1}$, carries frame to frame, so that it is continuous and carries the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D')$ over $y$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ over $\tau_{Y}(y)$; it respects the join, $M_{0}$ commuting with $M_{0}^{3}$ (\texttt{L7\_cube}). $M_{0}$ commutes with the fold, so $\nu_{D}$ carries the first lift to the first and the second to the second. For $f$ continuous with derivatives along the fibre that are continuous in $(p,s)$, so is $f\circ M_{0}^{-1}$, and in the same way so is $g\circ M_{0}$ for such a $g$, so that $\nu_{D}$ carries the sections differentiable along the fibre onto the sections differentiable along the fibre; and $\partial(f\circ M_{0}^{-1})/\partial\bar z_{s-L}=(\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s})\circ M_{0}^{-1}$, while the pull-back by $\tau_{Y}^{-1}$ carries $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ to $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s-L}$: $\nu_{D}$ carries $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$.
```

### M2-W128 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the direction of time]\label{prop:timedir}
Let $Y$, $J_{s}$, $z_{s}$, $D=\sum a_{i}P_{i}$ and $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:twisttime}, let $x,y$ be the coordinates of M1, Proposition~5.8, and let $\partial_{s}$ be the vector field on $Y$ of the curves $t\mapsto(p,s+t)$, so that $\partial_{s}f$ is the derivative of $f$ in $s$ at a fixed point $p$ of the fibre. (i) In the lengths $\mathrm{d}s^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$ of M1, Proposition~5.8, $\partial_{s}$ has length one and is at right angles to the fibre, and the only vectors of length one at right angles to the fibre are $\partial_{s}$ and $-\partial_{s}$. The join of Definition~\ref{def:time}, the fold and the map $\tau_{Y}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime}(iv) carry $\partial_{s}$ to $\partial_{s}$; the map $(s,x,y)\mapsto(-s,-y,x)$ of M1, Proposition~5.8, which keeps the lengths, carries $\partial_{s}$ to $-\partial_{s}$. For a function $f$ on $Y$ with continuous first derivatives, $\partial_{s}f$ is a continuous function on $Y$. (ii) At a fixed point of the fibre, $\partial_{s}z_{s}=-\kappa\,\bar z_{s}$. The derivative in $s$ of the form $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=e^{-\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y-ie^{\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x$, taken coefficient by coefficient in $\mathrm{d}x$ and $\mathrm{d}y$, is $-\kappa\,\mathrm{d}z_{s}$, which is not a $(0,1)$-form of $J_{s}$. For $s'\neq s$, $J_{s'}$ is not $J_{s}$. (iii) Let $\sigma$ be a section of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ that, near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ and away from it, is $\sigma=f\cdot1=g\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$, where $g$ has continuous first derivatives near the corner circle. Then, away from the corner circle,
\[
(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1\;=\;\Bigl(\partial_{s}g+a_{i}\kappa\,\frac{\bar z_{s,i}}{z_{s,i}}\,g\Bigr)\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}} .
\]
The function $\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ has modulus one and takes every value of modulus one in every neighbourhood of every point of the corner circle. The section $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$, which is defined away from the corner circles, is therefore continuous up to a point of the corner circle of $P_{i}$ exactly when $a_{i}g=0$ at that point; and where $a_{i}\neq0$ the sections $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$ and $(\partial_{s}g)\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ differ wherever $g\neq0$. Each of the twenty divisors of flux three on the corners has a corner with $a_{i}\neq0$. (iv) Away from the corner circles, the join, the first and the second lift, and the map $\nu_{D}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime}(iv) carry the section $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$ made from a section $f\cdot1$ to the one made from its image. For a function $c$ of the time alone with no zero and with a continuous derivative, the map $c\,\nu_{D}$ does so exactly when $c$ is constant. (v) Let $A$ be a continuous function on $Y$ away from the corner circles. The section $(\partial_{s}f+Af)\cdot1$ is continuous over the corner circles, for every section $\sigma$ as in (iii) at each corner circle, exactly when, for each $i$, $A+a_{i}\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is continuous up to the corner circle of $P_{i}$. Such functions $A$ exist, and if $A$ is one of them the others are the functions $A+B$, $B$ a continuous function on $Y$. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```

### M2-W129 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i) and (ii). In the coordinates $(s,x,y)$ the lengths $\mathrm{d}s^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$ have no term in which $\mathrm{d}s$ stands with $\mathrm{d}x$ or with $\mathrm{d}y$. The vector $\partial_{s}$, whose components are $(1,0,0)$, therefore has length one and is at right angles to every vector along the fibre; and a vector with the components $(a,b,c)$ that is at right angles to $\partial_{x}$ and to $\partial_{y}$ has $e^{2\kappa s}b=0$ and $e^{-2\kappa s}c=0$, so that it is $a\,\partial_{s}$, of length $\lvert a\rvert$. The curves $t\mapsto(p,s+3L+t)$ and $t\mapsto(M_{0}^{3}p,s+t)$ are one curve of $Y$ (Definition~\ref{def:time}), so that $\partial_{s}$ is a vector field on $Y$ and the join carries $\partial_{s}$ to $\partial_{s}$. The fold carries the curve $t\mapsto(p,s+t)$ to $t\mapsto(\iota(p),s+t)$, and $\tau_{Y}$ carries it to $t\mapsto(M_{0}p,s-L+t)$: each carries $\partial_{s}$ to $\partial_{s}$. The map $(s,x,y)\mapsto(-s,-y,x)$ ``keeps these lengths'' (M1, Proposition~5.8) and carries the curve $t\mapsto(s+t,x,y)$ to $t\mapsto(-s-t,-y,x)$, so that it carries $\partial_{s}$ to $-\partial_{s}$. A function on $Y$ is a function $f$ of $(p,s)$ with $f(p,s+3L)=f(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$; differentiating in $s$ gives the same for $\partial_{s}f$, which is continuous when the first derivatives of $f$ are. This is (i). For (ii): $z_{s}=e^{-\kappa s}y+ie^{\kappa s}x$, so that at fixed $x$ and $y$, $\partial_{s}z_{s}=-\kappa e^{-\kappa s}y+i\kappa e^{\kappa s}x=-\kappa\,(e^{-\kappa s}y-ie^{\kappa s}x)=-\kappa\,\bar z_{s}$ (the signs are certified, with integers in the place of the real numbers, in \texttt{L8\_dz}). In the same way the derivative in $s$ of the coefficients of $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=e^{-\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y-ie^{\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x$ gives $-\kappa e^{-\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y-i\kappa e^{\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x=-\kappa\,\mathrm{d}z_{s}$. The $(0,1)$-forms of $J_{s}$ are the multiples of $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, and $-\kappa\,\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ is not one: the determinant of the coefficients of $\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ and of $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ in $\mathrm{d}x$ and $\mathrm{d}y$ is $2i$, and $\kappa\neq0$ (M1, Definition~2.4(ii)). If $J_{s'}=J_{s}$, then $z_{s'}$ is a coordinate of $J_{s}$ that is linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$, and so is $c\,z_{s}$ for a constant $c\neq0$ (the first step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}); comparing the coefficients of $y$ and of $x$ gives $c=e^{-\kappa(s'-s)}$ and $c=e^{\kappa(s'-s)}$, so that $e^{2\kappa(s'-s)}=1$ and $s'=s$.
```
- **Place:** `supply 210`; the first step of the proof of Proposition `prop:timedir` (M2-W128)
- **Step:** S14b2
- **Rests on:** M2-W039 (the join of the points; the fold on $Y$), M2-W048 ($z_{s}$), M2-W073 ($\tau_{Y}$), M2-W107 (another coordinate of $J$ that is linear is $cz$); M1 v7.3, Proposition 5.8 (l.704), Definition 2.4 (l.190)
- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full; certificate `L8_dz` (the pair of the derivatives of $(u,v)$ is $-k$ times the conjugate pair, over the integers; it certifies nothing for real numbers); script `papers/M2/checks/S14b2_time.py`, checks (1), (2a), (2b), (2c), (3), (4a), (4b). READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.704 ("The map $(s,x,y)\mapsto(-s,-y,x)$, time run backwards together with the quarter-turn, keeps these lengths, respects the join because $SM_{0}S^{-1}=M_{0}^{-1}$, and swaps $\kappa$ for $-\kappa$"; the quotation "keeps these lengths" checked by script, `S14b2_quotes.py`), l.190 ("for a constant $\kappa\neq0$"); M2-W039 as logged ("in which $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ are one point"; "The fold acts on $Y$ by $(p,s)\mapsto(\iota(p),s)$"); M2-W107 as logged ("Another coordinate of $J$ that is linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$ is $cz$ for a constant $c\neq0$"). Hand steps, in full: the squared length of $(a,b,c)$ is $a^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}b^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}c^{2}$ and the inner product of $(a,b,c)$ with $(0,1,0)$ is $e^{2\kappa s}b$, with $(0,0,1)$ it is $e^{-2\kappa s}c$; a vector along the fibre is a combination of $\partial_{x}$ and $\partial_{y}$; the coefficients of $\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ in $\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}y$ are $(ie^{\kappa s},e^{-\kappa s})$ and those of $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ are $(-ie^{\kappa s},e^{-\kappa s})$, with the determinant $ie^{\kappa s}e^{-\kappa s}+ie^{\kappa s}e^{-\kappa s}=2i$; if $J_{s'}=J_{s}$ the function $z_{s'}$ is holomorphic for $J_{s}$ and linear, so it is a coordinate of $J_{s}$ as M2-W107 has it; in $c\,z_{s}=z_{s'}$ the coefficient of $y$ gives $ce^{-\kappa s}=e^{-\kappa s'}$ and that of $x$ gives $ce^{\kappa s}=e^{\kappa s'}$. That M1's map "respects the join" is M1's and is not used here. It brings in no reading beyond the identification steps named in the mark of M2-W128

### M2-W130 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii). Near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ and away from it, $f=g\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$. For $p$ near $P_{i}$, $z_{s,i}(p)$ is $z_{s}$ of the difference $p-P_{i}$, $z_{s}$ being linear, so that by (ii) $\partial_{s}z_{s,i}=-\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}$, and
\[
\partial_{s}\bigl(z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}\bigr)\;=\;-a_{i}\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}-1}\cdot(-\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i})\;=\;a_{i}\kappa\,\frac{\bar z_{s,i}}{z_{s,i}}\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}
\]
(the product of the factors $-a_{i}$ and $-\kappa$ is certified, with integers in the place of the real numbers, in \texttt{L8\_frame\_factor}). By the rule for the derivative of a product, $\partial_{s}f=\bigl(\partial_{s}g+a_{i}\kappa\,(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,g\bigr)z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$, which is the display. At a time $s$, at the points of the fibre with $z_{s,i}=re^{i\theta}$, $r>0$, the function $\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is $e^{-2i\theta}$: it has modulus one, and on every small circle $r=\text{constant}$ about the corner it takes every value of modulus one. Let $q$ be a point of the corner circle; a section is \emph{continuous up to} $q$ when its function in the frame $z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ has a limit at $q$. The function of $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$ in that frame is $\partial_{s}g+a_{i}\kappa\,(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,g$, and $\partial_{s}g$ is continuous at $q$. If $a_{i}g(q)=0$, then $\lvert a_{i}\kappa\,(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,g\rvert=a_{i}\lvert\kappa\rvert\,\lvert g\rvert$ tends to $0$ at $q$, and the function has the limit $\partial_{s}g(q)$. If $a_{i}g(q)\neq0$, then in the fibre through $q$, along the ray of angle $\theta$, the second term tends to $a_{i}\kappa\,e^{-2i\theta}g(q)$, which for $\theta=0$ and for $\theta=\pi/2$ are two different numbers: the function has no limit at $q$. The difference of $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$ and $(\partial_{s}g)\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ is $a_{i}\kappa\,(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,\sigma$, which is not $0$ where $a_{i}\neq0$ and $g\neq0$, $\kappa$ being not $0$. There are twenty divisors of flux three on the corners, and each has a corner with $a_{i}\neq0$ (\texttt{L8\_twenty}, \texttt{L8\_some\_corner}).
```

- **Step:** S14b2

READ: M2-W117 as logged ("$z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ with $z_{s,i}=z_{s}-z_{s}(P_{i})$") and its mark ("$z_{s,i}=z_{s}-z_{s}(P_{i})$ is, for $p$ near $P_{i}$, $z_{s}(p-P_{i})$, $z_{s}$ being linear"). Hand steps, in full: $z^{-a}$ is a function of $z$ with the derivative $-az^{-a-1}$ where $z\neq0$, and $z_{s,i}\neq0$ away from the corner circle, so the chain rule gives the first equality of the display; $\bar z/z$ for $z=re^{i\theta}$ is $re^{-i\theta}/re^{i\theta}=e^{-2i\theta}$, and $\theta\mapsto e^{-2i\theta}$ takes every value of modulus one; every neighbourhood of $q$ contains such a circle in the fibre through $q$; $\partial_{s}g$ is continuous near the corner circle by hypothesis; if $a_{i}=0$ the second term is $0$, and if $g(q)=0$ then $\lvert g\rvert\to0$ at $q$, $g$ being continuous; if $a_{i}g(q)\neq0$, along the ray of angle $\theta$ in the fibre through $q$ the function $g$ tends to $g(q)$ and $\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is constantly $e^{-2i\theta}$, and $e^{0}=1\neq-1=e^{-i\pi}$; the difference of the two sections is $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1-(\partial_{s}g)z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}=a_{i}\kappa(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,g\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$. The words "continuous up to" are explained in the sentence and are not made a term of the glossary: they are used in this proposition only. **This is the weakest step of the card:** the "exactly when" is a hand argument on a limit; the script checks its inputs at sample points only. It brings in no reading beyond the matching named in the mark of M2-W128

### M2-W131 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iv). Away from the corner circles a section is $F\cdot1$ for a function $F$ of $(p,s)$. By Definition~\ref{def:twisttime} a section that is $f$ in the frame $1$ near $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ is $f\circ M_{0}^{3}$ near $(p,s+3L)$: $F(p,s+3L)=F(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$, and differentiating in $s$ gives the same for $\partial_{s}F$. The first lift carries the section with the function $F(p,s)$ to the one with $F(\iota(p),s)$, and the second to the one with $-F(\iota(p),s)$ (M1, Definition~7.4, at each time); $\nu_{D}$ carries it to the one with the function $H(q,t)=F(M_{0}^{-1}q,t+L)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime}(iv)). Each of these maps carries corner circles to corner circles. In each the point of the fibre is moved by a map that does not depend on the time, and the time is shifted by a constant, so that the derivative in the time of the image is the image of the derivative: $\partial_{t}H(q,t)=(\partial_{s}F)(M_{0}^{-1}q,t+L)$, and likewise for the lifts. The map $c\,\nu_{D}$ carries the section with the function $F$ to the one with $b\,H$, where $b=c\circ\tau_{Y}^{-1}$ is a function of the time alone (the third step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime}), with a continuous derivative $b'$ when $c$ has one; and $\partial_{t}(bH)=b\,\partial_{t}H+b'H$, while the image of $(\partial_{s}F)\cdot1$ is $b\,\partial_{t}H\cdot1$. The two are the same for every section exactly when $b'H=0$ for every $H$, that is when $b'=0$: $b$, and with it $c$, is constant.
```

### M2-W132 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(v). Let $\sigma$ be as in (iii) at the corner circle of $P_{i}$. Near that circle and away from it, by (iii),
\[
(\partial_{s}f+Af)\cdot1\;=\;\Bigl(\partial_{s}g+\bigl(A+a_{i}\kappa\,\frac{\bar z_{s,i}}{z_{s,i}}\bigr)\,g\Bigr)\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}} .
\]
If $A+a_{i}\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is continuous up to the corner circle, so is the function on the right, for every such $g$. Conversely the frame $z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ is itself such a section, with $g=1$ and $\partial_{s}g=0$, and for it the function on the right is $A+a_{i}\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$. This is the first sentence of (v). Such a function exists. For $0\leq s\leq3L$ and a vector $w$ of the plane with the components $(x,y)$ along $v_{1},v_{2}$, $\lvert z_{s}(w)\rvert^{2}=e^{-2\kappa s}y^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}x^{2}\geq e^{-6\lvert\kappa\rvert L}(x^{2}+y^{2})$, and two different corners differ by a vector with $x^{2}+y^{2}\geq\tfrac14$, as does a corner from itself moved by a vector of $\Z^{2}$ other than $0$. With $\delta=\tfrac14e^{-3\lvert\kappa\rvert L}$ the sets $\lvert z_{s,i}\rvert<\delta$ about the four corner circles are therefore discs in each fibre and are disjoint, for $0\leq s\leq3L$; and $\lvert z_{s,i}\rvert$ at the time $0$ and at the time $3L$ agree through the join, since $z_{s,i}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L,i}$ (the first step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime}). Let $\rho$ be a continuous function on $[0,\infty)$ that is $1$ on $[0,\delta/2]$ and $0$ on $[\delta,\infty)$, and let
\[
A\;=\;-\sum_{i}a_{i}\kappa\,\rho(\lvert z_{s,i}\rvert)\,\frac{\bar z_{s,i}}{z_{s,i}} ,
\]
the term of $i$ being $0$ outside the set $\lvert z_{s,i}\rvert<\delta$. It is a continuous function on $Y$ away from the corner circles, and where $0<\lvert z_{s,i}\rvert\leq\delta/2$ the function $A+a_{i}\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is $0$. If $A$ and $A'$ are two such functions, $A'-A$ is continuous away from the corner circles and, near the corner circle of $P_{i}$, is the difference of $A'+a_{i}\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ and $A+a_{i}\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$, which are continuous up to it: $A'-A$ extends to a continuous function $B$ on $Y$. And for a continuous function $B$ on $Y$, $A+B$ is such a function when $A$ is.
```

- **Step:** S14b2

### M2-W135 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i) and (ii). In the coordinates $(s,x,y)$ the lengths $\mathrm{d}s^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y^{2}$ have no term in which $\mathrm{d}s$ stands with $\mathrm{d}x$ or with $\mathrm{d}y$. The vector $\partial_{s}$, whose components are $(1,0,0)$, therefore has length one and is at right angles to every vector along the fibre; and a vector with the components $(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)$ that is at right angles to $\partial_{x}$ and to $\partial_{y}$ has $e^{2\kappa s}\beta=0$ and $e^{-2\kappa s}\gamma=0$, so that it is $\alpha\,\partial_{s}$, of length $\lvert\alpha\rvert$, which is one exactly when $\alpha=1$ or $\alpha=-1$. The curves $t\mapsto(p,s+3L+t)$ and $t\mapsto(M_{0}^{3}p,s+t)$ are one curve of $Y$ (Definition~\ref{def:time}), so that $\partial_{s}$ is a vector field on $Y$ and the join carries $\partial_{s}$ to $\partial_{s}$. The fold carries the curve $t\mapsto(p,s+t)$ to $t\mapsto(\iota(p),s+t)$, and $\tau_{Y}$ carries it to $t\mapsto(M_{0}p,s-L+t)$: each carries $\partial_{s}$ to $\partial_{s}$. The map $(s,x,y)\mapsto(-s,-y,x)$ ``keeps these lengths'' (M1, Proposition~5.8) and carries the curve $t\mapsto(s+t,x,y)$ to $t\mapsto(-s-t,-y,x)$, so that it carries $\partial_{s}$ to $-\partial_{s}$. A function on $Y$ is a function $f$ of $(p,s)$ with $f(p,s+3L)=f(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$; differentiating in $s$ gives the same for $\partial_{s}f$, which is continuous when the first derivatives of $f$ are. This is (i). For (ii): $z_{s}=e^{-\kappa s}y+ie^{\kappa s}x$, so that at fixed $x$ and $y$, $\partial_{s}z_{s}=-\kappa e^{-\kappa s}y+i\kappa e^{\kappa s}x=-\kappa\,(e^{-\kappa s}y-ie^{\kappa s}x)=-\kappa\,\bar z_{s}$ (the signs are certified, with integers in the place of the real numbers, in \texttt{L8\_dz}). In the same way the derivative in $s$ of the coefficients of $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=e^{-\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y-ie^{\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x$ gives $-\kappa e^{-\kappa s}\mathrm{d}y-i\kappa e^{\kappa s}\mathrm{d}x=-\kappa\,\mathrm{d}z_{s}$. The $(0,1)$-forms of $J_{s}$ are the multiples of $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, and $-\kappa\,\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ is not one: the determinant of the coefficients of $\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ and of $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ in $\mathrm{d}x$ and $\mathrm{d}y$ is $2i$, and $\kappa\neq0$ (M1, Definition~2.4(ii)). If $J_{s'}=J_{s}$, then $z_{s'}$ is a coordinate of $J_{s}$ that is linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$, and so is $c\,z_{s}$ for a constant $c\neq0$ (the first step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}); comparing the coefficients of $y$ and of $x$ gives $c=e^{-\kappa(s'-s)}$ and $c=e^{\kappa(s'-s)}$, so that $e^{2\kappa(s'-s)}=1$ and $s'=s$.
```
- **Place:** `supply 210`; the first step of the proof of Proposition `prop:timedir` (M2-W128), in the place of M2-W129, which it replaces
- **Step:** S14b2
- **Rests on:** M2-W039 (the join of the points; the fold on $Y$), M2-W048 ($z_{s}$), M2-W073 ($\tau_{Y}$), M2-W107 (another coordinate of $J$ that is linear is $cz$); M1 v7.3, Proposition 5.8 (l.704), Definition 2.4 (l.190)
- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full; certificate `L8_dz` (the pair of the derivatives of $(u,v)$ is $-k$ times the conjugate pair, over the integers; it certifies nothing for real numbers); script `papers/M2/checks/S14b2_time.py`, checks (1), (2a), (2b), (2c), (3), (4a), (4b). READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.704 ("The map $(s,x,y)\mapsto(-s,-y,x)$, time run backwards together with the quarter-turn, keeps these lengths, respects the join because $SM_{0}S^{-1}=M_{0}^{-1}$, and swaps $\kappa$ for $-\kappa$"; the quotation "keeps these lengths" checked by script, `S14b2_quotes.py`), l.190 ("for a constant $\kappa\neq0$"); M2-W039 as logged ("in which $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ are one point"; "The fold acts on $Y$ by $(p,s)\mapsto(\iota(p),s)$"); M2-W107 as logged ("Another coordinate of $J$ that is linear in $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$ is $cz$ for a constant $c\neq0$"). Hand steps, in full: the squared length of $(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)$ is $\alpha^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}\beta^{2}+e^{-2\kappa s}\gamma^{2}$ and the inner product of $(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)$ with $(0,1,0)$ is $e^{2\kappa s}\beta$, with $(0,0,1)$ it is $e^{-2\kappa s}\gamma$; a vector along the fibre is a combination of $\partial_{x}$ and $\partial_{y}$; the coefficients of $\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ in $\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}y$ are $(ie^{\kappa s},e^{-\kappa s})$ and those of $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ are $(-ie^{\kappa s},e^{-\kappa s})$, with the determinant $ie^{\kappa s}e^{-\kappa s}+ie^{\kappa s}e^{-\kappa s}=2i$; if $J_{s'}=J_{s}$ the function $z_{s'}$ is holomorphic for $J_{s}$ and linear, so it is a coordinate of $J_{s}$ as M2-W107 has it; in $c\,z_{s}=z_{s'}$ the coefficient of $y$ gives $ce^{-\kappa s}=e^{-\kappa s'}$ and that of $x$ gives $ce^{\kappa s}=e^{\kappa s'}$. That M1's map "respects the join" is M1's and is not used here. It brings in no reading beyond the identification steps named in the mark of M2-W128. In this entry $t$ is the parameter of a curve and $c$ a constant; in M2-W131 $t$ is the time and $c$ a function of the time. Changed from M2-W129 (fresh reader, P247): the components of the vector are written $(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)$, the statement having used $c$ both for a component and for the constant of $c\,z_{s}$; and the clause "which is one exactly when $\alpha=1$ or $\alpha=-1$" is added, part (i) saying that the only vectors of length one at right angles to the fibre are $\partial_{s}$ and $-\partial_{s}$. Nothing else changed

### M2-W136 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii). Near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ and away from it, $f=g\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$. For $p$ near $P_{i}$, $z_{s,i}(p)$ is $z_{s}$ of the difference $p-P_{i}$, $z_{s}$ being linear, so that by (ii) $\partial_{s}z_{s,i}=-\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}$, and
\[
\partial_{s}\bigl(z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}\bigr)\;=\;-a_{i}\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}-1}\cdot(-\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i})\;=\;a_{i}\kappa\,\frac{\bar z_{s,i}}{z_{s,i}}\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}
\]
(the product of the factors $-a_{i}$ and $-\kappa$ is certified, with integers in the place of the real numbers, in \texttt{L8\_frame\_factor}). By the rule for the derivative of a product, $\partial_{s}f=\bigl(\partial_{s}g+a_{i}\kappa\,(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,g\bigr)z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$, which is the display. At a time $s$, at the points of the fibre with $z_{s,i}=re^{i\theta}$, $r>0$, the function $\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is $e^{-2i\theta}$: it has modulus one, and on every small circle $r=\text{constant}$ about the corner it takes every value of modulus one; every neighbourhood of a point of the corner circle contains such a circle, in the fibre through that point. Let $q$ be a point of the corner circle. A function that is defined near $q$ and away from the corner circle is \emph{continuous up to} $q$ when it has a limit at $q$, and a section is continuous up to $q$ when its function in the frame $z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ is; a function or a section is continuous up to the corner circle, or over it, when it is continuous up to each of its points. The function of $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$ in that frame is $\partial_{s}g+a_{i}\kappa\,(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,g$, and $\partial_{s}g$ is continuous at $q$. If $a_{i}g(q)=0$, then $\lvert a_{i}\kappa\,(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,g\rvert=a_{i}\lvert\kappa\rvert\,\lvert g\rvert$ tends to $0$ at $q$, and the function has the limit $\partial_{s}g(q)$. If $a_{i}g(q)\neq0$, then in the fibre through $q$, along the ray of angle $\theta$, the second term tends to $a_{i}\kappa\,e^{-2i\theta}g(q)$, which for $\theta=0$ and for $\theta=\pi/2$ are two different numbers: the function has no limit at $q$. The difference of $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$ and $(\partial_{s}g)\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ is $a_{i}\kappa\,(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,\sigma$, which is not $0$ where $a_{i}\neq0$ and $g\neq0$, $\kappa$ being not $0$. There are twenty divisors of flux three on the corners, and each has a corner with $a_{i}\neq0$ (\texttt{L8\_twenty}, \texttt{L8\_some\_corner}).
```
- **Place:** `supply 220`; the second step of the proof of Proposition `prop:timedir`, in the place of M2-W130, which it replaces
- **Step:** S14b2
- **Rests on:** M2-W135 (part (ii); in place of M2-W129, retired); M2-W117 ($z_{s,i}=z_{s}-z_{s}(P_{i})$; the two frames); M1 v7.3, Definition 2.4 (l.190, $\kappa\neq0$)
- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full; certificate `L8_frame_factor` ($-a\cdot-k=a\cdot k$ over the integers), `L8_twenty`, `L8_some_corner` (the list of the twenty divisors with every $a_{i}$ at most three and flux three, and that none has all $a_{i}$ zero); script `S14b2_time.py`, checks (5), (6a), (6b), (9a). READ: M2-W117 as logged ("$z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ with $z_{s,i}=z_{s}-z_{s}(P_{i})$") and its mark ("$z_{s,i}=z_{s}-z_{s}(P_{i})$ is, for $p$ near $P_{i}$, $z_{s}(p-P_{i})$, $z_{s}$ being linear"). Hand steps, in full: $z^{-a}$ is a function of $z$ with the derivative $-az^{-a-1}$ where $z\neq0$, and $z_{s,i}\neq0$ away from the corner circle, so the chain rule gives the first equality of the display; $\bar z/z$ for $z=re^{i\theta}$ is $re^{-i\theta}/re^{i\theta}=e^{-2i\theta}$, and $\theta\mapsto e^{-2i\theta}$ takes every value of modulus one; $\partial_{s}g$ is continuous near the corner circle by hypothesis; if $a_{i}=0$ the second term is $0$, and if $g(q)=0$ then $\lvert g\rvert\to0$ at $q$, $g$ being continuous; if $a_{i}g(q)\neq0$, along the ray of angle $\theta$ in the fibre through $q$ the function $g$ tends to $g(q)$ and $\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is constantly $e^{-2i\theta}$, and $e^{0}=1\neq-1=e^{-i\pi}$; the difference of the two sections is $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1-(\partial_{s}g)z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}=a_{i}\kappa(\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i})\,g\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$. The words "continuous up to" are defined in this entry, for a function and for a section, and are a term of the glossary (`papers/M2/GLOSSARY.md`); they are first used in M2-W128(iii) and (v), which stand before this entry in the paper, and are used in M2-W132 and M2-W133. In this entry $q$ is a point of a corner circle and $\theta$ an angle; in M2-W131 $q$ is a point of the fibre. Changed from M2-W130 (fresh reader, P247): the sentence on every neighbourhood, which stood in the mark only, is in the statement; and the words "continuous up to", which the statement explained for a section only while M2-W128(v) and M2-W132 use them of a function, are defined for both, with "over" the corner circle. Nothing else changed. **This is the weakest step of the card:** the "exactly when" is a hand argument on a limit; the script checks its inputs at sample points only. It brings in no reading beyond the matching named in the mark of M2-W128

### M2-W138 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
After Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime} it was said that nothing is made there that differentiates a state in the direction of time. By Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}(i), in the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, the only vectors of length one at right angles to the fibre are $\partial_{s}$ and $-\partial_{s}$, and for a function $f$ on $Y$ with continuous first derivatives $\partial_{s}f$ is a continuous function on $Y$; M1 counts the direction of time among what a relabelling carries (M1, Definition~3.3). By (iii), for a section $\sigma=f\cdot1=g\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ as there and a corner circle with $a_{i}\neq0$, the sections $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$ and $(\partial_{s}g)\,z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ differ wherever $g\neq0$, and the first is continuous up to a point of that corner circle exactly when $g=0$ at that point. By (v), for a continuous function $A$ on $Y$ away from the corner circles, the sections $(\partial_{s}f+Af)\cdot1$ are continuous over the corner circles, for every section $\sigma$ as in (iii), exactly when each $A+a_{i}\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is continuous up to the corner circle of $P_{i}$; such functions $A$ exist, and two of them differ by a continuous function on $Y$. By (iv), among the maps of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime}(v) whose function of the time alone has a continuous derivative, those that carry the derivative in $s$ in the frame $1$, away from the corner circles, to itself are the constant multiples of $\nu_{D}$. The constants $1$ and $-1$ are among them: on $Y$, the derivative in $s$ in the frame $1$ does not decide the sixth choice of this paper.
```

### M2-W140 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the five divisors at every time]\label{prop:fivetime}
Let $Y$, $J_{s}$, $z_{s}$, $z_{s,i}$ and $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:twisttime} and $\partial_{s}$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}. For each time $s$ let $\wp_{s}$ be the Weierstrass function of $E$ for the coordinate $z_{s}$, its periods being the values of $z_{s}$ at $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$ as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}, and $\wp_{s}'$ its derivative in $z_{s}$; read both as functions of the point $p$ of $E$ and of $s$, and let $e_{k}(s)=\wp_{s}(P_{k})$ for $k=2,3,4$. For $D=\sum a_{i}P_{i}$ one of the five divisors of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iv) let $m_{D}$ be the function of $(p,s)$ that at each time $s$ is the function named for $D$ there, made with $\wp_{s}$; and let $b_{4}=3$ and $b_{1}=b_{2}=b_{3}=0$. (i) Away from the corner circle of $P_{1}$, $\wp_{s}(p)$ and $\wp_{s}'(p)$ are continuous in $(p,s)$ and have derivatives in $s$, at a fixed point $p$, that are continuous in $(p,s)$; and $\wp_{s+3L}(p)=\wp_{s}(M_{0}^{3}p)$ and $\wp_{s+3L}'(p)=\wp_{s}'(M_{0}^{3}p)$, so that they are functions on $Y$ away from that corner circle. The functions $e_{k}$ are continuous with continuous derivatives, $e_{k}(s+3L)=e_{k}(s)$, and at each time $e_{2}$, $e_{3}$ and $e_{4}$ are three different numbers. (ii) $m_{D}$ is a continuous function with no zero on $Y$ away from the corner circles, and $\partial_{s}m_{D}$ is continuous there. Near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ and away from it, $m_{D}=z_{s,i}^{a_{i}-b_{i}}\,h$, where $h$ and $\partial_{s}h$ are continuous up to the corner circle and the limit of $h$ at each of its points is not $0$. (iii) Multiplication by $m_{D}$ in the frames $1$ is an isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ onto $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$ that covers the identity of $Y$; it carries the sections differentiable along the fibre onto the sections differentiable along the fibre, $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$, and the lift with two states on the pillowcase to the lift with two states on the pillowcase. (iv) An isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ onto $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$ that covers the identity of $Y$ and carries sections differentiable along the fibre to such sections and $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$ is multiplication by $c\,m_{D}$, for a continuous function $c$ with no zero that depends on the time alone. (v) Let $f\cdot1$ be a section of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ away from the corner circles, $f$ having continuous first derivatives. Then $\partial_{s}(m_{D}f)=m_{D}\bigl(\partial_{s}f+(\partial_{s}m_{D}/m_{D})\,f\bigr)$: for a continuous function $A$ on $Y$ away from the corner circles, multiplication by $m_{D}$ carries the section $(\partial_{s}f+Af)\cdot1$ to the section $\bigl(\partial_{s}(m_{D}f)+A'\,m_{D}f\bigr)\cdot1$ with $A'=A-\partial_{s}m_{D}/m_{D}$. The function $A$ is one of the functions of Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}(v) for $D$ exactly when $A'$ is one of them for $3P_{4}$. (vi) Let $D$ be one of the four divisors other than $3P_{4}$. There is no function $c$ of the time alone, with no zero and with a continuous derivative, for which multiplication by $c\,m_{D}$ carries $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$ to $\bigl(\partial_{s}(c\,m_{D}f)\bigr)\cdot1$ for every section $f\cdot1$ as in (v); in particular multiplication by $m_{D}$ does not. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```

no proof of M1 changes:** M1 takes $E$ with one complex structure and says that the counts do not depend on which (l.829); the proposition takes the Weierstrass function at every time and says how the multipliers behave in the time; part (vi) is said of functions and of a derivative, not of counts. "Continuous up to", "corner circle" and "differentiable along the fibre" are used as the glossary has them; in (ii) "the limit of $h$" is the limit that "continuous up to" gives. The letter $c$ is a function of the time alone in (iv) and (vi); $h$ is a function near a corner circle in (ii); $f$ is the function of a section in the frame $1$ in (v) and (vi). In (v) the hypothesis "$f$ having continuous first derivatives" is that of M2-W128(i) and (iii); the product rule uses only the derivative in $s$. **The weakest step** is M2-W141 (the convergence uniform in the time is a hand argument, and the derivative in the time rests on a corollary that the source states without proof); then the square root of M2-W142. The card leans harder than S14 and S14b did on M2-W114 (multiplication by a function with poles is an isomorphism of lines) and on M2-W119 with M2-W125 (the twist carried round time is a line on $Y$), each now used for all times at once; on M2-W136 no harder
