### M2-W141 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i). A point $p$ of $E$ is the class of a vector $u$ of $\R^{2}$, and $z_{s}$ is linear, so that the lattice of the periods at the time $s$ is the set of the numbers $z_{s}(n)$, $n\in\Z^{2}$, and
\[
\wp_{s}(p)=z_{s}(u)^{-2}+\sum_{n\neq0}\bigl\{z_{s}(u-n)^{-2}-z_{s}(n)^{-2}\bigr\},\qquad
\wp_{s}'(p)=-2\sum_{n}z_{s}(u-n)^{-3},
\]
the sums being over the points $n$ of $\Z^{2}$ \cite[\S\S20.2, 20.21]{WhittakerWatson}; the first series converges absolutely (the same place), and the second does by the bound below, so that the sums do not depend on the order of the terms, and $\wp_{s}$ depends on the lattice and not on the two periods chosen for it. Let $S>0$ and $R>0$, and let $\lvert s\rvert\leq S$ and $\lvert u\rvert\leq R$. For every vector $w$ with the components $(x,y)$ along $v_{1},v_{2}$, $\lvert z_{s}(w)\rvert^{2}=e^{-2\kappa s}y^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}x^{2}$ lies between $e^{-2\lvert\kappa\rvert S}\lvert w\rvert^{2}$ and $e^{2\lvert\kappa\rvert S}\lvert w\rvert^{2}$. Let $\lvert n\rvert\geq2e^{2\lvert\kappa\rvert S}R$, and write $z=z_{s}(u)$ and $\omega=z_{s}(n)$; then $\lvert z\rvert\leq\tfrac12\lvert\omega\rvert$, and
\[
\bigl\lvert(z-\omega)^{-2}-\omega^{-2}\bigr\rvert\leq\frac{10\lvert z\rvert}{\lvert\omega\rvert^{3}},\qquad
\bigl\lvert(z-\omega)^{-3}\bigr\rvert\leq\frac{8}{\lvert\omega\rvert^{3}} .
\]
At a fixed vector $w$, $\partial_{s}z_{s}(w)=-\kappa\,\overline{z_{s}(w)}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}(ii)), so that $\partial_{s}\bigl(z_{s}(w)^{-2}\bigr)=2\kappa\,\eta(z_{s}(w))$ with $\eta(\zeta)=\bar\zeta/\zeta^{3}$, and $\partial_{s}\bigl(z_{s}(w)^{-3}\bigr)=3\kappa\,\overline{z_{s}(w)}/z_{s}(w)^{4}$; $\eta$ is an even function, and
\[
\bigl\lvert\eta(\omega-z)-\eta(\omega)\bigr\rvert\leq\frac{46\lvert z\rvert}{\lvert\omega\rvert^{3}},\qquad
\Bigl\lvert\frac{\overline{z-\omega}}{(z-\omega)^{4}}\Bigr\rvert\leq\frac{8}{\lvert\omega\rvert^{3}}
\]
(the arithmetic of the constant $46$ is certified in \texttt{L9\_const}). Each term of the two series, and each derivative in $s$ of a term, is therefore at most $K/\lvert n\rvert^{3}$ in modulus, for a number $K$ that depends on $S$ and $R$ only. The points $n$ of $\Z^{2}$ whose larger coordinate in modulus is $k\geq1$ number $8k$, and each has $\lvert n\rvert\geq k$ (\texttt{L9\_shell}); and $1/k^{2}\leq1/(k-1)-1/k$ for $k\geq2$ (\texttt{L9\_tail}). The sum of $1/\lvert n\rvert^{3}$ over the points whose larger coordinate in modulus exceeds $k_{0}$ is therefore at most $8/k_{0}$. So for every $\epsilon>0$ there is a $k_{0}$ such that, for all $s$ and $u$ with $\lvert s\rvert\leq S$ and $\lvert u\rvert\leq R$ at once, the terms beyond $k_{0}$ of each of the two series, and of each of the two series of the derivatives in $s$, add up to less than $\epsilon$ in modulus. The sum of the terms up to $k_{0}$ is continuous in $(u,s)$ where no $z_{s}(u-n)$ is $0$, that is where $p$ is not $P_{1}$; two values of the whole sum then differ by at most the difference of these finite sums and $2\epsilon$, and the four sums are continuous in $(p,s)$ away from the corner circle of $P_{1}$. Each series converges, and its series of derivatives in $s$ converges uniformly; so the derivative in $s$ of $\wp_{s}(p)$ and of $\wp_{s}'(p)$, at a fixed point $p$, exists and is the sum of the series of the derivatives \cite[\S4.7, Corollary]{WhittakerWatson}, which is continuous in $(p,s)$. With the terms of $n=0$ left out the same holds near $u=0$: near the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and on it, $\wp_{s}(p)-z_{s,1}^{-2}$ and $\wp_{s}'(p)+2z_{s,1}^{-3}$ are continuous in $(p,s)$ with derivatives in $s$ that are continuous in $(p,s)$. Since $z_{s+3L}=z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}) and $M_{0}^{3}$, an integer matrix of determinant one, carries $\Z^{2}$ one-to-one onto itself, $z_{s+3L}(u-n)=z_{s}(M_{0}^{3}u-M_{0}^{3}n)$: the series at $(p,s+3L)$ are the series at $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ with their terms in another order, and $\wp_{s+3L}(p)=\wp_{s}(M_{0}^{3}p)$, $\wp_{s+3L}'(p)=\wp_{s}'(M_{0}^{3}p)$. The corners $P_{2}$, $P_{3}$, $P_{4}$ are not on the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and $M_{0}^{3}$ leaves each corner in place (M1, Corollary~7.8), so that the $e_{k}$ are continuous with continuous derivatives and $e_{k}(s+3L)=e_{k}(s)$; and at each time the only zeros of $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ are at $P_{k}$ (the second step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}), so that $e_{j}\neq e_{k}$ for $j\neq k$.
```

READ: M2-W048 as logged ("$z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$"); M2-W113 as logged ("where $2\omega_{1}$ and $2\omega_{2}$ are the values of $z$ at $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$"; "for each of these half-periods $\omega$ the only zeros of $\wp-\wp(\omega)$ are double zeros at the points congruent to $\omega$"); M2-W128(ii) as logged ("At a fixed point of the fibre, $\partial_{s}z_{s}=-\kappa\,\bar z_{s}$"); `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.952 ("which leaves every corner in place"), l.308 ("its matrix $M$ has determinant $+1$"). Hand steps, in full. The source's $\Omega_{m,n}=2m\omega_{1}+2n\omega_{2}$ is $m\,z_{s}(1,0)+n\,z_{s}(0,1)=z_{s}(m,n)$, $z_{s}$ being linear, and $z-\Omega_{m,n}=z_{s}(u)-z_{s}(n)=z_{s}(u-n)$. $v_{1},v_{2}$ are orthonormal (the mark of M2-W048), so $x^{2}+y^{2}=\lvert w\rvert^{2}$, and $e^{\pm2\kappa s}$ lies between $e^{-2\lvert\kappa\rvert S}$ and $e^{2\lvert\kappa\rvert S}$. $\lvert z\rvert\leq e^{\lvert\kappa\rvert S}R$ and $\lvert\omega\rvert\geq e^{-\lvert\kappa\rvert S}\lvert n\rvert\geq2e^{\lvert\kappa\rvert S}R$. With $\lvert z\rvert\leq\tfrac12\lvert\omega\rvert$: $\lvert z-\omega\rvert\geq\tfrac12\lvert\omega\rvert$; $(z-\omega)^{-2}-\omega^{-2}=z(2\omega-z)/\bigl((z-\omega)^{2}\omega^{2}\bigr)$, of modulus at most $\lvert z\rvert\cdot\tfrac52\lvert\omega\rvert\cdot4/\lvert\omega\rvert^{4}$; $\lvert z-\omega\rvert^{-3}\leq8/\lvert\omega\rvert^{3}$, and $\lvert\overline{\zeta}/\zeta^{4}\rvert=\lvert\zeta\rvert^{-3}$; $\eta(\omega-z)-\eta(\omega)=\bigl(-\bar z\omega^{3}+\bar\omega(3\omega^{2}z-3\omega z^{2}+z^{3})\bigr)/\bigl((\omega-z)^{3}\omega^{3}\bigr)$, whose numerator has modulus at most $\lvert z\rvert\lvert\omega\rvert^{3}(1+3+\tfrac32+\tfrac14)=\tfrac{23}{4}\lvert z\rvert\lvert\omega\rvert^{3}$ and whose denominator has modulus at least $\lvert\omega\rvert^{6}/8$; $\eta(-\zeta)=-\bar\zeta/(-\zeta^{3})=\eta(\zeta)$, so that the derivative in $s$ of the term $z_{s}(u-n)^{-2}-z_{s}(n)^{-2}$ is $2\kappa\bigl(\eta(\omega-z)-\eta(\omega)\bigr)$. The chain rule: $\partial_{s}(\zeta^{-2})=-2\zeta^{-3}\cdot(-\kappa\bar\zeta)$ and $\partial_{s}(\zeta^{-3})=-3\zeta^{-4}\cdot(-\kappa\bar\zeta)$ for $\zeta=z_{s}(w)\neq0$. $1/\lvert\omega\rvert^{3}\leq e^{3\lvert\kappa\rvert S}/\lvert n\rvert^{3}$, so $K$ may be taken as $46\max(1,2\lvert\kappa\rvert,3\lvert\kappa\rvert)\,e^{4\lvert\kappa\rvert S}\max(1,R)$. The points with larger coordinate $k$ in modulus are those of the square of side $2k+1$ not in the square of side $2k-1$: $(2k+1)^{2}-(2k-1)^{2}=8k$; such a point has $\lvert n\rvert\geq k$; the sum over them of $1/\lvert n\rvert^{3}$ is at most $8k/k^{3}=8/k^{2}$, and $\sum_{k>k_{0}}8/k^{2}\leq8\sum_{k>k_{0}}\bigl(1/(k-1)-1/k\bigr)=8/k_{0}$. The finitely many terms with $\lvert n\rvert<2e^{2\lvert\kappa\rvert S}R$ are each continuous with a continuous derivative in $s$ where $z_{s}(u-n)\neq0$, and $z_{s}(u-n)=0$ only for $u=n$, $z_{s}$ being one-to-one. Every point of $E$ has a lift with $\lvert u\rvert\leq R$ for $R=1$, and every time lies in some $\lvert s\rvert\leq S$. The continuity of the sum is the argument of the source's §3.32 (its prose read in the machine-read text; it is stated there for functions of one complex variable and is not cited: the three lines are given here). **Identification steps (CLAUDE.md §5), the architect's:** the Corollary of §4.7 is stated for $\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}z$ with $h\to0$ "along a definite simple curve"; it is used here with the curve the line of the time $s$ at a fixed vector $u$, for terms that have continuous derivatives in $s$; the source gives no proof and refers to its §4.44, not read. That an absolutely convergent series has the same sum in every order is used and is not cited. The symbol $\wp_{s}'$ is the source's $\wp'=\mathrm{d}\wp/\mathrm{d}z$ for the lattice at the time $s$. In this entry $n$ is a point of $\Z^{2}$, $k$ and $k_{0}$ are whole numbers in the count and $k$ is $2,3,4$ in $e_{k}$; $S$ and $R$ are bounds, $S$ not being the quarter-turn of M1. **This is the weakest step of the card:** no certificate checks the convergence, and the script checks the bounds and the sums at sample points only. It brings in no reading beyond the identification steps named here and in the mark of M2-W140
- **Tag:** none (a step of a proof)
- **Failure test:** a pair $z,\omega$ with $\lvert z\rvert\leq\tfrac12\lvert\omega\rvert$ that breaks one of the four bounds; a point and a time, away from the corner circle of $P_{1}$, at which the derivative in $s$ of $\wp_{s}(p)$ is not the sum of the series of the derivatives; a point at which $\wp_{s+3L}(p)\neq\wp_{s}(M_{0}^{3}p)$; or an error in the cited statements.

### M2-W142 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(ii). Write $G=\wp_{s}(p)-z_{s,1}^{-2}$ near the corner circle of $P_{1}$; by the step above $G$ and $\partial_{s}G$ are continuous near that circle and on it. At each time $s$ and for $k=2,3,4$, the function $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ on $E$ has the divisor $2P_{k}-2P_{1}$ (the second step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}). Its translate $p\mapsto\wp_{s}(p+P_{k})-e_{k}$ is again an elliptic function, with the divisor moved as adding $P_{k}$ moves the corners: $2P_{1}-2P_{k}$ (\texttt{L9\_translate}). The product of the two has the divisor zero (\texttt{L9\_product\_zero}): it is an elliptic function with no pole and no zero, and so a constant $c_{k}(s)\neq0$ \cite[\S20.12]{WhittakerWatson}. In the same way $(\wp_{s}')^{2}$ divided by $(\wp_{s}-e_{2})(\wp_{s}-e_{3})(\wp_{s}-e_{4})$ has the divisor zero (\texttt{L9\_square\_zero}) and is a constant $c_{0}(s)\neq0$. The numbers $c_{k}(s)$ and $c_{0}(s)$ are the values of these functions at the point $(\tfrac14,\tfrac14)$, which with its translates by the corners is not a corner; by (i) they are continuous in $s$ with continuous derivatives. Near the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and away from it, $\wp_{s}-e_{k}=z_{s,1}^{-2}\bigl(1+z_{s,1}^{2}(G-e_{k})\bigr)$. Near the corner circle of $P_{k}$ and away from it, $z_{s,1}(p-P_{k})=z_{s,k}(p)$, and by the constant product
\[
\wp_{s}(p)-e_{k}\;=\;\frac{c_{k}(s)}{\wp_{s}(p-P_{k})-e_{k}}\;=\;\frac{c_{k}(s)\,z_{s,k}^{2}}{1+z_{s,k}^{2}\bigl(G(p-P_{k},s)-e_{k}\bigr)} .
\]
Near the corner circle of $P_{j}$, $j\neq1,k$, and on it, $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ is continuous with a continuous derivative in $s$, and on the circle it is $e_{j}-e_{k}\neq0$, by (i). The function $z_{s,i}^{2}$ is continuous with the continuous derivative $-2\kappa\lvert z_{s,i}\rvert^{2}$ in $s$ and is $0$ on the corner circle of $P_{i}$. Near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ and away from it, therefore, $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ is $z_{s,i}^{r}$ times a function that, with its derivative in $s$, is continuous up to the corner circle and has a limit that is not $0$ at each of its points, $r$ being the integer of $2P_{k}-2P_{1}$ at $P_{i}$. The first four of the functions $m_{D}$ are $1$ or products and quotients of the $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$, and the integers add up to $a_{i}-b_{i}$ (the third step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}): this is the second sentence of (ii) for them. Away from the corner circles the $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ are continuous with continuous derivatives in $s$ by (i) and have no zero, so that the first sentence of (ii) holds for them. For the fifth, $m_{D}=\wp_{s}'/(\wp_{s}-e_{4})^{2}$ with $D=P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$, and by the constant $c_{0}$, $m_{D}^{2}=c_{0}(s)\,(\wp_{s}-e_{2})(\wp_{s}-e_{3})/(\wp_{s}-e_{4})^{3}$, whose integers at the corners are twice the $a_{i}-b_{i}$ (\texttt{L9\_fifth\_square}). Away from the corner circles $m_{D}$ is continuous with a continuous derivative in $s$ by (i), and it has no zero, its square having none. Near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ and away from it let $h=m_{D}\,z_{s,i}^{b_{i}-a_{i}}$; then $h^{2}$ is the restriction of a function $q$ that, with $\partial_{s}q$, is continuous near the corner circle and on it and has no zero. On a set $\lvert z_{s,i}\rvert<\delta$, $\lvert s-s_{0}\rvert<\delta$, which in the coordinates $(z_{s,i},s)$ is convex, $q$ has a continuous logarithm, as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase} \cite[Proposition~1.33]{Hatcher}, and so a continuous square root $\varrho$ with no zero. With the corner circle taken away the set is connected, and on it $h/\varrho$ is continuous with square $1$: it is constantly $1$ or constantly $-1$. So $h$ is continuous up to the corner circle with a limit that is not $0$, and $\partial_{s}h=\partial_{s}q/(2h)$ is continuous up to it.
```

Verified reference V-M2-13 (§20.12 (IV): "An elliptic function, f(z), with no poles in a cell is merely a constant."), READ at source by chat C8b, the row read by this chat; V-M2-7 (Hatcher, Proposition 1.33), through M2-W035 as logged ("by the lifting criterion … there is a continuous $\xi\colon\R^{2}\to\R$ with $\gamma/\lvert\gamma\rvert=e^{2\pi i\xi}$. READ: M2-W113 as logged ("the divisors on $E$ are therefore: of $\wp-e_{k}$, $2P_{k}-2P_{1}$; of $\wp'$, $P_{2}+P_{3}+P_{4}-3P_{1}$"); M2-W114 as logged ("the divisor of $m$ is $D-3P_{4}$"). Hand steps, in full. **An identification step, the architect's:** for an elliptic function $\varphi$ and a half-period $\omega$, $z\mapsto\varphi(z+\omega)$ has the same periods and is analytic except at poles, and its order at a point $z_{0}$ is that of $\varphi$ at $z_{0}+\omega$; read on $E$, its divisor is that of $\varphi$ moved by $p\mapsto p-P_{k}$, and $-P_{k}$ is $P_{k}$ in $E$. A product of elliptic functions with the same periods is elliptic and its divisor is the sum; a divisor zero means that every pole of the one factor is met by a zero of the other of the same order, so that the product, with its values at those points filled in, has no pole and no zero; the same for the quotient of $(\wp_{s}')^{2}$, whose divisor is $2(P_{2}+P_{3}+P_{4}-3P_{1})$. The point $(\tfrac14,\tfrac14)$ and its sums with $(\tfrac12,0)$, $(0,\tfrac12)$, $(\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$ have a coordinate $\tfrac14$ or $\tfrac34$ and are not corners; at them $\wp_{s}$ and $\wp_{s}'$ are continuous with continuous derivatives in $s$ (M2-W141) and $\wp_{s}-e_{k}\neq0$, the zeros lying at corners. $\wp_{s}=z_{s,1}^{-2}+G$ gives $\wp_{s}-e_{k}=z_{s,1}^{-2}(1+z_{s,1}^{2}(G-e_{k}))$. For $p$ near $P_{k}$, $p-P_{k}$ is near $P_{1}$ and $z_{s,1}(p-P_{k})=z_{s}(p-P_{k})=z_{s,k}(p)$ (M2-W117); the constant product at the point $p-P_{k}$ is $(\wp_{s}(p)-e_{k})(\wp_{s}(p-P_{k})-e_{k})=c_{k}(s)$. $\partial_{s}(z_{s,i}^{2})=2z_{s,i}\cdot(-\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i})$. The bracket $1+z_{s,i}^{2}(G-e_{k})$ is continuous with a continuous derivative in $s$ near the circle and on it, and is $1$ on it, so it has no zero near it; a quotient of such functions with limits not $0$ is such a function. The integers $r$: $-2$ at $P_{1}$, $2$ at $P_{k}$, $0$ at the other two. The coordinates $(z_{s,i},s)$: the map $(p,s)\mapsto(z_{s,i}(p),s)$ is continuous and one-to-one near the corner circle with a continuous inverse, $z_{s}$ being linear and one-to-one at each time with coefficients continuous in $s$; a product of a disc and an interval is convex, and with the points $z_{s,i}=0$ taken away it is the product of a punctured disc and an interval, which is path-connected. $\varrho=e^{\frac12\log q}$; $(h/\varrho)^{2}=1$ and a continuous function with the values $\pm1$ on a connected set is constant. $\partial_{s}(h^{2})=2h\,\partial_{s}h$ away from the circle, where $h=m_{D}z_{s,i}^{b_{i}-a_{i}}$ has a derivative in $s$. The function $q$ is the product of $c_{0}(s)$ and of the functions found above for $\wp_{s}-e_{2}$, $\wp_{s}-e_{3}$ and the inverse cube of that for $\wp_{s}-e_{4}$. In this entry $c_{k}$ and $c_{0}$ are numbers that depend on the time (they are not the function $c$ of M2-W140(iv)), $r$ is an integer, $\delta$ a small positive number, $\varrho$ a square root, and $G$ a function near the corner circle of $P_{1}$. **Weak:** the square root, and that the product of two functions with opposite divisors has its singularities removed, are hand steps that no certificate checks. It brings in no reading beyond the identification steps named here and in the mark of M2-W140

### M2-W143 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii) and (iv). At each time $s$, $m_{D}$ is the function named for $D$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iv) for $(E,J_{s})$ with the coordinate $z_{s}$, and by the third step of the proof of that proposition multiplication by it is an isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ onto $\mathcal{O}(3P_{4})$ of $(E,J_{s})$ that covers the identity of $E$ and carries differentiable sections onto differentiable sections, $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$ and the lift with two states to the lift with two states. In the frames $1$ the map is multiplication by $m_{D}$, and from the frame $z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ to the frame $z_{s,i}^{-b_{i}}$ it is multiplication by the function $h$ of (ii), taken on the corner circle with its limit; by (ii) both are continuous in $(p,s)$ with no zero. By (i), and since $M_{0}^{3}$ leaves each corner in place, $m_{D}(p,s+3L)=m_{D}(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$, so that the map respects the join of Definition~\ref{def:twisttime}. It is therefore an isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ onto $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$ that covers the identity of $Y$, and at each time it carries the lift with two states to the lift with two states. At each time $m_{D}$ and $h$ are holomorphic, and their derivatives in $z_{s}$ are continuous in $(p,s)$: that of $\wp_{s}$ is $\wp_{s}'$; that of $\wp_{s}'$ is, by the constant $c_{0}$ of the step above, $\tfrac12c_{0}(s)$ times the sum of the three products of two of the $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$; that of $G$ is $\wp_{s}'+2z_{s,1}^{-3}$, continuous near the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and on it by the first step; and that of the $h$ of the fifth divisor is that of $q$ divided by $2h$. For $f$ continuous with derivatives along the fibre that are continuous in $(p,s)$, so therefore is $m_{D}f$, and in the same way so is $f/m_{D}$: the map carries the sections differentiable along the fibre onto the sections differentiable along the fibre, and, since $\partial(m_{D}f)/\partial\bar z_{s}=m_{D}\,\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s}$, it carries $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$. This is (iii). For (iv): let $\beta$ be an isomorphism as in (iv). For $w\neq0$ in the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ over $y$, $\beta(w)=c(y)\,m_{D}w$ for a number $c(y)\neq0$ that does not depend on $w$, and $c\colon Y\to\C\setminus\{0\}$ is continuous. The frames $e$ of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ are differentiable along the fibre with $\bar\partial e=0$; so are their images $m_{D}e$ under the map of (iii), and their images $c\,m_{D}e$ under $\beta$. In the frames of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$ the function of $m_{D}e$ is $m_{D}$ or $h$, at each time holomorphic with no zero, and that of $c\,m_{D}e$ is $c$ times it; so at each time $c$ is continuously differentiable on $E$ with $\partial c/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$ at every point, and as in the last step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre} it is an elliptic function with no pole, and so a constant \cite[\S20.12]{WhittakerWatson}. So $c$ depends on the time alone.
```

### M2-W144 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(v) and (vi). By (ii), away from the corner circles $m_{D}$ has no zero and a derivative in $s$ that is continuous, and by the rule for the derivative of a product $\partial_{s}(m_{D}f)=m_{D}\,\partial_{s}f+(\partial_{s}m_{D})f=m_{D}\bigl(\partial_{s}f+(\partial_{s}m_{D}/m_{D})\,f\bigr)$; so $m_{D}(\partial_{s}f+Af)=\partial_{s}(m_{D}f)+(A-\partial_{s}m_{D}/m_{D})\,m_{D}f$. The function $\partial_{s}m_{D}$ is a function on $Y$ away from the corner circles, as in the first step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}, so that $A'=A-\partial_{s}m_{D}/m_{D}$ is a continuous function on $Y$ away from the corner circles. Near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ and away from it, $m_{D}=z_{s,i}^{a_{i}-b_{i}}h$ and $\partial_{s}z_{s,i}=-\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}$ (the second step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}), so that
\[
\frac{\partial_{s}m_{D}}{m_{D}}=-(a_{i}-b_{i})\,\kappa\,\frac{\bar z_{s,i}}{z_{s,i}}+\frac{\partial_{s}h}{h},\qquad
A'+b_{i}\kappa\,\frac{\bar z_{s,i}}{z_{s,i}}=\Bigl(A+a_{i}\kappa\,\frac{\bar z_{s,i}}{z_{s,i}}\Bigr)-\frac{\partial_{s}h}{h} .
\]
By (ii) $\partial_{s}h/h$ is continuous up to the corner circle; so $A'+b_{i}\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is continuous up to the corner circle of $P_{i}$ exactly when $A+a_{i}\kappa\,\bar z_{s,i}/z_{s,i}$ is, which by Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}(v) is the last sentence of (v). For (vi): let $c$ be a function as in (vi). The frame $1$ is a section as in (v), with $f=1$ and $\partial_{s}f=0$; so $\partial_{s}(c\,m_{D})=0$ away from the corner circles, and for each point $p$ of $E$ that is not a corner the number $c(s)\,m_{D}(p,s)$ does not depend on $s$. Call it $\Theta(p)$, and let $s\neq s'$. At each time $m_{D}$ is holomorphic on $E$ away from the corners with the divisor $D-3P_{4}$, which is not zero (the third step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}); so $\Theta$ is holomorphic for $J_{s}$ and is not constant, and there is a point $p_{0}$, not a corner, at which its derivative in $z_{s}$ is a number $\alpha\neq0$: there $\mathrm{d}\Theta=\alpha\,\mathrm{d}z_{s}$. $\Theta$ is holomorphic for $J_{s'}$ as well, and $\mathrm{d}\Theta=\alpha'\,\mathrm{d}z_{s'}$ at $p_{0}$; $\alpha\,\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ is one-to-one on the tangent plane, so $\alpha'\neq0$ and $\mathrm{d}z_{s'}=(\alpha/\alpha')\,\mathrm{d}z_{s}$. Both coordinates are linear, so $z_{s'}=(\alpha/\alpha')\,z_{s}$, and comparing the coefficients of $y$ and of $x$ as in the first step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir} gives $s'=s$. There is therefore no such $c$; the constant $1$ is a function of the time alone with no zero and with a continuous derivative, so that multiplication by $m_{D}$ itself does not carry $(\partial_{s}f)\cdot1$ to $\bigl(\partial_{s}(m_{D}f)\bigr)\cdot1$ for every such section.
```

### M2-W147 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i). A point $p$ of $E$ is the class of a vector $u$ of $\R^{2}$, and $z_{s}$ is linear, so that the lattice of the periods at the time $s$ is the set of the numbers $z_{s}(n)$, $n\in\Z^{2}$, and
\[
\wp_{s}(p)=z_{s}(u)^{-2}+\sum_{n\neq0}\bigl\{z_{s}(u-n)^{-2}-z_{s}(n)^{-2}\bigr\},\qquad
\wp_{s}'(p)=-2\sum_{n}z_{s}(u-n)^{-3},
\]
the sums being over the points $n$ of $\Z^{2}$ \cite[\S\S20.2, 20.21]{WhittakerWatson}; the first series converges absolutely (the same place), and the second does by the bound below, so that the sums do not depend on the order of the terms \cite[\S2.41]{WhittakerWatson}, and $\wp_{s}$ depends on the lattice and not on the two periods chosen for it. Let $S>0$ and $R>0$, and let $\lvert s\rvert\leq S$ and $\lvert u\rvert\leq R$. For every vector $w$ with the components $(x,y)$ along $v_{1},v_{2}$, $\lvert z_{s}(w)\rvert^{2}=e^{-2\kappa s}y^{2}+e^{2\kappa s}x^{2}$ lies between $e^{-2\lvert\kappa\rvert S}\lvert w\rvert^{2}$ and $e^{2\lvert\kappa\rvert S}\lvert w\rvert^{2}$. Let $\lvert n\rvert\geq2e^{2\lvert\kappa\rvert S}R$, and write $\xi=z_{s}(u)$ and $\Omega=z_{s}(n)$; then $\lvert \xi\rvert\leq\tfrac12\lvert\Omega\rvert$, and
\[
\bigl\lvert(\xi-\Omega)^{-2}-\Omega^{-2}\bigr\rvert\leq\frac{10\lvert \xi\rvert}{\lvert\Omega\rvert^{3}},\qquad
\bigl\lvert(\xi-\Omega)^{-3}\bigr\rvert\leq\frac{8}{\lvert\Omega\rvert^{3}} .
\]
At a fixed vector $w$, $\partial_{s}z_{s}(w)=-\kappa\,\overline{z_{s}(w)}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}(ii)), so that $\partial_{s}\bigl(z_{s}(w)^{-2}\bigr)=2\kappa\,\eta(z_{s}(w))$ with $\eta(\zeta)=\bar\zeta/\zeta^{3}$, and $\partial_{s}\bigl(z_{s}(w)^{-3}\bigr)=3\kappa\,\overline{z_{s}(w)}/z_{s}(w)^{4}$; $\eta$ is an even function, and
\[
\bigl\lvert\eta(\Omega-\xi)-\eta(\Omega)\bigr\rvert\leq\frac{46\lvert \xi\rvert}{\lvert\Omega\rvert^{3}},\qquad
\Bigl\lvert\frac{\overline{\xi-\Omega}}{(\xi-\Omega)^{4}}\Bigr\rvert\leq\frac{8}{\lvert\Omega\rvert^{3}}
\]
(the arithmetic of the constant $46$ is certified in \texttt{L9\_const}). Each term of the two series, and each derivative in $s$ of a term, is therefore at most $K/\lvert n\rvert^{3}$ in modulus, for a number $K$ that depends on $S$ and $R$ only. For a whole number $\ell\geq1$ the points of $\Z^{2}$ whose two coordinates are at most $\ell$ in modulus number $(2\ell+1)^{2}$, which is $8\ell$ more than $(2\ell-1)^{2}$ (the identity of whole numbers is certified in \texttt{L9\_shell}); so the points whose larger coordinate in modulus is $\ell$ number $8\ell$, and each of them has $\lvert n\rvert\geq\ell$. For $\ell\geq2$, $(\ell-1)\ell\leq\ell^{2}$ (\texttt{L9\_tail}), so that $1/\ell^{2}\leq1/\bigl((\ell-1)\ell\bigr)=1/(\ell-1)-1/\ell$. The sum of $1/\lvert n\rvert^{3}$ over the points whose larger coordinate in modulus exceeds $\ell_{0}$ is therefore at most $8/\ell_{0}$. So for every $\epsilon>0$ there is an $\ell_{0}$ such that, for all $s$ and $u$ with $\lvert s\rvert\leq S$ and $\lvert u\rvert\leq R$ at once, the terms beyond $\ell_{0}$ of each of the two series, and of each of the two series of the derivatives in $s$, add up to less than $\epsilon$ in modulus. The sum of the terms up to $\ell_{0}$ is continuous in $(u,s)$ where no $z_{s}(u-n)$ is $0$, that is where $p$ is not $P_{1}$; two values of the whole sum then differ by at most the difference of these finite sums and $2\epsilon$, and the four sums are continuous in $(p,s)$ away from the corner circle of $P_{1}$. Each series converges, and its series of derivatives in $s$ converges uniformly; so the derivative in $s$ of $\wp_{s}(p)$ and of $\wp_{s}'(p)$, at a fixed point $p$, exists and is the sum of the series of the derivatives \cite[\S4.7, Corollary]{WhittakerWatson}, which is continuous in $(p,s)$. With the terms of $n=0$ left out the same holds near $u=0$: near the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and on it, $\wp_{s}(p)-z_{s,1}^{-2}$ and $\wp_{s}'(p)+2z_{s,1}^{-3}$ are continuous in $(p,s)$ with derivatives in $s$ that are continuous in $(p,s)$. Since $z_{s+3L}=z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}) and $M_{0}^{3}$, an integer matrix of determinant one, carries $\Z^{2}$ one-to-one onto itself, $z_{s+3L}(u-n)=z_{s}(M_{0}^{3}u-M_{0}^{3}n)$: the series at $(p,s+3L)$ are the series at $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ with their terms in another order, and $\wp_{s+3L}(p)=\wp_{s}(M_{0}^{3}p)$, $\wp_{s+3L}'(p)=\wp_{s}'(M_{0}^{3}p)$; the points $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ being one point of $Y$ (Definition~\ref{def:time}), they are functions on $Y$ away from the corner circle of $P_{1}$. The corners $P_{2}$, $P_{3}$, $P_{4}$ are not on the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and $M_{0}^{3}$ leaves each corner in place (M1, Corollary~7.8), so that the $e_{k}$ are continuous with continuous derivatives and $e_{k}(s+3L)=e_{k}(s)$; and at each time the only zeros of $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ are at $P_{k}$ (the second step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}), so that $e_{j}\neq e_{k}$ for $j\neq k$.
```
- **Place:** `supply 290`; the first step of the proof of Proposition `prop:fivetime` (M2-W140), in the place of M2-W141, which it replaces
- **Step:** S14b2b (a correction after the fresh reader's check, P251)
- **Rests on:** M2-W048 ($z_{s}$; $v_{1},v_{2}$ orthonormal; $z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$), M2-W113 (the periods at the values of $z$ at $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$; the zeros of $\wp-e_{k}$), M2-W128(ii) with M2-W135, M2-W132 (the bound on $\lvert z_{s}(w)\rvert$, there for $0\leq s\leq3L$); M1 v7.3, Section 4.1 (l.308: the matrix of a step has determinant $+1$), Corollary 7.8 (l.952); Verified References V-M2-14 and V-M2-13; M2-W039 (`def:time`: the points of $Y$); Verified Reference V-M2-14 also for §2.41
- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full; certificate `L9_shell` (the identity $(2(k+1)+1)^{2}=(2(k+1)-1)^{2}+8(k+1)$ of whole numbers, and nothing of points), `L9_tail` ($(k+1)k\leq(k+1)^{2}$), `L9_const` (integers only); script `papers/M2/checks/S14b2b_five.py`, checks (1), (2), (3), (5), (11) and (12), at sample points and times. The mark of M2-W141, which is kept with that entry, holds for this one with $\xi$, $\Omega$, $\ell$ and $\ell_{0}$ in the place of its $z$, $\omega$, $k$ and $k_{0}$ of the count: its readings, its hand steps in full, its identification steps and what it says is the weakest step of the card. Verified reference V-M2-14, to which §2.41 is added (p.25: "the sum of an absolutely convergent series is not affected by changing the order in which the terms occur", the prose read in the machine-read text; the page image not looked at; the source states it for a series indexed by the positive integers, and it is used here for a series over the points of $\Z^{2}$ counted in some order, **an identification step, the architect's**). READ: M2-W039 as logged ("in which $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ are one point"). Hand step added: that the count of the points with larger coordinate $\ell$ in modulus is the difference of the two squares, and that such a point has $\lvert n\rvert\geq\ell$, are hand steps and are not certified. In this entry $n$ is a point of $\Z^{2}$, $k$ is $2,3,4$ in $e_{k}$, and $S$ and $R$ are bounds, $S$ not being the quarter-turn of M1

### M2-W148 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(ii). Write $G=\wp_{s}(p)-z_{s,1}^{-2}$ near the corner circle of $P_{1}$; by the step above $G$ and $\partial_{s}G$ are continuous near that circle and on it. At each time $s$ and for $k=2,3,4$, the function $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ on $E$ has the divisor $2P_{k}-2P_{1}$ (the second step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}). Its translate $p\mapsto\wp_{s}(p+P_{k})-e_{k}$ is again an elliptic function, with the divisor moved as adding $P_{k}$ moves the corners; adding $P_{k}$ exchanges $P_{1}$ with $P_{k}$ and the other two corners with one another (\texttt{L9\_translate}), so that the divisor is $2P_{1}-2P_{k}$. The product of the two has the divisor zero (\texttt{L9\_product\_zero}): it is an elliptic function with no pole and no zero, and so a constant $c_{k}(s)\neq0$ \cite[\S20.12]{WhittakerWatson}. In the same way $(\wp_{s}')^{2}$ divided by $(\wp_{s}-e_{2})(\wp_{s}-e_{3})(\wp_{s}-e_{4})$ has the divisor zero (\texttt{L9\_square\_zero}) and is a constant $c_{0}(s)\neq0$. The numbers $c_{k}(s)$ and $c_{0}(s)$ are the values of these functions at the point $(\tfrac14,\tfrac14)$, which with its translates by the corners is not a corner; by (i) they are continuous in $s$ with continuous derivatives. Near the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and away from it, $\wp_{s}-e_{k}=z_{s,1}^{-2}\bigl(1+z_{s,1}^{2}(G-e_{k})\bigr)$. Near the corner circle of $P_{k}$ and away from it, $z_{s,1}(p-P_{k})=z_{s,k}(p)$, and by the constant product
\[
\wp_{s}(p)-e_{k}\;=\;\frac{c_{k}(s)}{\wp_{s}(p-P_{k})-e_{k}}\;=\;\frac{c_{k}(s)\,z_{s,k}^{2}}{1+z_{s,k}^{2}\bigl(G(p-P_{k},s)-e_{k}\bigr)} .
\]
Near the corner circle of $P_{j}$, $j\neq1,k$, and on it, $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ is continuous with a continuous derivative in $s$, and on the circle it is $e_{j}-e_{k}\neq0$, by (i). The function $z_{s,i}^{2}$ is continuous with the continuous derivative $-2\kappa\lvert z_{s,i}\rvert^{2}$ in $s$ and is $0$ on the corner circle of $P_{i}$. Near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ and away from it, therefore, $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ is $z_{s,i}^{r}$ times a function that, with its derivative in $s$, is continuous up to the corner circle and has a limit that is not $0$ at each of its points, $r$ being the integer of $2P_{k}-2P_{1}$ at $P_{i}$. The first four of the functions $m_{D}$ are $1$ or products and quotients of the $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$, and the integers add up to $a_{i}-b_{i}$ (the third step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}): this is the second sentence of (ii) for them. Away from the corner circles the $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$ are continuous with continuous derivatives in $s$ by (i) and have no zero, so that the first sentence of (ii) holds for them. For the fifth, $m_{D}=\wp_{s}'/(\wp_{s}-e_{4})^{2}$ with $D=P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$, and by the constant $c_{0}$, $m_{D}^{2}=c_{0}(s)\,(\wp_{s}-e_{2})(\wp_{s}-e_{3})/(\wp_{s}-e_{4})^{3}$, whose divisor is twice $D-3P_{4}$ (\texttt{L9\_fifth\_square}), with the integer $2(a_{i}-b_{i})$ at $P_{i}$. Away from the corner circles $m_{D}$ is continuous with a continuous derivative in $s$ by (i), and it has no zero, its square having none. Near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ and away from it let $h=m_{D}\,z_{s,i}^{b_{i}-a_{i}}$; then $h^{2}$ is the restriction of a function $q$ that, with $\partial_{s}q$, is continuous near the corner circle and on it and has no zero. On a set $\lvert z_{s,i}\rvert<\delta$, $\lvert s-s_{0}\rvert<\delta$, which in the coordinates $(z_{s,i},s)$ is convex, $q$ has a continuous logarithm, as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase} \cite[Proposition~1.33]{Hatcher}, and so a continuous square root $\varrho$ with no zero. With the corner circle taken away the set is connected, and on it $h/\varrho$ is continuous with square $1$: it is constantly $1$ or constantly $-1$. So $h$ is continuous up to the corner circle with a limit that is not $0$, and $\partial_{s}h=\partial_{s}q/(2h)$ is continuous up to it. By (i), and since $M_{0}^{3}$ leaves each corner in place (M1, Corollary~7.8), $m_{D}(p,s+3L)=m_{D}(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ for each of the five: $m_{D}$ is a function on $Y$ away from the corner circles (Definition~\ref{def:time}).
```

(iii) and (iv). At each time $s$, $m_{D}$ is the function named for $D$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iv) for $(E,J_{s})$ with the coordinate $z_{s}$, and by the third step of the proof of that proposition multiplication by it is an isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ onto $\mathcal{O}(3P_{4})$ of $(E,J_{s})$ that covers the identity of $E$ and carries differentiable sections onto differentiable sections, $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$ and the lift with two states on the pillowcase to the lift with two states on the pillowcase. In the frames $1$ the map is multiplication by $m_{D}$, and from the frame $z_{s,i}^{-a_{i}}$ to the frame $z_{s,i}^{-b_{i}}$ it is multiplication by the function $h$ of (ii), taken on the corner circle with its limit; by (ii) both are continuous in $(p,s)$ with no zero. By (i), and since $M_{0}^{3}$ leaves each corner in place, $m_{D}(p,s+3L)=m_{D}(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$, so that the map respects the join of Definition~\ref{def:twisttime}. It is therefore an isomorphism of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ onto $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$ that covers the identity of $Y$, and at each time it carries the lift with two states on the pillowcase to the lift with two states on the pillowcase. At each time $m_{D}$ and $h$ are holomorphic, and their derivatives in $z_{s}$ are continuous in $(p,s)$: that of $\wp_{s}$ is $\wp_{s}'$; that of $\wp_{s}'$ is, by the constant $c_{0}$ of the step above, $\tfrac12c_{0}(s)$ times the sum of the three products of two of the $\wp_{s}-e_{k}$; that of $G$ is $\wp_{s}'+2z_{s,1}^{-3}$, continuous near the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and on it by the first step; and that of the $h$ of the fifth divisor is that of $q$ divided by $2h$. For $f$ continuous with derivatives along the fibre that are continuous in $(p,s)$, so therefore is $m_{D}f$, and in the same way so is $f/m_{D}$: the map carries the sections differentiable along the fibre onto the sections differentiable along the fibre, and, since $\partial(m_{D}f)/\partial\bar z_{s}=m_{D}\,\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s}$, it carries $\bar\partial$ to $\bar\partial$. This is (iii). For (iv): let $\beta$ be an isomorphism as in (iv). For $w\neq0$ in the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ over $y$, $\beta(w)=c(y)\,m_{D}w$ for a number $c(y)\neq0$ that does not depend on $w$, and $c\colon Y\to\C\setminus\{0\}$ is continuous. The frames $\varepsilon$ of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ are differentiable along the fibre with $\bar\partial\varepsilon=0$; so are their images $m_{D}\varepsilon$ under the map of (iii), and their images $c\,m_{D}\varepsilon$ under $\beta$. In the frames of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$ the function of $m_{D}\varepsilon$ is $m_{D}$ or $h$, at each time holomorphic with no zero, and that of $c\,m_{D}\varepsilon$ is $c$ times it; so at each time $c$ is continuously differentiable on $E$ with $\partial c/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$ at every point, and as in the last step of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre} it is an elliptic function with no pole, and so a constant \cite[\S20.12]{WhittakerWatson}. So $c$ depends on the time alone.

### M2-W152 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the twist over the unfolded geometry; the operator $\bar\partial_{B}$ of the base]\label{def:dbarbase}
Let $\widetilde{M}$ be the unfolded geometry (Definition~\ref{def:unfolded}) with the almost complex structure $\widetilde{J}$ of Definition~\ref{def:step}, write $B=\CPtwo\times\Stwo$ for the base (M1, Theorem~7.10), so that a point of $\widetilde{M}$ is $(y,u,b)$ with $y\in Y$, $u\in S^{3}$ and $b\in B$, and let $D$ be a divisor on the corners. The \emph{twist over the unfolded geometry}, for $D$, is the bundle $\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{M}}(D)$ on $\widetilde{M}$ whose fibre over $(y,u,b)$ is the fibre of the twist carried round time $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ (Definition~\ref{def:twisttime}) over $y$: it is M1's twist of the fibre, the same over every point of space and of the base. Let $S_{D}=\Lambda^{0,*}_{\widetilde{J}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{M}}(D)$. The forms of type $(0,*)$ of $\widetilde{J}$ are the tensor product of those along the fibre, those of time and of space, and those of the base (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}, its step for a direct sum taken once more for the directions of time and of space and those of the base), so that the fibre of $S_{D}$ over $(y,u,b)$ is $V_{y,u}\otimes\Lambda^{0,*}_{b}$, where $\Lambda^{0,*}_{b}$ is the space of the forms of type $(0,*)$ of $B$ at $b$, and $V_{y,u}$ is the tensor product of the fibre over $y$ of the forms of type $(0,*)$ along the fibre with values in $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ and the space of the forms of type $(0,*)$ of time and of space at the time of $y$ and at $u$; $V_{y,u}$ does not depend on $b$. For a section $\psi$ of $S_{D}$ and a point $(y,u)$, the \emph{restriction of $\psi$ to the base over $(y,u)$} is the map $b\mapsto\psi(y,u,b)$, a form of type $(0,*)$ on $B$ with values in the vector space $V_{y,u}$; the \emph{part of type $(0,q)$ along the base} of $\psi$ is the section whose restriction to the base over each $(y,u)$ is the part of type $(0,q)$ of that of $\psi$. The section $\psi$ is \emph{differentiable along the base} when each of its restrictions to the base has continuous first derivatives on $B$. Then $\bar\partial_{B}\psi$ is the section of $S_{D}$ whose restriction to the base over each $(y,u)$ is the sum, over $q=0,1,2,3$, of the part of type $(0,q+1)$ of the exterior derivative on $B$ of the part of type $(0,q)$ of the restriction of $\psi$.
\end{definition}
```
- **Place:** `supply 360`; after M2-W151
- **Step:** S14c
- **Rests on:** M1 v7.3, Definition 7.4 (l.832, the twist is a line on $E$), Theorem 7.10 (l.1064); M2-W052, M2-W053, M2-W054(ii), M2-W066, M2-W069, M2-W117; named in the mark: M2-W115, M2-W126
- **Mark:** A definition, confirmed by read-back (P254); no numbered choice. **Three identification steps, the architect's, each told to the PI in the read-back:** that M1's twist of the fibre is taken "the same over every point of space and of the base", its ground being M2-W054(ii) as logged ("$N$ the pull-back to $\widetilde{M}$ of one of the sixteen lines of (i)") and M1 l.832 (the twist is "the line $\mathcal{O}(D)$ on $E$"), as M2-W117 takes the twist at every time; that the forms of time, of space and of the base are split in two factors by the step that M2-W066 uses for a direct sum ("the exterior algebra of a direct sum $A\oplus B$ is the tensor product of the exterior algebras of $A$ and of $B$"), which M2-W066 itself takes once only; that the operators are made on $S_{D}$, with M1's twist, and not on the spinor bundle $S_{4}$ up to isomorphism (M2-W115 and M2-W126 say the like of the fibre). "Exterior derivative", "type $(0,q)$" and "tensor product" are used in their standard senses; the proof step M2-W160 states that of the types and uses the exterior derivative without stating it; "tensor product" is stated nowhere in the section. **Not said:** that $\bar\partial_{B}$ applied twice is zero; anything of space.
- **Tag:** none (a definition)

### M2-W153 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[$\bar\partial$ along the fibre, over the unfolded geometry]\label{def:dbarwhole}
Let $S_{D}$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:dbarbase}. For a section $\psi$ of $S_{D}$, a time $s$, and points $u$ of $S^{3}$ and $b$ of $B$, the \emph{restriction of $\psi$ to the fibre over $(s,u,b)$} is the map $p\mapsto\psi((p,s),u,b)$ on $E$; its values at $p$ lie in the tensor product of the fibre over $p$ of $\Lambda^{0,*}_{J_{s}}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D)$, the spinor bundle of the fibre for $J_{s}$ (Definition~\ref{def:dbar}), and the vector space $W_{s,u,b}$ of the forms of type $(0,*)$ of time and of space at $(s,u)$ tensored with $\Lambda^{0,*}_{b}$, which does not depend on $p$. The section $\psi$ is \emph{differentiable along the fibre} when, in a basis of $W_{s,u,b}$, the components of each such restriction in the summand $\mathcal{O}(D)$ are differentiable sections of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ (Definition~\ref{def:dbar}). Then $\bar\partial\psi$ is the section of $S_{D}$ whose restriction to the fibre over each $(s,u,b)$ has, as its components in that basis, the $\bar\partial$ of Definition~\ref{def:dbar}, for $J_{s}$, of the components of the restriction of $\psi$.
\end{definition}
```

### M2-W154 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the base and the whole geometry]\label{prop:wholesupply}
Let $\widetilde{M}$, $\widetilde{J}$, $B$, $D$, $\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{M}}(D)$, $S_{D}$ and $V_{y,u}$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:dbarbase}. (i) $\bar\partial_{B}$ does not depend on a basis of $V_{y,u}$ or on coordinates of $B$: it is made from the complex structures of $\CPtwo$ and of $\Stwo$ and from the exterior derivative on $B$, and no length enters it. It carries the part of type $(0,q)$ along the base into the part of type $(0,q+1)$ along the base, and it is zero on the part of type $(0,3)$: $B$ has complex dimension three, and its forms of type $(0,q)$ have the ranks $1,3,3,1$ for $q=0,1,2,3$ (certificates \texttt{L10\_base\_dim}, \texttt{L10\_types}). (ii) Let $D'$ be a divisor on the corners, with $V'_{y,u}$ for $S_{D'}$ as $V_{y,u}$ for $S_{D}$, let $\gamma$ be a one-to-one map of $Y\times S^{3}$ onto itself, and let $\Theta$ be a map from $S_{D}$ to $S_{D'}$ that carries the fibre over each $(y,u,b)$ to the fibre over $(\gamma(y,u),b)$ by $\theta_{y,u}\otimes1$, where $\theta_{y,u}$ is a linear map of $V_{y,u}$ into $V'_{\gamma(y,u)}$ that does not depend on $b$ and $1$ is the identity of $\Lambda^{0,*}_{b}$. Then $\Theta$ carries sections differentiable along the base to sections differentiable along the base, and $\bar\partial_{B}$ to $\bar\partial_{B}$. (iii) The following are maps as in (ii): (a) for the first and for the second lift of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ (Definition~\ref{def:twisttime}), read on $\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{M}}(D)$, the lift of the fold $\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda$ of $S_{D}$ (Definition~\ref{def:spinorwhole}); (b) for $D$ one of the five divisors of Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(iv), the isomorphism of Proposition~\ref{prop:fivetime}(iii), multiplication by $m_{D}$ in the frames $1$, read from $S_{D}$ to $S_{3P_{4}}$; (c) with $\tau_{Y}$, $D'$ and $\nu_{D}$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime}(iv), the lift of the step $(\tau^{-1})^{*}\otimes\nu_{D}$ from $S_{D'}$ to $S_{D}$ (Definition~\ref{def:step}), and its product with a continuous function with no zero on $\widetilde{M}$ that depends on the time and on the point of space alone; (d) the isomorphism of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(vi) between the spinor bundles for $\widetilde{J}$ and for $\widetilde{J}'$, taken with the line $\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{M}}(D)$. Each of them therefore carries $\bar\partial_{B}$ to $\bar\partial_{B}$. (iv) Each of the maps of (iii) carries sections differentiable along the fibre to sections differentiable along the fibre and the $\bar\partial$ of Definition~\ref{def:dbarwhole} to itself. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `supply 380`; after M2-W153
- **Step:** S14c
- **Rests on:** M1 v7.3, Proposition 7.2 (l.792), Section 7.4 (l.827), Theorem 7.10 (l.1064); M2-W052, M2-W053, M2-W054(vi), M2-W062, M2-W066, M2-W069, M2-W071, M2-W105, M2-W106(iii) and (iv), M2-W117, M2-W118(iv), M2-W140(iii), M2-W152, M2-W153; the proof, M2-W160, M2-W156 and M2-W161 (M2-W155 and M2-W157 retired after the fresh reader's check, P255)
- **Mark:** CHECKED (the proof, M2-W160, M2-W156 and M2-W161, by hand; certificate `MEFProof/Paper_M2/M2_L10_base.lean`, four theorems named `L10_…`, of which `L10_base_dim` and `L10_types` certify the counts of part (i), namely that $4/2+2/2=3$ and that the numbers of ways of taking $q$ of three are $1,3,3,1,0$, and `L10_types_sum` and `L10_rank` certify counts that no entry uses; that the forms of type $(0,q)$ have those ranks is the text's (M2-W160) and is not certified, so that the parenthesis of (i) names the certificates for the numbers and not for the ranks: found by the fresh reader, P255, and told to the PI in the closing list; script `papers/M2/checks/S14c_base.py`, parts (1) to (3), numerical at sample points for two changes of coordinates, and the counts); confirmed by read-back (P254). An addition to M1: M1 has no operator on the base. **Identification steps, the architect's, told to the PI in the read-back:** the lifts of the fold, the multipliers and the lifts of the step read on $S_{D}$. **Not shown, told to the PI in the read-back:** the converse of (iii); that $\bar\partial_{B}$ applied twice is zero; anything of space. **Told to the PI in the closing list and not in the read-back:** in (iii) the words "maps as in (ii)" cover (c) with the two divisors exchanged and (d) with the bundle made for $\widetilde{J}'$ in the second place, as the proof step M2-W156 says; that the exterior derivative does not depend on coordinates is the chain rule, taken as standard and read at no source (M2-W160); part (iv)(c) uses M2-W118(iv), whose "differentiable along the fibre" is the term of M2-W117, at each time (M2-W161); the letter $D'$ is the second divisor in (ii) and the first in (iii)(c), and $1$ is the identity of $\Lambda^{0,*}_{b}$ in (ii) and the frame in (iii)(b) (found by the fresh reader, P255).

### M2-W155 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i). Let $v_{1},\dots,v_{n}$ be a basis of $V_{y,u}$. A form $\varphi$ on $B$ with values in $V_{y,u}$ is $\sum_{k}\varphi_{k}v_{k}$ for forms $\varphi_{k}$ on $B$ with complex values, and its exterior derivative is $\sum_{k}(\mathrm{d}\varphi_{k})v_{k}$. For another basis $v'_{j}=\sum_{k}c_{jk}v_{k}$, with constants $c_{jk}$, and $\varphi=\sum_{j}\varphi'_{j}v'_{j}$, one has $\varphi_{k}=\sum_{j}c_{jk}\varphi'_{j}$ and, $\mathrm{d}$ being linear, $\mathrm{d}\varphi_{k}=\sum_{j}c_{jk}\,\mathrm{d}\varphi'_{j}$, so that $\sum_{k}(\mathrm{d}\varphi_{k})v_{k}=\sum_{j}(\mathrm{d}\varphi'_{j})v'_{j}$: the exterior derivative does not depend on the basis. The exterior derivative of a form on $B$ does not depend on the coordinates in which it is computed (the chain rule). A one-form $\alpha$ at a point of $B$ is of type $(0,1)$ when $\alpha(Jv)=-i\,\alpha(v)$ for every tangent vector $v$, $J$ being the complex structure of $B$, that of $\CPtwo$ on the one factor and that of $\Stwo$ on the other, and of type $(1,0)$ when $\alpha(Jv)=i\,\alpha(v)$; every one-form is in one way the sum of one of each type, a form of degree $q$ is of type $(0,q)$ when it is a sum of products of $q$ forms of type $(0,1)$, and every form of degree $q+1$ is in one way a sum of forms that are sums of products of $a$ forms of type $(1,0)$ and $q+1-a$ of type $(0,1)$, $a=0,\dots,q+1$; its part of type $(0,q+1)$ is the term with $a=0$. So $\bar\partial_{B}$ is made from $J$ and the exterior derivative, and from no length. $B$ has real dimension $4+2$, and so complex dimension three (certificate \texttt{L10\_base\_dim}). Its forms of type $(0,1)$ at a point are a space of dimension three, and those of type $(0,q)$ a space whose dimension is the number of ways of taking $q$ of three, which is $1,3,3,1$ for $q=0,1,2,3$ and $0$ for $q=4$ (\texttt{L10\_types}). The exterior derivative of a form of type $(0,q)$ has degree $q+1$, and $\bar\partial_{B}$ takes its part of type $(0,q+1)$, which for $q=3$ is zero.
```
- **Place:** `supply 390`; the first step of the proof of Proposition `prop:wholesupply` (M2-W154)
- **Step:** S14c
- **Rests on:** M2-W152; M2-W053 (the complex structures of $\CPtwo$ and of $\Stwo$)
- **Mark:** CHECKED by hand, in full, for the independence of the basis. **Standard and read at no source (weak; on the list of weak steps):** that the exterior derivative does not depend on coordinates, and the decomposition of forms by type on a manifold with a complex structure; the script `papers/M2/checks/S14c_base.py`, parts (1) and (2), checks at sample points that the part of type $(0,1)$ of the derivative of a function is the same in M1's coordinates $z$ and $w=1/z$ of the sphere (l.827) and in two coordinate systems of the projective plane, and no more. The counts: certificate `L10_base_dim`, `L10_types`; script part (3). No reading.

- **Failure test:** a basis in which the exterior derivative differs; coordinates of $B$ in which the part of type $(0,q+1)$ differs; a form of type $(0,4)$ of $B$ that is not zero.

### M2-W156 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(ii) and (iii). Let $\Theta$ be as in (ii) and $\psi$ a section of $S_{D}$. The image of $\psi$ is the section $\Theta\circ\psi\circ g^{-1}$, $g$ being the map $(y,u,b)\mapsto(\gamma(y,u),b)$, and its restriction to the base over $\gamma(y,u)$ is $b\mapsto(\theta_{y,u}\otimes1)\,\psi(y,u,b)$. In bases of $V_{y,u}$ and of $V'_{\gamma(y,u)}$ the components of this form are sums of those of the restriction of $\psi$ with coefficients, the entries of the matrix of $\theta_{y,u}$, that do not depend on $b$; so they have continuous first derivatives if those of $\psi$ have, their parts of each type are the same sums of the parts of that type, and, the exterior derivative being linear, so are their exterior derivatives. Hence $\bar\partial_{B}$ of the image is the image of $\bar\partial_{B}\psi$. This is (ii). In (iii) the maps of (c) are as in (ii) with the two divisors exchanged, and that of (d) with the bundle made for $\widetilde{J}'$ in the second place. For (a): the fold of $\widetilde{M}$ is the fold of $Y$ and the identity on $S^{3}\times B$ (Definition~\ref{def:unfolded}); $\iota^{*}$ is the identity on the forms of time, of space and of the base (Definition~\ref{def:spinorwhole}) and the pull-back by the fold of $Y$ on those along the fibre; and the first and the second lift carry the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ over $y$ to that over the image of $y$ under the fold. So $\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda$ is $\theta_{y,u}\otimes1$, with $\gamma(y,u)$ the image of $y$ under the fold, with $u$. For (b): multiplication by $m_{D}$ in the frames $1$ is a linear map of the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ over $y$ onto that of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(3P_{4})$ over $y$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:fivetime}(iii)), and $\gamma$ is the identity. For (c): $\tau$ is $\tau_{Y}$ on $Y$ and the identity on $S^{3}\times B$ (Definition~\ref{def:step}), so that $(\tau^{-1})^{*}$ is the identity on $\Lambda^{0,*}_{b}$ and, on the forms along the fibre and of time and of space, a linear map that depends on $(y,u)$ alone; $\nu_{D}$ carries the fibre of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D')$ over $y$ onto that of $\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)$ over $\tau_{Y}(y)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime}(iv)); and a function of the time and of the point of space alone multiplies $\theta_{y,u}$ by a number that does not depend on $b$. Here $\gamma(y,u)$ is $\tau_{Y}(y)$ with $u$. For (d): the isomorphism of Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(vi) is the pull-back of an isomorphism between the two bundles of forms on $T\times S^{3}$, tensored with the identity on the other factors and on the line (the last step of the proof of that proposition); it does not depend on $b$, and $\gamma$ is the identity.
```

### M2-W158 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
After Proposition~\ref{prop:fivetime} it was said that nothing is said there of space, of the base, or of the unfolded geometry as a whole. By Proposition~\ref{prop:wholesupply}(i), on the base $\bar\partial_{B}$ is made from the complex structures of $\CPtwo$ and of $\Stwo$ and from the exterior derivative on $B$, and no length enters it. Along the fibre, $\bar\partial$ is made at each time from $J_{s}$ and $D$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:fibre}(i)), and $J_{s}$ is the only complex structure on the tangent planes of the fibre that keeps the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, on the fibre at $s$ and the handedness of $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:fibretime}(i)); the derivative in $s$ is taken along $\partial_{s}$, which has length one in the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8 (Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}(i)). The lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, on time together with the fibre therefore enter the operations made in this section; no length on space, on the base, or between the base and the fibre enters any of them. By Proposition~\ref{prop:wholesupply}(iii)(c) and~(iv), the lift of the step $(\tau^{-1})^{*}\otimes\nu_{D}$, multiplied by a continuous function with no zero that depends on the time and on the point of space alone, carries $\bar\partial_{B}$ to $\bar\partial_{B}$ and the $\bar\partial$ of Definition~\ref{def:dbarwhole} to itself. The constants $1$ and $-1$ are such functions: on the unfolded geometry, $\bar\partial_{B}$ and $\bar\partial$ along the fibre do not decide the sixth choice of this paper. No operation made in this section is an action of an element of the fibre algebra on a state, and nothing made in this section makes, from a state, a field on time and space.
```

### M2-W159 · retired (reset, Decision Log P264)
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
Proposition~\ref{prop:wholesupply} speaks of the unfolded geometry and of the directions along the base and along the fibre. No operation is made here that differentiates a state along space: to make one, the forms of type $(0,*)$ of time and of space at different points of space would have to be compared, and nothing in this section compares them; M1 singles out no complex structure on the directions of time and of space, and $J_{0}$ was fixed to write the spinors down (Definition~\ref{def:spinorwhole}). It is not shown here that what M1 gives makes such an operation, and it is not shown that it makes none. It is not shown that the maps of Proposition~\ref{prop:wholesupply}(iii) are the only ones that carry $\bar\partial_{B}$ to $\bar\partial_{B}$. Nothing is said here of $\bar\partial_{B}$ applied twice, of the order in which $\bar\partial_{B}$ and $\bar\partial$ along the fibre are applied, of how the functions $m_{D}$ are carried by the step, of the divisors of flux three whose odd corner is not $P_{4}$, or of whether what M1 gives singles out one of the functions $A$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:timedir}(v). This section does not fix which of the two pieces of M1's display is scaled, and by a function on which.
```

### M2-W163 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the shadow of a state]\label{def:shadowstate}
Let $\psi$ be a state of the geometry, read through the lift of the fold (Definition~\ref{def:unknown}). The \emph{shadow} of $\psi$ is the shadow, as M1, Definition~2.1(i), has it, of the set of points of the geometry at which $\psi$ is not zero: the set of points $(s,x)$ of time and space such that $\psi$ is not zero at some point $(p,s,x,b)$ of $\widetilde{M}$, $p\in E$, $b\in\CPtwo\times\Stwo$, the time $s$ being taken on the time circle of three steps. That the shadow of a state is the shadow of the set on which it is not zero is a choice and is not derived: it is the eighth choice of this paper. Clause~(b) of M1, Definition~3.2, is its ground: it brings in no object that M1 does not have. The shadow of a state records where in time and space the state is present; the values of the state, and any field on time and space, are not part of it.
\end{definition}
```
- **Place:** `conditions 070`; after M2-W162
- **Step:** S07
- **Rests on:** M2-W162; M1, Definition 2.1(i), l.154; M2-W052, M2-W085; clause (b) of M1, Definition 3.2
- **Mark:** DECLARED: a reading, the eighth choice of this paper (Minimality M2-8), confirmed by read-back (P265); the PI's choice of option 1 of choice 1 of P222 (P256), "shadow" as M1 has it (P262). The alternatives not taken are options 2 and 3 of choice 1 of P222. The cost was told to the PI in the words of P222 ("Its values, and any field in four dimensions, are not part of the shadow."); the last sentence of the statement says it in the statement's own words ("any field on time and space"), as the PI confirmed them (corrected after the fresh reader's check, chat C8b5). The fold of the unfolded geometry is the identity on time and space (M2-W052), so the set is the image under M1's $\pi$ of a subset of the geometry of M1, Theorem 8.1.

Let $\psi$ be a state. The section $((\tau^{-1})^{*}\otimes\nu_{1})\circ\psi\circ\tau^{-1}$ of $S_{2}$ that one step makes of $\psi$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}(iv)) is not zero at $\tau(m)$ exactly when $\psi$ is not zero at $m$, the lift of the step being linear and one-to-one on each fibre (Definition~\ref{def:step}). Since $\tau(p,s,r)=(M_{0}p,s-L,r)$, the set of points $(s,x)$ of time and space over which that section is not zero is the shadow of $\psi$ moved by one step in time: it is the set of the points $(s-L,x)$, $(s,x)$ in the shadow of $\psi$. ### M2-W165 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the fifth axiom for an equation]\label{def:A5eq}
An equation for a state (Definition~\ref{def:family}) \emph{meets the fifth axiom} if (i) it has two linearly independent solutions with the same shadow; and (ii) it has a solution whose shadow after one step is not its shadow. That the fifth axiom asks this of the equation is a choice and is not derived: it is the ninth choice of this paper. Clause~(i) is M1, Definition~2.1(iii), asked of the solutions, and clause~(ii) is the last words of M1, Definition~2.1(ii), ``what is seen of one thing is not the same at every step'', asked of a solution. The first words of M1, Definition~2.1(ii), on the structure, are met by what is given, whichever equation is taken (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}(iv)).
\end{definition}
```

### M2-W167 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the maps]\label{prop:maps}
Let $\widetilde{M}$, $\widetilde{J}$, $\tau$, $N_{4}$, $\lambda_{4}$ and $S_{4}$ be as in Proposition~\ref{prop:step}, and $s$, $x$, $y$, $L$, $\kappa$ and $S$ as in M1, Proposition~5.8. (i) The fold keeps the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, keeps $\widetilde{J}$, commutes with $\tau$, and carries $S_{4}$ to itself by its lift of the fold. (ii) The maps $\tau$ and $\tau$ applied twice keep those lengths and $\widetilde{J}$ and commute with $\tau$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}(i)). Neither has a lift from $N_{4}$ to itself that carries $\lambda_{4}$ to $\lambda_{4}$ or to $-\lambda_{4}$: they carry the corner circle of $P_{4}$ to that of $P_{2}$ and to that of $P_{3}$. Applied three times $\tau$ is the identity. (iii) For a real number $c$ the map $(p,s,r)\mapsto(p,s+c,r)$ multiplies the lengths along $x$ by $e^{\kappa c}$ and those along $y$ by $e^{-\kappa c}$; it keeps the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, only if $c=0$. (iv) The map $R(p,s,r)=(Sp,-s,r)$, time run backwards together with the quarter-turn, is well defined on $\widetilde{M}$, since $M_{0}^{3}SM_{0}^{3}=S$. It keeps the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, and commutes with the fold. It carries the corner circles of $P_{1}$ and of $P_{4}$ to themselves and exchanges those of $P_{2}$ and $P_{3}$. It is holomorphic from the fibre at the time $s$ to the fibre at the time $-s$, with $z_{-s}\circ R=-i\,z_{s}$. Applied twice it is the fold, and $R\tau R^{-1}=\tau^{-1}$. It carries $\partial_{s}$ to $-\partial_{s}$ and is the identity on space, so that it reverses the handedness of the directions of time and of space and does not keep $J_{0}$. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `supply 010`; the first entry of the section, and of card T1
- **Step:** T1
- **Rests on:** M1, Proposition 5.8 (`prop:A2`), l.704; Remark 3.4 (`rem:mirror`), l.291; the paragraph after Corollary 7.7, l.949; Proposition 4.4 (`prop:threecycle`); M2-W039, M2-W048, M2-W052, M2-W053, M2-W069, M2-W082; M2-W168

### M2-W168 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
Let $f$ be a map of $\widetilde{M}$ that commutes with the fold and carries the corner circle of $P_{1}$ to itself, and let $\nu$ be a continuous map of $N_{4}$ to itself that carries the fibre over each point $m$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre over $f(m)$, with $\nu\lambda_{4}=\epsilon\lambda_{4}\nu$, $\epsilon=\pm1$. Over a point $m$ of a corner circle $C$, on which $\lambda_{4}$ has the sign $\epsilon_{C}$, this gives $\epsilon_{C}=\epsilon\,\epsilon_{f(C)}$. For $C$ the corner circle of $P_{1}$ it gives $\epsilon=1$, and then for that of $P_{4}$ it gives $-1=\epsilon_{f(C)}$: $f$ carries the corner circle of $P_{4}$ to itself.
```

### M2-W170 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[time run backwards and the spinor bundle]\label{prop:reverse}
Let $\widetilde{M}$, $\widetilde{J}$, $N_{4}$, $\lambda_{4}$ and $S_{4}$ be as in Proposition~\ref{prop:step}, $R$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iv), and $\mathrm{d}s,e^{1},e^{2},e^{3}$ the forms dual to the frame $\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$ of Definition~\ref{def:spinorwhole}. (i) There is a continuous map $\nu_{R}$ of $N_{4}$ to itself that carries the fibre over each point $m$ of $\widetilde{M}$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre over $R(m)$, with $\nu_{R}\lambda_{4}=\lambda_{4}\nu_{R}$. (ii) $R$ carries $\widetilde{J}$ to the almost complex structure $\widetilde{J}^{R}$ that is $\widetilde{J}$ on the directions along the fibre and on the base and is, on the directions of time and of space, the structure $J_{0}^{R}$ with $J_{0}^{R}\partial_{s}=-e_{1}$ and $J_{0}^{R}e_{2}=e_{3}$; $J_{0}^{R}$ has the handedness opposite to that of $J_{0}$. The $(0,1)$-forms of $J_{0}$ are spanned at each point by $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$ and $e^{2}-ie^{3}$, those of $J_{0}^{R}$ by $\mathrm{d}s+ie^{1}$ and $e^{2}-ie^{3}$. The pull-back $(R^{-1})^{*}$ carries the $(0,*)$-forms of $\widetilde{J}$ at $m$ to the $(0,*)$-forms of $\widetilde{J}^{R}$ at $R(m)$: $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ to $-i\,\mathrm{d}\bar z_{-s}$, $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$ to $-(\mathrm{d}s+ie^{1})$, $e^{2}-ie^{3}$ to itself, and the forms of the base to themselves. (iii) Let $B$ be the map from the $(0,*)$-forms of $\widetilde{J}^{R}$ to those of $\widetilde{J}$, covering the identity of $\widetilde{M}$, that is linear on each fibre, keeps the product of forms, carries $\mathrm{d}s+ie^{1}$ to $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$ and is the identity on $e^{2}-ie^{3}$, on the forms of the fibre and on the forms of the base. Then $\Phi_{QC}=(B\circ(R^{-1})^{*})\otimes\nu_{R}$ is a continuous map of $S_{4}$ to itself that carries the fibre over each point $m$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre over $R(m)$, and $\Phi_{QC}\circ(\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda_{4})=(\iota^{*}\otimes\lambda_{4})\circ\Phi_{QC}$. In this sense $R$ has a lift that carries $S_{4}$ with its lift of the fold to itself. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```

### M2-W171 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i). Let $R_{Y}(p,s)=(Sp,-s)$ be the map of $Y$ that $R$ covers; it is well defined and commutes with the fold of $Y$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iv)). Let $N_{Y}$, with its lift of the fold $\lambda_{Y}$, be the line on $Y$ of which $N_{4}$ with $\lambda_{4}$ is the pull-back (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}). The pull-back $R_{Y}^{*}N_{Y}$, whose fibre over a point $y$ is the fibre of $N_{Y}$ over $R_{Y}(y)$, is a line on $Y$, and $\lambda_{Y}$ gives a lift of the fold to it, since $R_{Y}$ commutes with the fold. Its restriction to the fibre over $s=0$ is the pull-back by $S$ of the restriction of $N_{Y}$; it has degree three, since $S$ is a one-to-one continuous map of $E$ onto itself that keeps the handedness ($\det S=1$). The sign of its lift on the corner circle of $P_{i}$ is the sign of $\lambda_{Y}$ on the corner circle of $SP_{i}$; $S$ leaves $P_{1}$ and $P_{4}$ in place and exchanges $P_{2}$ and $P_{3}$, so that the signs on the corner circles of $P_{1},P_{2},P_{3},P_{4}$ are $+1,+1,+1,-1$, those of $\lambda_{Y}$. By Proposition~\ref{prop:time}(ii) there is an isomorphism of $N_{Y}$ onto $R_{Y}^{*}N_{Y}$ that covers the identity of $Y$ and carries the one lift to the other. Followed by the map of $R_{Y}^{*}N_{Y}$ to $N_{Y}$ that covers $R_{Y}$ and is the identity on each fibre, it is a continuous map of $N_{Y}$ to itself that carries the fibre over $y$ linearly and one-to-one onto the fibre over $R_{Y}(y)$ and commutes with $\lambda_{Y}$. Its pull-back to $\widetilde{M}$ is $\nu_{R}$: $R$ covers $R_{Y}$ and is the identity on $S^{3}\times\CPtwo\times\Stwo$.
```

### M2-W172 · active

(ii). $R$ carries $\partial_{s}$ to $-\partial_{s}$ and $e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$ to themselves (Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iv)), so that the structure to which it carries $J_{0}$ sends $-\partial_{s}$ to $e_{1}$ and $e_{2}$ to $e_{3}$: it is $J_{0}^{R}$. The frame $\partial_{s},J_{0}\partial_{s},e_{2},J_{0}e_{2}$ is $\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$, and the frame $\partial_{s},J_{0}^{R}\partial_{s},e_{2},J_{0}^{R}e_{2}$ is $\partial_{s},-e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$: the two have opposite handedness. A form is of type $(0,1)$ for a structure $J$ when it vanishes on every vector $v-iJv$; $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$ and $e^{2}-ie^{3}$ vanish on $\partial_{s}-ie_{1}$ and $e_{2}-ie_{3}$ and on their multiples, and $\mathrm{d}s+ie^{1}$ and $e^{2}-ie^{3}$ vanish on $\partial_{s}+ie_{1}$ and $e_{2}-ie_{3}$ and on their multiples. Since $(R^{-1})^{*}\mathrm{d}s=-\mathrm{d}s$ and $(R^{-1})^{*}e^{a}=e^{a}$ for $a=1,2,3$, the pull-back carries $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$ to $-(\mathrm{d}s+ie^{1})$ and $e^{2}-ie^{3}$ to itself. On the fibre $z_{-s}\circ R=-i\,z_{s}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iv)), so that $R$ carries the complex structure of the fibre at the time $s$ to that of the fibre at the time $-s$, $z_{s}\circ R^{-1}=i\,z_{-s}$, and $(R^{-1})^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=-i\,\mathrm{d}\bar z_{-s}$. On the base $R$ is the identity.

### M2-W175 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the twist along time; smooth states]\label{def:smoothstate}
Let $z_{s}$ be the coordinates of Definition~\ref{def:step}, $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ the $(0,1)$-form of the fibre at the time $s$ as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}, and $D=\sum a_{i}P_{i}$ a divisor of flux three on the corners whose odd corner is $P_{4}$ (M1, Definition~7.4 and Corollary~7.7). At each time $s$ the twist of the fibre is taken to be the line $\mathcal{O}(D)$ of M1, Definition~7.4, for the complex structure of $z_{s}$: its states at rest at the time $s$ are the functions on the fibre at $s$ that are holomorphic in $z_{s}$ off the corners and whose poles are bounded by $D$. That the twist at each time is this line, for the complex structure that the lengths of M1, Proposition~5.8, give at that time, is a reading and is not derived: it is the tenth choice of this paper. Clause~(b) of M1, Definition~3.2, is its ground: it brings in no object that M1 does not have. M1 defines the twist and its states at rest for one complex structure, and has of time: ``The drive is not a holomorphic map of $E$; it acts on structures through its action on the corners.'' (M1, Definition~7.4). Near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ let $w_{i}(p,s)=z_{s}(p-P_{i})$. A \emph{smooth section of the twist}, for $D$, is a complex function $f$ on $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles, smooth there, such that $w_{i}^{a_{i}}f$ extends to a smooth function across the points over the corner circle of $P_{i}$, for $i=1,\dots,4$. The fold acts on these by $f\mapsto\epsilon\,f\circ\iota$, with $\epsilon=+1$ if $a_{4}$ is odd and $\epsilon=-1$ if $a_{4}$ is even: of the two lifts of M1, Definition~7.4, it is the one whose signs are $+1,+1,+1,-1$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}(iv)). A \emph{smooth section of $S_{4}$}, for $D$, is a form $\psi$ on $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles, with complex coefficients, smooth there and of type $(0,*)$ for $\widetilde{J}$, such that $w_{i}^{a_{i}}\psi$ extends smoothly across the points over the corner circle of $P_{i}$, for $i=1,\dots,4$. The lift of the fold acts on these by $\psi\mapsto\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$, and a \emph{smooth state} is a smooth section of $S_{4}$ that it leaves unchanged. An \emph{operation of differentiation along time} is a map $\nabla$ of the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ to themselves with $\nabla(\psi+\psi')=\nabla\psi+\nabla\psi'$ and $\nabla(f\psi)=(\partial_{s}f)\psi+f\,\nabla\psi$ for every smooth function $f$ on $\widetilde{M}$.
\end{definition}
```
- **Place:** `supply 032`; after M2-W174; the first entry of card T2
- **Step:** T2
- **Rests on:** M1 v7.3, Definition 7.4 (`def:states`), Corollary 7.7, Proposition 5.8, Definition 3.2 clause (b); M2-W069 (the coordinates $z_{s}$), M2-W048 (the form $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$; $z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$), M2-W023(iv) (the signs), M2-W052, M2-W053, M2-W082 ($S_{4}$); Minimality M2-10
- **Mark:** DECLARED (a reading and a numbered choice, the tenth of M2; the reading is the architect's and was confirmed by the PI by read-back, P276, with its opposite: that the twist is given only as a line with its flux and its signs, under which nothing is determined along the fibre). That it is numbered the tenth is presentation. READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.832 ("A \emph{twist of the fibre} of \emph{flux} $d\geq1$ is the line $\mathcal{O}(D)$ on $E$ of a divisor $D=\sum a_{i}P_{i}$ on the corners, with $a_{i}\geq0$ and $\sum a_{i}=d$."), l.834 ("A \emph{state at rest} is a holomorphic section of the twist: for $d\geq1$, a function $f$ on the covering torus $E$ whose poles are bounded by $D$. The fold acts on states at rest by $f(z)\mapsto f(-z)$ or by $f(z)\mapsto-f(-z)$, its two lifts, called here the first and the second."), l.836 ("The \emph{corner sign} of the twist at $P_{i}$ is $(-1)^{a_{i}}$, the action of the first lift on the fibre of $\mathcal{O}(D)$ there."), l.838 (the quotation; and "The \emph{structure} of a twist is its flux together with its corner signs up to the overall sign", which is the ground of the opposite reading), l.829 ("with a complex structure for which the fold is $\iota(z)=-z$"). CHECKED: `papers/M2/checks/T2_operations.py`, A3 (the five divisors) and A5 (for each of the five, the signs $\epsilon(-1)^{a_{i}}$ are $+1,+1,+1,-1$); certificate `MEFProof/Paper_M2/M2_L13_operations.lean`, `L13_flux`, `L13_odd_P4`. Hand steps, in full: $p-P_{i}$ is taken with its small representative in $\R^{2}$, on which $z_{s}$ is linear, so $w_{i}$ is a smooth function near the corner circle, with a simple zero on it; $w_{i}(\iota(p),s)=z_{s}(-p-P_{i})=-w_{i}(p,s)$, since $2P_{i}\in\Z^{2}$; the two names $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ of a point give the same $w_{i}$, since $z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$ (M2-W048) and $M_{0}^{3}P_{i}=P_{i}$ modulo $\Z^{2}$ (M2-W039), so the conditions do not depend on the name; in the frame $w_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ near the corner circle a section $f=g\,w_{i}^{-a_{i}}$ has the value $g$, and $\epsilon f\circ\iota=\epsilon(-1)^{a_{i}}(g\circ\iota)\,w_{i}^{-a_{i}}$, so the sign there is $\epsilon(-1)^{a_{i}}$. **Identification steps** (CLAUDE.md §5): (1) the reading itself; (2) the smooth functions with $w_{i}^{a_{i}}f$ smooth are taken as the smooth sections of the line whose holomorphic sections are M1's functions with poles bounded by $D$; (3) "smooth" is new in M2: the lines of the earlier entries are continuous, and M2-W086 does not say how smooth a state is. Not shown and not claimed: that a state must be smooth; anything for a divisor whose odd corner is not $P_{4}$

### M2-W176 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the operations of order one]\label{prop:operations}
Let $D$, $\epsilon$, $w_{i}$ and the smooth sections of the twist and of $S_{4}$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:smoothstate}. (i) The smooth sections of the twist, for $D$, are sections of a line on $\widetilde{M}$ that is pulled back from $Y$ and carries the lift of the fold $f\mapsto\epsilon\,f\circ\iota$; an isomorphism that covers the identity of $\widetilde{M}$ carries it to $N_{4}$ and its lift of the fold to $\lambda_{4}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}). In this sense the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ are sections of $S_{4}$. (ii) Space and the base. Let $\mathrm{d}=\mathrm{d}_{Y}+\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}+\mathrm{d}_{B}$ be the exterior derivative of $\widetilde{M}=Y\times S^{3}\times(\CPtwo\times\Stwo)$, written as the sum of its parts along the three factors. For a smooth section $\psi$ of $S_{4}$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi$ are forms on $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles, with complex coefficients and smooth there, and $w_{i}^{a_{i}}\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi$ and $w_{i}^{a_{i}}\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi$ extend smoothly across the points over the corner circle of $P_{i}$; they are not in general of type $(0,*)$. $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ commute with $\psi\mapsto\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$ and with multiplication by every smooth function pulled back from $Y$. (iii) The fibre. Every smooth section of $S_{4}$ is in exactly one way $\psi=\psi'+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''$, where $\psi'$ and $\psi''$ are at each point $(0,*)$-forms of time, of space and of the base (Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(iv)). On the fibre over a time $s$ and a point $r$ of $S^{3}\times\CPtwo\times\Stwo$ these forms are one vector space, and with $z_{s}=a+ib$, $a$ and $b$ real, let $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}=\tfrac12(\partial/\partial a+i\,\partial/\partial b)$ act on $\psi'$ along that fibre. Then
\[
\bar\partial_{F}\psi\;=\;\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\frac{\partial\psi'}{\partial\bar z_{s}}
\]
is a smooth section of $S_{4}$; $\bar\partial_{F}$ commutes with $\psi\mapsto\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$, so that it carries a smooth state to a smooth section that the lift of the fold leaves unchanged, and it commutes with multiplication by every smooth function that is constant along the fibres. A smooth section $f$ of the twist on the fibre at $s$ has $\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$ exactly when it is a state at rest at the time $s$. (iv) For two of the five divisors $D$, $D'$ of flux three whose odd corner is $P_{4}$, and each time $s$, there is a function $h_{s}$ on the fibre at $s$, a product of integer powers of $\wp-e_{1}$, $\wp-e_{2}$, $\wp-e_{3}$ and $\wp'$ for the periods $z_{s}(1,0)$ and $z_{s}(0,1)$, such that $f\mapsto h_{s}f$ carries the smooth sections of the twist for $D$ on that fibre one-to-one onto those for $D'$, carries the lift of the fold to the lift of the fold, and commutes with $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$. (v) The map $\psi'\mapsto\partial\psi'/\partial z_{s}$, with $\partial/\partial z_{s}=\tfrac12(\partial/\partial a-i\,\partial/\partial b)$, does not carry the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ to smooth sections of $S_{4}$, nor the smooth states. (vi) Time. The map $\psi\mapsto\partial_{s}\psi$, taken on the coefficients of $\psi$ in the forms $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$, $e^{2}-ie^{3}$ and the forms of the base, does not carry the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ to smooth sections of $S_{4}$, nor the smooth states. Let $c(s)=\exp\bigl(\cos(2\pi s/L)\bigr)$. Multiplication by $c$ is a map of the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ onto themselves that covers the identity of $\widetilde{M}$; it commutes with the lift of the fold, with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ and $\bar\partial_{F}$, with the maps $h_{s}$ of (iv), with the pull-backs by the step $\tau$ and by $R$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iv)), and it carries the states at rest at each time to states at rest at that time. For every operation of differentiation along time $\nabla$,
\[
c\,\nabla\bigl(c^{-1}\psi\bigr)\;=\;\nabla\psi+\frac{2\pi}{L}\sin\Bigl(\frac{2\pi s}{L}\Bigr)\psi ,
\]
which is not $\nabla\psi$. No operation of differentiation along time is therefore carried to itself by every map of $S_{4}$ to itself that covers the identity and carries to itself each of the things named; in this sense what is given determines an operation of order one along space and along the base, the operation $\bar\partial_{F}$ along the fibre, and none along time. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `supply 034`; after M2-W175

### M2-W177 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i). Off the corner circles the function $1$, and near the corner circle of $P_{i}$ the function $w_{i}^{-a_{i}}$, are smooth sections of the twist with no zero in the sense that every smooth section there is a smooth function times them; on the overlap the one is $w_{i}^{a_{i}}$ times the other, a smooth function with no zero. The smooth sections of the twist are therefore the smooth sections of a line, and since $w_{i}$ and the conditions are those of $Y$, the same at the points of $\widetilde{M}$ over one point of $Y$, the line is pulled back from $Y$. The map $f\mapsto\epsilon\,f\circ\iota$ carries smooth sections to smooth sections, since $w_{i}\circ\iota=-w_{i}$; applied twice it is the identity; it is a lift of the fold, and its sign on the corner circle of $P_{i}$ is $\epsilon(-1)^{a_{i}}$: $+1,+1,+1,-1$. On the fibre over $s=0$ the line is the twist $\mathcal{O}(D)$ for the complex structure of $z_{0}$, a line of degree three (Proposition~\ref{prop:pillowcase}). $N_{4}$ with $\lambda_{4}$ is the pull-back of a line on $Y$ with a lift of the fold whose restriction to the fibre over $s=0$ has degree three and the same signs (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}), and two such lines on $Y$ are carried one to the other by an isomorphism that covers the identity of $Y$ and carries the one lift to the other (Proposition~\ref{prop:time}(ii)). This is (i).
```

This is (ii).
