### M2-W179 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii) and (iv). The $(0,*)$-forms of $\widetilde{J}$ at a point are the tensor product of those of the fibre, spanned by $1$ and $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, with those of time, of space and of the base (Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(iv)); this gives $\psi=\psi'+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''$ in exactly one way, and the forms of time, of space and of the base, being pulled back from $T\times S^{3}\times\CPtwo\times\Stwo$, are one vector space along a fibre. Since $w_{i}$ is $z_{s}$ less a constant on the fibre, $\partial w_{i}/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$, so $w_{i}^{a_{i}}\,\partial\psi'/\partial\bar z_{s}=\partial(w_{i}^{a_{i}}\psi')/\partial\bar z_{s}$ extends smoothly, and $\bar\partial_{F}\psi$ is a smooth section of $S_{4}$; at a point with the two names $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ the two definitions agree, since $z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$. The fold has $z_{s}\circ\iota=-z_{s}$, so $\iota^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ and $\partial(\psi'\circ\iota)/\partial\bar z_{s}=-(\partial\psi'/\partial\bar z_{s})\circ\iota$; the two signs cancel, and $\bar\partial_{F}$ commutes with $\psi\mapsto\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$. A smooth function constant along the fibres passes through $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$. A smooth section $f$ of the twist on the fibre at $s$ with $\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$ is holomorphic in $z_{s}$ off the corners, and $w_{i}^{a_{i}}f$ is smooth, so its poles are bounded by $D$: it is a state at rest at $s$; and a state at rest is such a section. For (iv): with $P_{1}$ the origin and $P_{2},P_{3},P_{4}$ the half-periods, $\wp-e_{k}$ has a double zero at $P_{k+1}$ and a double pole at $P_{1}$ and no other zero or pole, and $\wp'$ has simple zeros at $P_{2},P_{3},P_{4}$ and a triple pole at $P_{1}$ and no other \cite[\S\S20.2, 20.32]{WhittakerWatson}; the five divisors are $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$, $2P_{2}+P_{4}$, $2P_{3}+P_{4}$ and $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$, and each less $3P_{4}$ is the divisor of $(\wp-e_{3})^{-1}$, of $(\wp-e_{1})(\wp-e_{3})^{-1}$, of $(\wp-e_{2})(\wp-e_{3})^{-1}$ and of $\wp'(\wp-e_{3})^{-2}$. If $h_{s}$ has the divisor $D-D'$, then $w_{i}^{a_{i}'}h_{s}f=(w_{i}^{a_{i}'-a_{i}}h_{s})\,(w_{i}^{a_{i}}f)$, the first factor smooth with no zero near $P_{i}$, so $f\mapsto h_{s}f$ carries the smooth sections for $D$ onto those for $D'$; $h_{s}$ is holomorphic in $z_{s}$ off the corners, so it passes through $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$; and $\wp-e_{k}$ is even and $\wp'$ odd \cite[\S20.21]{WhittakerWatson}, so that $h_{s}\circ\iota=\pm h_{s}$, with the sign $-1$ exactly when $\epsilon$ differs for $D$ and $D'$: $h_{s}$ carries the lift of the fold to the lift of the fold.
```

```tex
(v), and the first sentence of (vi). Let $\chi$ be a smooth function on $Y$ that the fold leaves unchanged, equal to $1$ near one corner circle and to $0$ near the other three. On the fibre, $\partial w_{i}/\partial z_{s}=1$ and $\partial\bar w_{i}/\partial z_{s}=0$; along time, at a fixed point of the fibre, $\partial_{s}z_{s}=-\kappa\bar z_{s}$, so $\partial_{s}w_{i}=-\kappa\bar w_{i}$ and $\partial_{s}\bar w_{i}=-\kappa w_{i}$. If $a_{4}$ is odd, so that $\epsilon=+1$, take $\chi$ at the corner circle of $P_{4}$ and $f=\chi\,\bar w_{4}\,w_{4}^{-a_{4}}$: it is a smooth section of the twist, and $f\circ\iota=f$ since $w_{4}\circ\iota=-w_{4}$, so it is a smooth state of form-degree zero. Near the corner circle, $w_{4}^{a_{4}}\,\partial f/\partial z_{s}=-a_{4}\,\bar w_{4}/w_{4}$, which has no limit on the circle; and $w_{4}^{a_{4}}\,\partial_{s}f=-\kappa w_{4}+a_{4}\kappa\,\bar w_{4}^{2}/w_{4}$, whose derivative along $w_{4}$ contains $-a_{4}\kappa\,\bar w_{4}^{2}/w_{4}^{2}$, which has no limit on the circle, so that it does not extend smoothly. If $a_{4}$ is even, $D=P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$ and $\epsilon=-1$; take $\chi$ at the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and $f=\chi\,w_{1}^{-1}$: it is a smooth section of the twist with $-f\circ\iota=f$, a smooth state. Near the corner circle, $w_{1}\,\partial f/\partial z_{s}=-1/w_{1}$, which is not bounded, and $w_{1}\,\partial_{s}f=\kappa\,\bar w_{1}/w_{1}$, which has no limit on the circle. In each case neither $\partial f/\partial z_{s}$ nor $\partial_{s}f$ is a smooth section. (vi), the rest. The function $c$ is smooth, has no zero and has the period $L$, so it is a function on $Y$ and on $\widetilde{M}$, and $c^{-1}$ is one too; multiplication by $c$ carries the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ onto themselves and covers the identity. It is constant along the fibres, along space and along the base, and the fold leaves it unchanged: it commutes with $\psi\mapsto\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$, with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ (part (ii)), with $\bar\partial_{F}$ (part (iii)) and with the maps $h_{s}$, and a state at rest at a time $s$ times the number $c(s)$ is a state at rest at $s$. Since $c(s-L)=c(s)$ and $c(-s)=c(s)$, $c\circ\tau=c$ and $c\circ R=c$, so that multiplication by $c$ commutes with the pull-backs by $\tau$ and by $R$. For an operation of differentiation along time $\nabla$, $c\,\nabla(c^{-1}\psi)=c\,(\partial_{s}c^{-1})\,\psi+\nabla\psi$, and $c\,\partial_{s}c^{-1}=-\partial_{s}\log c=(2\pi/L)\sin(2\pi s/L)$, which is not zero at $s=L/4$. This is (vi).
```

### M2-W182 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
What Proposition~\ref{prop:operations} does not say. It does not say that no operation of differentiation along time exists: it says that none is carried to itself by multiplication by $c$, so that what M1 and the entries of this paper give singles none out. The object that is missing along time is a rule for differentiating a smooth section of the twist along time; along the fibre, it is a rule for differentiating one in the direction $\partial/\partial z_{s}$. Whether a length on the twist, or a rule of either kind, is singled out by a condition that M1 does not state is not examined. M1 has no line with ``Hermitian'', ``connection'' or ``curvature''. It is not shown that the functions $h_{s}$ of part~(iv) depend smoothly on the time, and they are used on one fibre at a time. Nothing is said of the Clifford multiplication, of an operation made from the frame $\partial_{s},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$ rather than from the exterior derivative, or of an operation with values in a bundle other than forms. Along time there is no operation for $R$ with its lift $\Phi_{QC}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}) to act on, so that which lift of $R$ is meant is not decided here. An equation made from the operations of parts~(ii) and~(iii) alone holds at each time separately.
```

- **Step:** T2
- **Rests on:** M2-W176; M2-W170, M2-W174 (the lift of $R$; its first trip-wire); M1 v7.3 (searched) *(M2-W176 is replaced by M2-W185, P278; flagged: this entry is unchanged, and where it names M2-W176 or its parts, M2-W185 is meant)*

READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` searched for "Hermitian", "connection" and "curvature", any case: no line. **Trip-wires** (DAG §3 rule 12): the first trip-wire of M2-W174 (which lift of $R$) is closed for card T2, there being no operation along time, and returns only if a later card has one; the missing rule along time is not put on hold: card T3 writes its candidates from the operations of (ii) and (iii), and if cards T4a to T5 leave nothing, the missing rule comes to the PI as one numbered choice. RECALLED, entered nowhere and a premise of nothing: that a line on a flat torus has a length whose curvature is the same at every point, unique up to a constant. The PI's two musings on the last sentence are leads (Decision Log P276) and enter no paper. **Added after the fresh reader's check (P277):** the ground of the last sentence, by hand: $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ and $\bar\partial_{F}$ differentiate only in directions along space, the base and the fibre, which lie in the set of the points of one time, and an equation for a state is a condition at each point made from the state and its derivatives there (M2-W086); so the condition at the points of one time uses the state at that time alone. "A length on the twist" is a Hermitian metric on the line; "the Clifford multiplication" is that of the spinor bundle of the source (V-M2-10); neither is defined in the paper, and both are named only to say what is not examined

For a smooth function $u$ pulled back from $Y$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(u\psi)=u\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}(u\psi)=u\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi$. That the result is not in general of type $(0,*)$: for a smooth function $g$ on $S^{3}$ and a smooth section $f$ of the twist that is pulled back from $Y$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(gf)=f\,\mathrm{d}g$, and $\mathrm{d}g$ is of type $(0,1)$ for $\widetilde{J}$ only if $e_{1}g=0$ and $e_{2}g=ie_{3}g$, the $(0,1)$-forms of $J_{0}$ being spanned by $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$ and $e^{2}-ie^{3}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}(ii)). For a real $g$ that is not constant there is a point of $S^{3}$ at which $\mathrm{d}g$ is not zero; there $e_{2}g=ie_{3}g$ with both sides real gives $e_{2}g=e_{3}g=0$, and with $e_{1}g=0$ it would give $\mathrm{d}g=0$: so $\mathrm{d}g$ is not of type $(0,1)$ at that point, and $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(gf)$ is not of type $(0,*)$ at the points over it at which $f$ is not zero. This is (ii).

### M2-W184 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(v), and the first sentence of (vi). Let $\chi$ be a smooth function on $Y$ that the fold leaves unchanged, equal to $1$ near one corner circle and to $0$ outside a neighbourhood of that circle on which the function $w_{i}$ of that circle is defined. On the fibre, $\partial w_{i}/\partial z_{s}=1$ and $\partial\bar w_{i}/\partial z_{s}=0$; along time, at a fixed point of the fibre, $\partial_{s}z_{s}=-\kappa\bar z_{s}$, so $\partial_{s}w_{i}=-\kappa\bar w_{i}$ and $\partial_{s}\bar w_{i}=-\kappa w_{i}$. If $a_{4}$ is odd, so that $\epsilon=+1$, take $\chi$ at the corner circle of $P_{4}$ and $f=\chi\,\bar w_{4}\,w_{4}^{-a_{4}}$: it is a smooth section of the twist, and $f\circ\iota=f$ since $w_{4}\circ\iota=-w_{4}$, so it is a smooth state that is a function, a form of degree zero. Near the corner circle, $w_{4}^{a_{4}}\,\partial f/\partial z_{s}=-a_{4}\,\bar w_{4}/w_{4}$, which has no limit on the circle; and $w_{4}^{a_{4}}\,\partial_{s}f=-\kappa w_{4}+a_{4}\kappa\,\bar w_{4}^{2}/w_{4}$, whose derivative along $w_{4}$ contains $-a_{4}\kappa\,\bar w_{4}^{2}/w_{4}^{2}$, which has no limit on the circle, so that it does not extend smoothly. If $a_{4}$ is even, $D=P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$ and $\epsilon=-1$; take $\chi$ at the corner circle of $P_{1}$ and $f=\chi\,w_{1}^{-1}$: it is a smooth section of the twist with $-f\circ\iota=f$, a smooth state. Near the corner circle, $w_{1}\,\partial f/\partial z_{s}=-1/w_{1}$, which is not bounded, and $w_{1}\,\partial_{s}f=\kappa\,\bar w_{1}/w_{1}$, which has no limit on the circle. In each case neither $\partial f/\partial z_{s}$ nor $\partial_{s}f$ is a smooth section. The forms $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$, $e^{2}-ie^{3}$ and those of the base do not enter, $f$ being a function.
```

### M2-W185 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the operations of order one]\label{prop:operations}
Let $D$, $\epsilon$, $w_{i}$ and the smooth sections of the twist and of $S_{4}$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:smoothstate}. (i) The smooth sections of the twist, for $D$, are sections of a line on $\widetilde{M}$ that is pulled back from $Y$ and carries the lift of the fold $f\mapsto\epsilon\,f\circ\iota$; an isomorphism that covers the identity of $\widetilde{M}$ carries it to $N_{4}$ and its lift of the fold to $\lambda_{4}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:step}). In this sense the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ are sections of $S_{4}$. (ii) Space and the base. Let $\mathrm{d}=\mathrm{d}_{Y}+\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}+\mathrm{d}_{B}$ be the exterior derivative of $\widetilde{M}=Y\times S^{3}\times(\CPtwo\times\Stwo)$, written as the sum of its parts along the three factors. For a smooth section $\psi$ of $S_{4}$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi$ are forms on $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles, with complex coefficients and smooth there, and $w_{i}^{a_{i}}\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi$ and $w_{i}^{a_{i}}\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi$ extend smoothly across the points over the corner circle of $P_{i}$; they are not in general of type $(0,*)$. $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ commute with $\psi\mapsto\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$ and with multiplication by every smooth function pulled back from $Y$. (iii) The fibre. Every smooth section of $S_{4}$ is in exactly one way $\psi=\psi'+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''$, where $\psi'$ and $\psi''$ are at each point $(0,*)$-forms of time, of space and of the base (Proposition~\ref{prop:whole}(iv)). On the fibre over a time $s$ and a point $r$ of $S^{3}\times\CPtwo\times\Stwo$ these forms are one vector space, and with $z_{s}=a+ib$, $a$ and $b$ real, let $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}=\tfrac12(\partial/\partial a+i\,\partial/\partial b)$ act on $\psi'$ along that fibre. Then
\[
\bar\partial_{F}\psi\;=\;\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\frac{\partial\psi'}{\partial\bar z_{s}}
\]
is a smooth section of $S_{4}$; $\bar\partial_{F}$ commutes with $\psi\mapsto\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$, so that it carries a smooth state to a smooth section that the lift of the fold leaves unchanged, and it commutes with multiplication by every smooth function that is constant along the fibres. A smooth section $f$ of the twist on the fibre at $s$ has $\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$ exactly when it is a state at rest at the time $s$. (iv) For two of the five divisors $D$, $D'$ of flux three whose odd corner is $P_{4}$, and each time $s$, there is a function $h_{s}$ on the fibre at $s$, a product of integer powers of $\wp-e_{1}$, $\wp-e_{2}$, $\wp-e_{3}$ and $\wp'$ for the periods $z_{s}(1,0)$ and $z_{s}(0,1)$, such that $f\mapsto h_{s}f$ carries the smooth sections of the twist for $D$ on that fibre one-to-one onto those for $D'$, carries the lift of the fold to the lift of the fold, and commutes with $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$. (v) The map $\psi'\mapsto\partial\psi'/\partial z_{s}$, with $\partial/\partial z_{s}=\tfrac12(\partial/\partial a-i\,\partial/\partial b)$, does not carry the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ to smooth sections of $S_{4}$, nor the smooth states. (vi) Time. The map $\psi\mapsto\partial_{s}\psi$, taken on the coefficients of $\psi$ in the forms $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$, $e^{2}-ie^{3}$ and the forms of the base, does not carry the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ to smooth sections of $S_{4}$, nor the smooth states. Let $c(s)=\exp\bigl(\cos(2\pi s/L)\bigr)$. Multiplication by $c$ is a map of the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ onto themselves that covers the identity of $\widetilde{M}$; it commutes with the lift of the fold, with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ and $\bar\partial_{F}$, with the maps $h_{s}$ of (iv), with the pull-backs by the step $\tau$ and by $R$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iv)), and it carries the states at rest at each time to states at rest at that time. For every operation of differentiation along time $\nabla$,
\[
c\,\nabla\bigl(c^{-1}\psi\bigr)\;=\;\nabla\psi+\frac{2\pi}{L}\sin\Bigl(\frac{2\pi s}{L}\Bigr)\psi ,
\]
which is not $\nabla\psi$. No operation of differentiation along time is therefore carried to itself by every map of $S_{4}$ to itself that covers the identity and carries to itself the lift of the fold, the operations $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ and $\bar\partial_{F}$, the maps $h_{s}$, the pull-backs by $\tau$ and by $R$, and the states at rest at each time: in this sense what is given determines none along time. The operations $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ along space and along the base and $\bar\partial_{F}$ along the fibre differentiate once and are written from the product $\widetilde{M}$, the coordinates $z_{s}$ and the divisor $D$ alone; that every such map carries them to themselves is not shown, and is not claimed. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `supply 034`; in the place of M2-W176, which it replaces
- **Step:** T2 (a corrected wording after the fresh reader's check, P277; confirmed by the PI, P278)
- **Rests on:** M2-W175; M2-W177, M2-W183, M2-W179, M2-W184 and M2-W181 (its proof; M2-W183 and M2-W184 in place of M2-W178 and M2-W180, retired; flagged at P277: the statement is unchanged); M2-W039, M2-W041(ii), M2-W023, M2-W052, M2-W082, M2-W054(iv), M2-W062, M2-W048, M2-W069, M2-W167(iv), M2-W170; M1 v7.3, Definition 7.4, Proposition 5.8; Verified Reference V-M2-13

Confirmed by read-back (P276). **The sense of "determines"** is the wording the architect gave in the read-back in the place of its third assumption of P275, which as worded would have struck every operation: every map of $S_{4}$ to itself that covers the identity and carries each given thing to itself carries the operation to itself. It is the architect's judgement of what part (i) of the principle (`papers/M2/DAG.md` §1) asks, confirmed with the read-back and not a ruling on the principle. **Identification steps** (CLAUDE.md §5): the tenth choice (M2-W175); in (i), that the line of the smooth sections is M1's $\mathcal{O}(D)$ on the fibre at $s=0$; in (iv), the reading of the source's functions as functions on the fibre (the note to V-M2-13). **Changed from the read-back, told to the PI at the close:** the read-back's numbering of the points (1 to 5) is here (ii), (iii), (v), (vi), with (i) and (iv) set apart; the function $c$ is given by a formula, the read-back having "a function $c(s)$ with no zero and period $L$"; (v) says only that the map does not carry smooth sections to smooth sections, the read-back's sentence on a length of the twist being RECALLED and entered nowhere. ### M2-W186 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[candidates]\label{def:candidate}
Let $D$, the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ and the smooth states be as in Definition~\ref{def:smoothstate}, and for a smooth section $\psi$ of $S_{4}$ let $\psi'$ and $\psi''$ be its parts, $\psi=\psi'+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(iii)); $\psi'$ and $\psi''$ are smooth sections of $S_{4}$. A \emph{term of order zero} is one of $\psi$, $\psi'$, $\psi''$. A \emph{term of order one} is one of $X\psi$, $X\psi'$, $X\psi''$, for $X$ one of $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}$, $\bar\partial_{F}$. A \emph{candidate} is an equation for a state (Definition~\ref{def:family}) whose condition on a smooth state $\psi$ is that a sum of terms, each multiplied by a complex number, is zero at every point of $\widetilde{M}$ off the corner circles. It is \emph{of order zero} if the sum can be written with terms of order zero alone, and \emph{of order one} otherwise. Two candidates with the same smooth states as solutions are counted as one. That ``made from the operations'' is read in this way is a reading and is not derived: it is the eleventh choice of this paper. Clause~(b) of M1, Definition~3.2, is its ground: it brings in no product of a state with itself or with its conjugate, no function in front of a term, and no map that sorts a form by its type, none of which Proposition~\ref{prop:operations} has.
\end{definition}
```

- **Mark:** DECLARED (a reading and a numbered choice, the eleventh of M2; the reading is the architect's, put to the PI as assumption 6 of the chat with what it shuts out, where others would hear something else and its opposite, and confirmed, P280; the wording confirmed by read-back, P281). ### M2-W187 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the candidates of order zero and of order one]\label{prop:candidates}
Let $\psi$ be a smooth state, and let $\delta=\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$ act on $\psi'$ and on $\psi''$ along each fibre as in Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(iii). (i) The twelve terms span a space of dimension eleven, $\bar\partial_{F}\psi$ and $\bar\partial_{F}\psi'$ being the same. The sum of a candidate is, in exactly one way,
\[
\bigl[a_{0}\psi'+b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi'+c_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi'\bigr]+\bigl[a_{1}\psi''+b_{1}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''+c_{1}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''\bigr]+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\bigl[a_{2}\psi''+b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''+c_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''+g_{0}\,\delta\psi'+g_{1}\,\delta\psi''\bigr]
\]
with eleven complex numbers, and every choice of the eleven numbers is a candidate. (ii) A smooth state satisfies the candidate exactly when all four of these hold:
\begin{align*}
\text{(I)}\quad & a_{0}\psi'+b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi'+c_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi'=0;\\
\text{(II)}\quad & a_{1}\psi''+b_{1}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''+c_{1}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''=0;\\
\text{(III)}\quad & a_{2}\psi''+b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''+c_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''+g_{0}\,\delta\psi'=0;\\
\text{(IV)}\quad & g_{1}\,\delta\psi''=0.
\end{align*}
(iii) If $a_{0}\neq0$, (I) holds exactly when $\psi'=0$. If $a_{1}\neq0$, (II) holds exactly when $\psi''=0$. (iv) The candidates of order zero are exactly four: no condition; $\psi'=0$; $\psi''=0$; $\psi=0$. (v) The candidates of order one are the others. Each holds at each time separately. The list at order zero and one is not empty, and it is the one form of (i) with its eleven numbers; which of the numbers are free is not said here. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```

- **Rests on:** M2-W192 (in place of M2-W186, retired; flagged at P283: the statement is unchanged); M2-W188 and M2-W191 (its proof; M2-W191 in place of M2-W189, retired; flagged at P282: the statement is unchanged); M2-W185 (parts (ii), (iii)), M2-W179, M2-W183, M2-W184, M2-W182, M2-W175; M2-W054(iv), M2-W066 and M2-W062 (the forms as a tensor product); Minimality M2-11

```tex
(i) and (ii). The form $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ is pulled back from $Y$ and has degree one, so that $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi'')=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}(\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi'')=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''$; and $\bar\partial_{F}\psi=\bar\partial_{F}\psi'=\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\delta\psi'$, $\bar\partial_{F}\psi''=\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\delta\psi''$. With $\psi=\psi'+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''$, each of the twelve terms is therefore a sum, with the numbers $1$ and $-1$ in front, of the eleven forms $\psi'$, $\psi''$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi'$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi'$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''$, $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''$, $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''$, $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''$, $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\delta\psi'$ and $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\delta\psi''$, and each of the eleven is such a sum of terms: $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''=\psi-\psi'$, $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''=\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi'-\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi$, $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''=\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi'-\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi$, and the other eight are terms. So the sum of a candidate is the display of (i) for eleven numbers, and every choice of the eleven is a candidate. For (ii): the lift of the fold $\Lambda\colon\theta\mapsto\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\theta$ acts on the forms on $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles, and since $\iota^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ and $\iota^{*}$ carries a form with no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ to one with none, $\Lambda\psi=\psi$ gives $\Lambda\psi'=\psi'$ and $\Lambda\psi''=-\psi''$. $\Lambda$ commutes with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(ii)), and $\delta(u\circ\iota)=-(\delta u)\circ\iota$, so that $\Lambda\delta\psi'=-\delta\psi'$ and $\Lambda\delta\psi''=\delta\psi''$. A form is in exactly one way $\theta_{0}+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\theta_{1}$ with no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ in $\theta_{0}$ and $\theta_{1}$, where it has no $\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ and no $\mathrm{d}s$ other than in $\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$, as the forms of the display have; so the display is zero exactly when its first two brackets together are zero and its third bracket is zero. If a form $\theta$ is zero then $\Lambda\theta$ is zero, and so are $\tfrac12(\theta+\Lambda\theta)$ and $\tfrac12(\theta-\Lambda\theta)$: of the first two brackets these are the first and the second, which gives (I) and (II); of the third bracket they are $g_{1}\,\delta\psi''$ and the rest, which gives (IV) and (III). Conversely (I) to (IV) give that the display is zero. That the eleven numbers are determined: let $u$ and $v$ be smooth sections of the twist, pulled back from $Y$, with $\epsilon\,u\circ\iota=u$ and $\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=-v$, and with $\delta u$ and $\delta v$ not zero at some point at which $u$ and $v$ are not zero; let $g$ be a smooth real function on $S^{3}$ and $k$ one on $\CPtwo\times\Stwo$, with $g$, $k$, $\mathrm{d}g$ and $\mathrm{d}k$ not zero at some point. Then $gku$ and $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge gkv$ are smooth states. If the display is zero for both, then for the first (I) is $a_{0}\,gku+b_{0}\,ku\,\mathrm{d}g+c_{0}\,gu\,\mathrm{d}k=0$, three forms of the degrees $(0,0)$, $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$ on time and space and on the base, each not zero at some point, so $a_{0}=b_{0}=c_{0}=0$, and (III) is $g_{0}\,gk\,\delta u=0$, so $g_{0}=0$; for the second, (II) and (III) give in the same way $a_{1}=b_{1}=c_{1}=0$ and $a_{2}=b_{2}=c_{2}=0$, and (IV) is $g_{1}\,gk\,\delta v=0$, so $g_{1}=0$. This is (i) and (ii).
```

(iii), (iv) and (v). The exterior derivative along a factor of a product raises the degree on that factor by one and leaves the other degrees as they are: $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}u_{p,q}$ has the degrees $(p+1,q)$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}u_{p,q}$ the degrees $(p,q+1)$. The part of (I) of the degrees $(p,q)$ is therefore $a_{0}u_{p,q}+b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}u_{p-1,q}+c_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}u_{p,q-1}=0$, a piece with a negative index being zero. Let $a_{0}\neq0$. If $u_{p',q'}=0$ whenever $p'+q'<p+q$, this gives $u_{p,q}=0$; so every piece of $\psi'$ is zero, by induction on $p+q$, and $\psi'=0$. Conversely $\psi'=0$ satisfies (I). The same holds of (II), $\psi''$ and $a_{1}$. This is (iii). For (iv): a sum of terms of order zero is the display of (i) with $b_{0},b_{1},b_{2},c_{0},c_{1},c_{2},g_{0},g_{1}$ zero, so that (I) is $a_{0}\psi'=0$, (II) is $a_{1}\psi''=0$, (III) is $a_{2}\psi''=0$ and (IV) is no condition. The conditions on a smooth state are: none, if $a_{0}=a_{1}=a_{2}=0$; $\psi'=0$, if $a_{0}\neq0$ and $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$; $\psi''=0$, if $a_{0}=0$ and one of $a_{1},a_{2}$ is not zero; $\psi'=0$ and $\psi''=0$, which is $\psi=0$, otherwise. Since no map that sorts a form by its type is used, a condition with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ or $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ is a condition on the whole of that derivative. Nothing is said of candidates of order two or more.

### M2-W192 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[candidates]\label{def:candidate}
Let $D$, the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ and the smooth states be as in Definition~\ref{def:smoothstate}, and for a smooth section $\psi$ of $S_{4}$ let $\psi'$ and $\psi''$ be its parts, $\psi=\psi'+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(iii)); $\psi'$ and $\psi''$ are smooth sections of $S_{4}$. A \emph{term of order zero} is one of $\psi$, $\psi'$, $\psi''$. A \emph{term of order one} is one of $X\psi$, $X\psi'$, $X\psi''$, for $X$ one of $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}$, $\bar\partial_{F}$. A \emph{candidate} is an equation for a state (Definition~\ref{def:family}) whose condition on a smooth state $\psi$ is that a sum of terms, each multiplied by a complex number, is zero at every point of $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles. Two candidates with the same smooth states as solutions are counted as one. A candidate is \emph{of order zero} if it is counted as one with a candidate whose sum is written with terms of order zero alone, and \emph{of order one} otherwise. That ``made from the operations'' is read in this way is a reading and is not derived: it is the eleventh choice of this paper. Clause~(b) of M1, Definition~3.2, is its ground: it brings in no product of a state with itself or with its conjugate, no function in front of a term, and no map that sorts a form by its type, none of which Proposition~\ref{prop:operations} has.
\end{definition}
```

### M2-W193 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
Let $f$ be a map of $\widetilde{M}$ and $\Phi$ a map of the smooth sections of $S_{4}$, for $D$, onto themselves that covers $f$ and commutes with the lift of the fold. A candidate is \emph{carried to itself by $f$ with $\Phi$} if, for every smooth state $\psi$, $\psi$ is a solution exactly when $\Phi\psi$ is.
```
- **Place:** `select 060`; after M2-W190; the first entry of card T4a
- **Step:** T4a
- **Rests on:** M2-W192 (`def:candidate`: "Two candidates with the same smooth states as solutions are counted as one"); M2-W175 (the smooth sections of $S_{4}$, the lift of the fold, a smooth state); M2-W086 (a solution); the principle of `papers/M2/DAG.md` §1, part (ii) (Decision Log P262)
- **Mark:** DECLARED (a reading; it is the architect's, put to the PI as assumption 5 of chat C11 in these words, with its ground, where others would hear something else and its opposite, and confirmed, P285). **Ground:** M2-W192 as logged ("Two candidates with the same smooth states as solutions are counted as one."): a candidate is told apart only by its smooth solutions, so that its image under a map is itself exactly when the image has the same ones. **Why it is not a condition on the sum of the candidate:** the lift $\Phi_{QC}$ of M2-W170 is a map of $S_{4}$, the forms of type $(0,*)$, and the sum of a candidate with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ or $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ is "not in general of type $(0,*)$" (M2-W185(ii)). **Told to the PI before confirming:** a mathematician would expect the operator to commute with the map, which is stronger; the opposite that the words allow is that the written sum is unchanged, number by number. "Covers $f$": the value of $\Phi\psi$ at $f(m)$ is made from the value of $\psi$ at $m$, as the maps of M2-W069 and M2-W170 carry the fibre over $m$ to the fibre over $f(m)$. Since $\Phi$ commutes with the lift of the fold it carries smooth states to smooth states (hand: if the lift leaves $\psi$ unchanged it leaves $\Phi\psi$ unchanged). No numbered choice is made in this entry. Not shown and not claimed: that the reading is the only one that part (ii) of the principle allows

### M2-W194 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[kept and struck]\label{def:kept}
Let $R$ be the map of Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iv), and write $\theta=\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$ and $\eta=e^{2}-ie^{3}$ for the $(0,1)$-forms of $J_{0}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}(ii). A form on $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles that has no $\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ and no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ is in exactly one way $\alpha+\theta\wedge\beta$ with no $\mathrm{d}s$ in $\alpha$ and in $\beta$; let $A$ carry it to $\alpha-\theta\wedge\beta$. Let $\rho$ carry such a form to the form with the same $\mathrm{d}s$, $e^{1}$, $e^{2}$, $e^{3}$ and forms of the base, each coefficient $F$ being replaced by $F\circ R^{-1}$. For a smooth section $\psi=\psi'+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''$ of $S_{4}$ let
\[
\Phi_{R}\psi\;=\;A\rho\psi'-i\,\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge A\rho\psi'' .
\]
It is the lift $\Phi_{QC}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}(iii) with the map $\nu_{R}$ taken to be $f\mapsto f\circ R^{-1}$. That the lift of $R$ by which a candidate is tested is this one, with no function in front, is a reading and is not derived: it is the twelfth choice of this paper. Clause~(b) of M1, Definition~3.2, is its ground: it brings in no function that Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse} does not have. A candidate is \emph{kept} if it is carried to itself by the fold with its lift and by $R$ with $\Phi_{R}$, and \emph{struck} otherwise, by the map that does not carry it to itself.
\end{definition}
```

### M2-W195 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the candidates that the maps keep]\label{prop:kept}
Let the candidates be those of Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}, with their conditions (I) to (IV), and write $\mathrm{I}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{II}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{III}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{IV}(\psi)$ for their left sides. (i) The lift of the fold leaves every smooth state unchanged, and the fold with its lift carries every candidate to itself. (ii) Let $D$ be one of the three divisors $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$, $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$, for which $a_{2}=a_{3}$. Then $\Phi_{R}$ is a map of the smooth sections of $S_{4}$, for $D$, onto themselves that covers $R$ and commutes with the lift of the fold; applied twice it is $\epsilon$ times the lift of the fold. For a smooth state $\psi$ with $\psi'=\alpha'+\theta\wedge\beta'$ and $\psi''=\alpha''+\theta\wedge\beta''$,
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{I}(\Phi_{R}\psi)&=A\rho\,\mathrm{I}(\psi)+2i\,b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta', &
\mathrm{II}(\Phi_{R}\psi)&=-i\bigl[A\rho\,\mathrm{II}(\psi)+2i\,b_{1}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta''\bigr],\\
\mathrm{III}(\Phi_{R}\psi)&=-i\bigl[A\rho\,\mathrm{III}(\psi)+2i\,b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta''\bigr], &
\mathrm{IV}(\Phi_{R}\psi)&=-A\rho\,\mathrm{IV}(\psi).
\end{align*}
(iii) Let $u$ be a smooth section of $S_{4}$ with no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, with pieces $u_{1,q}=\theta\wedge F_{q}+\eta\wedge G_{q}$, and let $v$ be a form with no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, smooth on $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles, that is at each point a $(0,*)$-form of time, of space and of the base. If $a\,u+b\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}u+c\,\mathrm{d}_{B}u+v=0$ for complex numbers $a,b,c$ with $b\neq0$, then $F_{q}=0$ for every $q$, and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta=0$ for the $\beta$ of $u=\alpha+\theta\wedge\beta$. (iv) For $D$ as in (ii), $R$ with $\Phi_{R}$ carries every candidate to itself: every candidate is kept, and none is struck. (v) Let $D$ be $2P_{2}+P_{4}$ or $2P_{3}+P_{4}$, and $D^{\sigma}$ the other of the two. The same formula $\Phi_{R}$ carries the smooth states for $D$ one-to-one onto the smooth states for $D^{\sigma}$, and the solutions of a candidate for $D$ onto the solutions of the candidate with the same eleven numbers for $D^{\sigma}$. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `select 080`; after M2-W194
- **Step:** T4a
- **Rests on:** M2-W193, M2-W194; M2-W196, M2-W197, M2-W198 (its proof); M2-W187 (the candidates and their conditions (I) to (IV)), M2-W191 (the pieces $u_{p,q}$), M2-W192; M2-W185(ii), (iii), M2-W183, M2-W179, M2-W175; M2-W167(iv), M2-W170(ii), (iii); M2-W053 (the frame); Minimality M2-12

### M2-W196 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i) and (ii). A smooth state is a smooth section of $S_{4}$ that the lift of the fold leaves unchanged (Definition~\ref{def:smoothstate}); the image of a smooth state $\psi$ under that lift is $\psi$, so that $\psi$ is a solution exactly when its image is. This is (i). For (ii): the map $R(p,s,r)=(Sp,-s,r)$ moves the point of $Y$ alone, so that $e_{a}(F\circ R^{-1})=(e_{a}F)\circ R^{-1}$ for $a=1,2,3$, and likewise for the derivatives along the base; and $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\mathrm{d}s=0$, $\mathrm{d}e^{1}=-2e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$, $\mathrm{d}e^{2}=-2e^{3}\wedge e^{1}$, $\mathrm{d}e^{3}=-2e^{1}\wedge e^{2}$, with coefficients that are constants. So $\rho$ commutes with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ and with $\mathrm{d}_{B}$. For $u=\alpha+\theta\wedge\beta$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}u=\mathrm{d}_{B}\alpha-\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}_{B}\beta$ with no $\mathrm{d}s$ in $\mathrm{d}_{B}\alpha$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}\beta$, so $A$ commutes with $\mathrm{d}_{B}$; and $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}u=\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\alpha+\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\theta\wedge\beta-\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\beta$ with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\theta=-i\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}$ and no $\mathrm{d}s$ in $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\alpha$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\beta$ and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta$, so that
\[
\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}Au-A\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}u\;=\;2i\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta .
\]
$A$ does not touch the coefficients and commutes with $\delta$; and since $z_{-s}\circ R=-i\,z_{s}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iv)), $\delta(F\circ R^{-1})=-i\,(\delta F)\circ R^{-1}$: $\delta\rho=-i\,\rho\delta$. On the forms of type $(0,*)$ with no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ the map $A\rho$ is $B\circ(R^{-1})^{*}$ with the coefficients carried by $f\mapsto f\circ R^{-1}$: both keep products, $(R^{-1})^{*}\theta=-(\mathrm{d}s+ie^{1})$, which $B$ carries to $-\theta$, and both leave $\eta$ and the forms of the base as they are; and $(R^{-1})^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=-i\,\mathrm{d}\bar z_{-s}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}(ii)). So $\Phi_{R}$ is $\Phi_{QC}$ with $\nu_{R}$ as named, and it covers $R$. Let $SP_{i}=P_{\sigma(i)}$ modulo $\Z^{2}$; $\sigma$ leaves $1$ and $4$ in place and exchanges $2$ and $3$. Then $w_{\sigma(i)}\circ R=-i\,w_{i}$, and $w_{\sigma(i)}^{a_{\sigma(i)}}\,(F\circ R^{-1})=\bigl((-i\,w_{i})^{a_{\sigma(i)}}F\bigr)\circ R^{-1}$, which extends smoothly across the points over the corner circle of $P_{\sigma(i)}$ when $a_{\sigma(i)}=a_{i}$ and $w_{i}^{a_{i}}F$ extends smoothly: for $a_{2}=a_{3}$, $\Phi_{R}$ carries the smooth sections of $S_{4}$, for $D$, to smooth sections of $S_{4}$, for $D$. $R$ commutes with the fold, so $\rho$ commutes with $\iota^{*}$; $A$ commutes with $\iota^{*}$, which is the identity on the forms of time, of space and of the base; and $\iota^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ on both sides: $\Phi_{R}$ commutes with the lift of the fold. $A$ applied twice is the identity, $\rho$ applied twice replaces $F$ by $F\circ\iota$, $R$ applied twice being the fold, and $(-i)^{2}=-1$: $\Phi_{R}\Phi_{R}\psi=\psi'\circ\iota-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''\circ\iota=\iota^{*}\psi$, which is $\epsilon$ times the image of $\psi$ under the lift of the fold; so $\Phi_{R}$ is one-to-one and onto. The parts of $\Phi_{R}\psi$ are $A\rho\psi'$ and $-i\,A\rho\psi''$, and $A\rho\psi'=\rho\alpha'-\theta\wedge\rho\beta'$. Hence $\mathrm{I}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=a_{0}A\rho\psi'+b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}A\rho\psi'+c_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}A\rho\psi'=A\rho\,\mathrm{I}(\psi)+b_{0}(\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}A-A\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma})\rho\psi'=A\rho\,\mathrm{I}(\psi)+2i\,b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta'$; likewise for $\mathrm{II}$ with $-i\,A\rho\psi''$; $\mathrm{III}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=-i\bigl[A\rho(a_{2}\psi''+b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''+c_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi'')+2i\,b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta''\bigr]+g_{0}\,\delta A\rho\psi'$ with $\delta A\rho\psi'=-i\,A\rho\,\delta\psi'$; and $\mathrm{IV}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=g_{1}\,\delta(-i\,A\rho\psi'')=-g_{1}A\rho\,\delta\psi''$. This is (ii).
```
- **Place:** `select 090`; after M2-W195; the first step of its proof
- **Step:** T4a
- **Rests on:** M2-W175 (a smooth state; $w_{i}$; the lift of the fold), M2-W194 ($A$, $\rho$, $\Phi_{R}$), M2-W167(iv) ($R$; $z_{-s}\circ R=-i\,z_{s}$; $R$ applied twice is the fold; $R$ commutes with the fold; the corner circles under $R$), M2-W170(ii), (iii) and M2-W172 ($(R^{-1})^{*}$ on the forms; $B$), M2-W171 ($S$ on the corners), M2-W053 (the frame $e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$ made by left translation from $i,j,k$), M2-W179 and M2-W188 ($\iota^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$), M2-W187 (the conditions)
- **Mark:** CHECKED: `T4a_kept.py` A2 (the brackets $[e_{1},e_{2}]=2e_{3}$ and cyclic), A3 (the derivative along space applied twice is zero with $\mathrm{d}e^{1}=-2e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$ and cyclic, and is not with the other sign), B1, B2 (the displayed formula, on polynomial coefficients), E1 ($\delta$ of $F(iz)$), E2, F1 ($S$ on the corners), and `M2_L15_kept.lean` (`L15_brackets`, `L15_minus_i_sq`, `L15_S_corners`); the rest by hand, in full in the statement. Hand steps, in full: $\mathrm{d}e^{c}(e_{a},e_{b})=-e^{c}([e_{a},e_{b}])$ for the forms dual to a frame, which with the brackets gives the three formulas (taken as standard, a weak step; the script checks the sign by the derivative applied twice); $R^{-1}$ carries the fibre at the time $-s$ to the fibre at $s$ with $z_{s}\circ R^{-1}=i\,z_{-s}$ (M2-W172), so that in the coordinate $F\circ R^{-1}$ is $F(iz)$ and the chain rule gives the factor $\overline{i}=-i$; $w_{\sigma(i)}(R(p,s))=z_{-s}(Sp-P_{\sigma(i)})=z_{-s}(S(p-P_{i}))=-i\,z_{s}(p-P_{i})$, the small representative of $p-P_{i}$ being carried by $S$ to that of $Sp-P_{\sigma(i)}$; $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta'=\rho(\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta')$, $\rho$ keeping products and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}$ having constant coefficients; that the forms of the base have coefficients that are functions on the base, which $R$ leaves as they are; the ε of the lift of the fold is the same number before and after, $a_{4}$ being unchanged. **Identification step:** that $f\mapsto f\circ R^{-1}$ is a map $\nu_{R}$ of M2-W170(i) (the mark of M2-W194). The script checks the formulas on forms of time and of space with polynomial coefficients on $S^{3}$; the forms of the base and the twist enter by hand. The step brings in no reading beyond those of M2-W193 and M2-W194

### M2-W197 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii). The pieces of $u$ of the degree two on time and space are $u_{2,q}=\theta\wedge\eta\wedge H_{q}$, and those of $v$ are $\theta\wedge\eta\wedge V_{q}$, the $(0,2)$-forms of time and of space being the multiples of $\theta\wedge\eta$. Since $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ raises the degree on time and space by one and $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ that on the base by one, the part of the equation of the degrees $(2,q)$ is
\[
a\,\theta\wedge\eta\wedge H_{q}+b\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(\theta\wedge F_{q}+\eta\wedge G_{q})+c\,\theta\wedge\eta\wedge\mathrm{d}_{B}H_{q-1}+\theta\wedge\eta\wedge V_{q}=0 .
\]
Now $\theta\wedge\eta=\mathrm{d}s\wedge\eta-ie^{1}\wedge\eta$ has no $e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$; the part of $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(\theta\wedge F_{q}+\eta\wedge G_{q})$ that has $\mathrm{d}s$ is $-\mathrm{d}s\wedge\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}F_{q}$, and its coefficient of $e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$ is $2iF_{q}-i(e_{2}-ie_{3})G_{q}$. The part of the equation that has $\mathrm{d}s$ gives $b\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}F_{q}=\eta\wedge(aH_{q}+c\,\mathrm{d}_{B}H_{q-1}+V_{q})$, so that $e_{1}F_{q}=0$ and $e_{3}F_{q}=-i\,e_{2}F_{q}$, which is $(e_{2}-ie_{3})F_{q}=0$; and its coefficient of $e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$ gives $2F_{q}=(e_{2}-ie_{3})G_{q}$, $b$ being not zero. Fix a point of $Y$ that is on no corner circle and a point of the base, and let $f$ and $g$ be the coefficients of $F_{q}$ and $G_{q}$ along one of a basis of the $(0,q)$-forms of the base there: they are smooth functions on $S^{3}$. The fields $e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$ are $q\mapsto qi,qj,qk$ on the unit quaternions, and each is the derivative of a family of rotations of $S^{3}$, which keep its measure; so the integral over $S^{3}$ of $e_{a}h$ is zero for every smooth function $h$, and that of $h_{1}\,e_{a}h_{2}$ is minus that of $(e_{a}h_{1})\,h_{2}$. Since $(e_{2}+ie_{3})(e_{2}-ie_{3})=e_{2}^{2}+e_{3}^{2}-2i\,e_{1}$, the function $f$ has $(e_{2}^{2}+e_{3}^{2})f=0$, and the integral of $\bar f\,(e_{2}^{2}+e_{3}^{2})f$, which is zero, is minus the integral of $|e_{2}f|^{2}+|e_{3}f|^{2}$: so $e_{2}f=e_{3}f=0$, and with $e_{1}f=0$ the function $f$ is constant on $S^{3}$. The integral of $2f=(e_{2}-ie_{3})g$ over $S^{3}$ is then $2f$ times the measure of $S^{3}$ on the left and zero on the right: $f=0$. So $F_{q}=0$ for every $q$. The part $\beta$ of $u$ is the sum of the $F_{q}$ and of the $\eta\wedge H_{q}$, and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\eta=-2e^{2}\wedge e^{3}\wedge(e^{2}-ie^{3})=0$, so $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta=0$. This is (iii).
```
- **Place:** `select 100`; after M2-W196; the second step of the proof
- **Step:** T4a
- **Rests on:** M2-W191 (the pieces; the degrees under $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}$), M2-W170(ii) ($\theta$ and $\eta$ span the $(0,1)$-forms of $J_{0}$), M2-W053 (the frame), M2-W196 (the formulas for $\mathrm{d}e^{a}$), M2-W175
- **Mark:** CHECKED: `T4a_kept.py` A1 (the matrices of the three fields are antisymmetric, with trace zero), C1 (the part with $\mathrm{d}s$), C2 (the coefficient of $e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$), C3, C4, D1 (the product of the two operators), D2 and D3 (the two integrals, on monomials: a model of the statement and not the statement), and `M2_L15_kept.lean` (`L15_antisym`, `L15_trace`); the rest by hand, in full in the statement. Hand steps, in full: $\mathrm{d}_{B}(\theta\wedge\eta\wedge H)=\theta\wedge\eta\wedge\mathrm{d}_{B}H$, $\theta\wedge\eta$ having degree two and no part along the base; $\mathrm{d}_{B}H_{q-1}$ is a form of degree $q$ on the base and need not be of type $(0,q)$: the equation $b\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}F_{q}=\eta\wedge(\dots)$ is read along a basis of all the forms of degree $q$ of the base that contains a basis of the $(0,q)$-forms, and along each of these $b\,(e_{1}f\,e^{1}+e_{2}f\,e^{2}+e_{3}f\,e^{3})$ is a multiple of $e^{2}-ie^{3}$ (C4); a field $q\mapsto qu$ with $u=i,j,k$ is the derivative at $t=0$ of $q\mapsto q\exp(tu)$, a rotation of $\R^{4}$ since $|q\exp(tu)|=|q|$, and for a rotation $r_{t}$ the integral of $h\circ r_{t}$ equals that of $h$, whose derivative at $t=0$ is the integral of $e_{a}h$; applied to $h=h_{1}h_{2}$ it gives the second statement; $e_{a}\bar f=\overline{e_{a}f}$, the fields being real. **Weak steps, taken as standard and read at no source** (told to the PI): that a rotation keeps the measure of the sphere; that a smooth function on $S^{3}$, which is connected, with $e_{1}f=e_{2}f=e_{3}f=0$ is constant, the three fields being a frame at every point (M2-W053). The statement is about every $u$ that satisfies the equation, and it does not say that one exists. The step brings in no reading

```tex
(iv) and (v). Let $D$ be as in (ii) and $\psi$ a smooth state that is a solution of a candidate, so that $\mathrm{I}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{II}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{III}(\psi)$ and $\mathrm{IV}(\psi)$ are zero (Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}(ii)). If $b_{0}\neq0$, (iii) with $u=\psi'$, $v=0$ and the numbers $a_{0},b_{0},c_{0}$ gives $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta'=0$, and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta'=\rho(\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta')=0$; so $b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta'=0$ whether $b_{0}$ is zero or not, and $\mathrm{I}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=0$ by (ii). In the same way (iii) with $u=\psi''$, $v=0$ and $a_{1},b_{1},c_{1}$ gives $\mathrm{II}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=0$, and (iii) with $u=\psi''$, $v=g_{0}\,\delta\psi'$ and $a_{2},b_{2},c_{2}$ gives $\mathrm{III}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=0$: $\delta\psi'$ is at each point a $(0,*)$-form of time, of space and of the base, $\delta$ acting on the coefficients. And $\mathrm{IV}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=-A\rho\,\mathrm{IV}(\psi)=0$. So $\Phi_{R}\psi$, which is a smooth state by (ii), is a solution. Conversely, if $\Phi_{R}\psi$ is a solution then so is $\Phi_{R}\Phi_{R}\psi$, which is $\epsilon\psi$ by (ii), $\psi$ being a smooth state; and the conditions being linear, $\psi$ is a solution. So $R$ with $\Phi_{R}$ carries every candidate to itself, and with (i) every candidate is kept. This is (iv). For (v): $D^{\sigma}$ has the numbers $a_{\sigma(i)}$ in the place of $a_{i}$ and the same $a_{4}$, hence the same $\epsilon$; by the computation of (ii), $w_{\sigma(i)}^{a_{i}}(F\circ R^{-1})$ extends smoothly when $w_{i}^{a_{i}}F$ does, so that $\Phi_{R}$ carries the smooth sections for $D$ to those for $D^{\sigma}$, and those for $D^{\sigma}$ to those for $D$; it commutes with the lifts of the fold, and applied twice it is $\iota^{*}$. The formulas of (ii) and the statement (iii) hold as they stand, no step of their proofs using $a_{2}=a_{3}$ other than that for the smooth sections. So $\Phi_{R}$ carries the smooth states for $D$ one-to-one onto those for $D^{\sigma}$, and a solution of a candidate for $D$ to a solution of the candidate with the same eleven numbers for $D^{\sigma}$; applied again it carries that solution to $\epsilon$ times the first. This is (v).
```

### M2-W200 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
Let $f$ be a map of $\widetilde{M}$ and $\Phi$ a map of the smooth sections of $S_{4}$, for $D$, onto themselves that commutes with the lift of the fold and \emph{covers} $f$: for every point $m$ over no corner circle, the value of $\Phi\psi$ at $f(m)$ is the image of the value of $\psi$ at $m$ under a linear one-to-one map that does not depend on $\psi$. A candidate is \emph{carried to itself by $f$ with $\Phi$} if, for every smooth state $\psi$, $\psi$ is a solution exactly when $\Phi\psi$ is.
```

### M2-W201 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[kept by the maps]\label{def:kept}
Of the maps of Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}, the fold and $R$ are tested. The maps $\tau$ and $\tau$ applied twice have no lift from $N_{4}$ to itself that carries $\lambda_{4}$ to $\lambda_{4}$ or to $-\lambda_{4}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(ii)), $\tau$ applied three times is the identity, and a shift of the time by $c$ keeps the lengths only if $c=0$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iii)): these are not tested. Write $\theta=\mathrm{d}s-ie^{1}$ and $\eta=e^{2}-ie^{3}$ for the $(0,1)$-forms of $J_{0}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}(ii). A form on $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles that has no $\mathrm{d}z_{s}$ and no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ is in exactly one way $\alpha+\theta\wedge\beta$ with no $\mathrm{d}s$ in $\alpha$ and in $\beta$; let $A$ carry it to $\alpha-\theta\wedge\beta$. Let $\rho$ carry such a form to the form with the same $\mathrm{d}s$, $e^{1}$, $e^{2}$, $e^{3}$ and forms of the base, each coefficient $F$ being replaced by $F\circ R^{-1}$. For a smooth section $\psi=\psi'+\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''$ of $S_{4}$ let
\[
\Phi_{R}\psi\;=\;A\rho\psi'-i\,\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge A\rho\psi'' .
\]
On the forms it is the map $B\circ(R^{-1})^{*}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}(iii), and on the smooth sections of the twist it is $f\mapsto f\circ R^{-1}$, which stands in the place of the map $\nu_{R}$ of that proposition; it is not followed by multiplication by a function. That $f\mapsto f\circ R^{-1}$ is a map $\nu_{R}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}(i) is not shown. That the lift of $R$ by which a candidate is tested is $\Phi_{R}$ is a reading and is not derived: it is the twelfth choice of this paper. Clause~(b) of M1, Definition~3.2, is its ground: it brings in no function that Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse} does not have. Let $D$ be a divisor whose numbers at $P_{2}$ and at $P_{3}$ are equal, so that $\Phi_{R}$ carries the smooth sections of $S_{4}$, for $D$, onto themselves (Proposition~\ref{prop:kept}(ii)). A candidate is \emph{kept by the maps} if it is carried to itself by the fold with its lift and by $R$ with $\Phi_{R}$, and it is \emph{struck by} one of these two maps if that map does not carry it to itself.
\end{definition}
```

### M2-W202 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the candidates that the maps keep]\label{prop:kept}
Let the candidates be those of Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}, with their conditions (I) to (IV), and write $\mathrm{I}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{II}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{III}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{IV}(\psi)$ for their left sides. (i) The lift of the fold leaves every smooth state unchanged, and the fold with its lift carries every candidate to itself. (ii) Let $D$ be one of the three divisors $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$, $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$, whose numbers at $P_{2}$ and at $P_{3}$ are equal. Then $\Phi_{R}$ is a map of the smooth sections of $S_{4}$, for $D$, onto themselves that covers $R$ and commutes with the lift of the fold; applied twice it is $\epsilon$ times the lift of the fold. For a smooth state $\psi$ with $\psi'=\alpha'+\theta\wedge\beta'$ and $\psi''=\alpha''+\theta\wedge\beta''$,
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{I}(\Phi_{R}\psi)&=A\rho\,\mathrm{I}(\psi)+2i\,b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta', &
\mathrm{II}(\Phi_{R}\psi)&=-i\bigl[A\rho\,\mathrm{II}(\psi)+2i\,b_{1}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta''\bigr],\\
\mathrm{III}(\Phi_{R}\psi)&=-i\bigl[A\rho\,\mathrm{III}(\psi)+2i\,b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta''\bigr], &
\mathrm{IV}(\Phi_{R}\psi)&=-A\rho\,\mathrm{IV}(\psi).
\end{align*}
(iii) Let $u$ be a smooth section of $S_{4}$ with no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, with pieces $u_{1,q}=\theta\wedge F_{q}+\eta\wedge G_{q}$, where $F_{q}$ and $G_{q}$ are at each point $(0,q)$-forms of the base, and let $v$ be a form with no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, smooth on $\widetilde{M}$ less the points over the corner circles, that is at each point a $(0,*)$-form of time, of space and of the base. If $t_{0}\,u+t_{1}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}u+t_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}u+v=0$ for complex numbers $t_{0},t_{1},t_{2}$ with $t_{1}\neq0$, then $F_{q}=0$ for every $q$, and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta=0$ for the $\beta$ of $u=\alpha+\theta\wedge\beta$. (iv) For $D$ as in (ii), $R$ with $\Phi_{R}$ carries every candidate to itself: every candidate is kept by the maps, and none is struck by the fold or by $R$. (v) Let $D$ be $2P_{2}+P_{4}$ or $2P_{3}+P_{4}$, and $D^{\sigma}$ the other of the two. The same formula $\Phi_{R}$ carries the smooth states for $D$ one-to-one onto the smooth states for $D^{\sigma}$, and the solutions of a candidate for $D$ onto the solutions of the candidate with the same eleven numbers for $D^{\sigma}$. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```

### M2-W203 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i) and (ii). A smooth state is a smooth section of $S_{4}$ that the lift of the fold leaves unchanged (Definition~\ref{def:smoothstate}). The lift of the fold is a map of the smooth sections of $S_{4}$ onto themselves, being the identity when applied twice; it covers the fold and commutes with itself; and the image of a smooth state $\psi$ under it is $\psi$, so that $\psi$ is a solution exactly when its image is. This is (i). For (ii): the map $R(p,s,r)=(Sp,-s,r)$ moves the point of $Y$ alone, so that $e_{a}(F\circ R^{-1})=(e_{a}F)\circ R^{-1}$ for $a=1,2,3$, and likewise for the derivatives along the base. $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\mathrm{d}s=0$, $\mathrm{d}s$ being pulled back from $Y$; and the fields that left translation makes from $i,j,k$ have $[e_{1},e_{2}]=2e_{3}$, $[e_{2},e_{3}]=2e_{1}$, $[e_{3},e_{1}]=2e_{2}$, so that $\mathrm{d}e^{1}=-2e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$, $\mathrm{d}e^{2}=-2e^{3}\wedge e^{1}$, $\mathrm{d}e^{3}=-2e^{1}\wedge e^{2}$, with coefficients that are constants. So $\rho$ commutes with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ and with $\mathrm{d}_{B}$. The map $A$ keeps products: it is the map that keeps products, is the identity on functions, on $e^{1},e^{2},e^{3}$ and on the forms of the base, and carries $\theta$ to $-\theta$, since $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are made from these without $\theta$. For $u=\alpha+\theta\wedge\beta$, $\mathrm{d}_{B}u=\mathrm{d}_{B}\alpha-\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}_{B}\beta$ with no $\mathrm{d}s$ in $\mathrm{d}_{B}\alpha$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}\beta$, so $A$ commutes with $\mathrm{d}_{B}$; and $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}u=\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\alpha+\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\theta\wedge\beta-\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\beta$ with $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\theta=-i\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}$ and no $\mathrm{d}s$ in $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\alpha$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\beta$ and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ adding only forms of $S^{3}$, so that
\[
\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}Au-A\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}u\;=\;2i\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta .
\]
$A$ does not touch the coefficients and commutes with $\delta$; and since $z_{-s}\circ R=-i\,z_{s}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:maps}(iv)), $\delta(F\circ R^{-1})=-i\,(\delta F)\circ R^{-1}$: $\delta\rho=-i\,\rho\delta$. On the forms of type $(0,*)$ with no $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ the map $A\rho$ is $B\circ(R^{-1})^{*}$ with the coefficients carried by $f\mapsto f\circ R^{-1}$: both keep products, $(R^{-1})^{*}\theta=-(\mathrm{d}s+ie^{1})$, which $B$ carries to $-\theta$, and both leave $\eta$ and the forms of the base as they are; and $(R^{-1})^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=-i\,\mathrm{d}\bar z_{-s}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}(ii)). So the value of $\Phi_{R}\psi$ at $R(m)$ is the image of the value of $\psi$ at $m$ under $B\circ(R^{-1})^{*}$, which is linear and one-to-one (Proposition~\ref{prop:reverse}(iii)): $\Phi_{R}$ covers $R$. Let $SP_{i}=P_{\sigma(i)}$ modulo $\Z^{2}$; $\sigma$ leaves $1$ and $4$ in place and exchanges $2$ and $3$. Then $w_{\sigma(i)}\circ R=-i\,w_{i}$, and $w_{\sigma(i)}^{n}\,(F\circ R^{-1})=\bigl((-i\,w_{i})^{n}F\bigr)\circ R^{-1}$ for every whole number $n$; with $n$ the number of $D$ at $P_{i}$ this extends smoothly across the points over the corner circle of $P_{\sigma(i)}$ when $w_{i}^{n}F$ extends smoothly across those over that of $P_{i}$. When the numbers of $D$ at $P_{2}$ and at $P_{3}$ are equal, $n$ is also the number of $D$ at $P_{\sigma(i)}$: the coefficients of $\Phi_{R}\psi$ then meet the conditions of Definition~\ref{def:smoothstate} at the four corner circles, its forms are of type $(0,*)$ for $\widetilde{J}$ with coefficients smooth off the corner circles, and $\Phi_{R}$ carries the smooth sections of $S_{4}$, for $D$, to smooth sections of $S_{4}$, for $D$. $R$ commutes with the fold, so $\rho$ commutes with $\iota^{*}$; $A$ commutes with $\iota^{*}$, which is the identity on the forms of time, of space and of the base; and $\iota^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ on both sides: $\Phi_{R}$ commutes with the lift of the fold. $A$ applied twice is the identity, $\rho$ applied twice replaces $F$ by $F\circ\iota$, $R$ applied twice being the fold, and $(-i)^{2}=-1$: $\Phi_{R}\Phi_{R}\psi=\psi'\circ\iota-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\psi''\circ\iota=\iota^{*}\psi$, which is $\epsilon$ times the image of $\psi$ under the lift of the fold; so $\Phi_{R}$ is one-to-one and onto. The parts of $\Phi_{R}\psi$ are $A\rho\psi'$ and $-i\,A\rho\psi''$, and $A\rho\psi'=\rho\alpha'-\theta\wedge\rho\beta'$. Hence $\mathrm{I}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=a_{0}A\rho\psi'+b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}A\rho\psi'+c_{0}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}A\rho\psi'=A\rho\,\mathrm{I}(\psi)+b_{0}(\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}A-A\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma})\rho\psi'=A\rho\,\mathrm{I}(\psi)+2i\,b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta'$; likewise for $\mathrm{II}$ with $-i\,A\rho\psi''$; $\mathrm{III}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=-i\bigl[A\rho(a_{2}\psi''+b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''+c_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi'')+2i\,b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta''\bigr]+g_{0}\,\delta A\rho\psi'$ with $\delta A\rho\psi'=-i\,A\rho\,\delta\psi'$; and $\mathrm{IV}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=g_{1}\,\delta(-i\,A\rho\psi'')=-g_{1}A\rho\,\delta\psi''$. This is (ii).
```
- **Place:** `select 090`; in the place of M2-W196, which it replaces; the first step of the proof
- **Step:** T4a

The computation is unchanged.** CHECKED: `T4a_kept.py` A2 (the brackets, on monomials of degree at most three), A3 (the derivative along space applied twice is zero with $\mathrm{d}e^{1}=-2e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$ and cyclic, and is not with the other sign), B1, B2 (the displayed formula, on polynomial coefficients), E1 ($\delta$ of $F(iz)$), E2, F1 ($S$ on the corners), and `M2_L15_kept.lean` (`L15_brackets`, `L15_minus_i_sq`, `L15_S_corners`); the rest by hand, in full in the statement. Hand steps, in full: the brackets: for the fields $x\mapsto xu$, $x\mapsto xv$ on the quaternions the bracket is $x\mapsto x(uv-vu)$, and $ij-ji=2k$ and cyclic (the certificate has the matrices, in the order $m_{b}m_{a}-m_{a}m_{b}$; the script has the brackets as operators on monomials); $\mathrm{d}e^{c}(e_{a},e_{b})=-e^{c}([e_{a},e_{b}])$ for the forms dual to a frame, which with the brackets gives the three formulas (**taken as standard, a weak step, read at no source**; the script checks the sign by the derivative applied twice); $R^{-1}$ carries the fibre at the time $-s$ to the fibre at $s$ with $z_{s}\circ R^{-1}=i\,z_{-s}$ (M2-W172), so that in the coordinate $F\circ R^{-1}$ is $F(iz)$ and the chain rule gives the factor $\overline{i}=-i$; $w_{\sigma(i)}(R(p,s))=z_{-s}(Sp-P_{\sigma(i)})=z_{-s}(S(p-P_{i}))=-i\,z_{s}(p-P_{i})$, the small representative of $p-P_{i}$ being carried by $S$ to that of $Sp-P_{\sigma(i)}$; $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta'=\rho(\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta')$, $\rho$ keeping products and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}$ having constant coefficients; the forms of the base have coefficients that are functions on the base, which $R$ leaves as they are; the $\epsilon$ of the lift of the fold is the same before and after, the number of $D$ at $P_{4}$ being unchanged; the lift of the fold covers the fold in the sense of M2-W200, the value of $\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$ at $\iota(m)$ being $\epsilon$ times the pull-back of the value at $m$. The script checks the formulas on forms of time and of space with polynomial coefficients on $S^{3}$; the forms of the base and the twist enter by hand. In the statement $a_{0},b_{0},c_{0},a_{2},b_{2},c_{2},g_{0},g_{1}$ are the numbers of the candidate (M2-W187), and the numbers of the divisor are named in words. ### M2-W204 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii). The pieces of $u$ of the degree two on time and space are $u_{2,q}=\theta\wedge\eta\wedge H_{q}$, and those of $v$ are $\theta\wedge\eta\wedge V_{q}$, where $H_{q}$ and $V_{q}$ are at each point $(0,q)$-forms of the base, the $(0,2)$-forms of time and of space being the multiples of $\theta\wedge\eta$. Since $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}$ raises the degree on time and space by one and $\mathrm{d}_{B}$ that on the base by one, the part of the equation of the degrees $(2,q)$ is
\[
t_{0}\,\theta\wedge\eta\wedge H_{q}+t_{1}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(\theta\wedge F_{q}+\eta\wedge G_{q})+t_{2}\,\theta\wedge\eta\wedge\mathrm{d}_{B}H_{q-1}+\theta\wedge\eta\wedge V_{q}=0 .
\]
Now $\theta\wedge\eta=\mathrm{d}s\wedge\eta-ie^{1}\wedge\eta$ has no $e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$; the part of $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(\theta\wedge F_{q}+\eta\wedge G_{q})$ that has $\mathrm{d}s$ is $-\mathrm{d}s\wedge\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}F_{q}$, and its coefficient of $e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$ is $2iF_{q}-i(e_{2}-ie_{3})G_{q}$. The part of the equation that has $\mathrm{d}s$ gives $t_{1}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}F_{q}=\eta\wedge(t_{0}H_{q}+t_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}H_{q-1}+V_{q})$, so that, $t_{1}$ being not zero, $e_{1}F_{q}=0$ and $e_{3}F_{q}=-i\,e_{2}F_{q}$, which is $(e_{2}-ie_{3})F_{q}=0$; and its coefficient of $e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$ gives $2F_{q}=(e_{2}-ie_{3})G_{q}$. Fix a point of $Y$ that is on no corner circle and a point of the base, and let $\varphi$ and $\gamma$ be the coefficients of $F_{q}$ and $G_{q}$ along one of a basis of the $(0,q)$-forms of the base there: they are smooth functions on $S^{3}$. The fields $e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}$ are $x\mapsto xi,xj,xk$ on the unit quaternions $x$, and each is the derivative of a family of rotations of $S^{3}$, which keep its measure; so the integral over $S^{3}$ of $e_{a}h$ is zero for every smooth function $h$, and that of $h_{1}\,e_{a}h_{2}$ is minus that of $(e_{a}h_{1})\,h_{2}$. By the brackets of (ii), $(e_{2}+ie_{3})(e_{2}-ie_{3})=e_{2}^{2}+e_{3}^{2}-2i\,e_{1}$, so that $(e_{2}^{2}+e_{3}^{2})\varphi=0$, and the integral of $\bar\varphi\,(e_{2}^{2}+e_{3}^{2})\varphi$, which is zero, is minus the integral of $|e_{2}\varphi|^{2}+|e_{3}\varphi|^{2}$: so $e_{2}\varphi=e_{3}\varphi=0$, and with $e_{1}\varphi=0$ the function $\varphi$ is constant on $S^{3}$. The integral of $2\varphi=(e_{2}-ie_{3})\gamma$ over $S^{3}$ is then $2\varphi$ times the measure of $S^{3}$ on the left and zero on the right: $\varphi=0$. So $F_{q}=0$ for every $q$. The part $\beta$ of $u$ is the sum of the $F_{q}$ and of the $\eta\wedge H_{q}$, and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\eta=-2e^{2}\wedge e^{3}\wedge(e^{2}-ie^{3})=0$, so $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta=0$. This is (iii).
```

The computation is unchanged.** CHECKED: `T4a_kept.py` A1 (the matrices of the three fields are antisymmetric, with trace zero), C1 (the part with $\mathrm{d}s$), C2 (the coefficient of $e^{2}\wedge e^{3}$), C3 and C4 (for $\theta\wedge\eta$ and $\mathrm{d}s\wedge\eta$ themselves), D1 (the product of the two operators, on monomials of degree at most three), and `M2_L15_kept.lean` (`L15_antisym`, `L15_trace`); the rest by hand, in full in the statement. **The checks D2 and D3 of the script, on the two integrals, carry nothing:** they are on monomials only, and they use a formula for the mean of a monomial over $S^{3}$ that is RECALLED and was read at no source (fresh reader; the header of the script says so). Hand steps, in full: $\mathrm{d}_{B}(\theta\wedge\eta\wedge H)=\theta\wedge\eta\wedge\mathrm{d}_{B}H$, $\theta\wedge\eta$ having degree two and no part along the base; $\mathrm{d}_{B}H_{q-1}$ is a form of degree $q$ on the base and need not be of type $(0,q)$: the equation $t_{1}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}F_{q}=\eta\wedge(\dots)$ is read along a basis of all the forms of degree $q$ of the base that contains a basis of the $(0,q)$-forms, and along each of these $t_{1}\,(e_{1}\varphi\,e^{1}+e_{2}\varphi\,e^{2}+e_{3}\varphi\,e^{3})$ is a multiple of $e^{2}-ie^{3}$, which has the coefficients $0,1,-i$ (C4); a field $x\mapsto xu$ with $u=i,j,k$ is the derivative at $t=0$ of $x\mapsto x\exp(tu)$, a rotation of $\R^{4}$ since $|x\exp(tu)|=|x|$, and for a rotation $r_{t}$ the integral of $h\circ r_{t}$ equals that of $h$, whose derivative at $t=0$ is the integral of $e_{a}h$; applied to $h=h_{1}h_{2}$ it gives the second statement; $e_{a}\bar\varphi=\overline{e_{a}\varphi}$, the fields being real; $(e_{2}+ie_{3})(e_{2}-ie_{3})=e_{2}^{2}+e_{3}^{2}-i[e_{2},e_{3}]$. **Weak steps, taken as standard and read at no source** (told to the PI): that a rotation keeps the measure of the sphere; that a smooth function on $S^{3}$, which is connected, with $e_{1}\varphi=e_{2}\varphi=e_{3}\varphi=0$ is constant, the three fields being a frame at every point (M2-W053). The second derivation, made blind, is named in the mark of M2-W202. The statement is about every $u$ and $v$ that satisfy the equation, and it does not say that such exist. "The integral over $S^{3}$" is that of the measure of the round sphere, which the paper does not define; it is used in this step alone. The step brings in no reading

### M2-W205 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iv) and (v). Let $D$ be as in (ii) and $\psi$ a smooth state that is a solution of a candidate, so that $\mathrm{I}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{II}(\psi)$, $\mathrm{III}(\psi)$ and $\mathrm{IV}(\psi)$ are zero (Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}(ii)). If $b_{0}\neq0$, (iii) with $u=\psi'$, $v=0$ and the numbers $a_{0},b_{0},c_{0}$ of the candidate for $t_{0},t_{1},t_{2}$ gives $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta'=0$, and $\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta'=\rho(\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\beta')=0$; so $b_{0}\,\mathrm{d}e^{1}\wedge\rho\beta'=0$ whether $b_{0}$ is zero or not, and $\mathrm{I}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=0$ by (ii). In the same way (iii) with $u=\psi''$, $v=0$ and $a_{1},b_{1},c_{1}$ gives $\mathrm{II}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=0$, and (iii) with $u=\psi''$, $v=g_{0}\,\delta\psi'$ and the numbers $a_{2},b_{2},c_{2}$ of the candidate gives $\mathrm{III}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=0$: $\delta\psi'$ is at each point a $(0,*)$-form of time, of space and of the base, $\delta$ acting on the coefficients. And $\mathrm{IV}(\Phi_{R}\psi)=-A\rho\,\mathrm{IV}(\psi)=0$. So $\Phi_{R}\psi$, which is a smooth state by (ii), is a solution. Conversely, if $\Phi_{R}\psi$ is a solution then so is $\Phi_{R}\Phi_{R}\psi$, which is $\epsilon\psi$ by (ii), $\psi$ being a smooth state; and the conditions being linear, $\psi$ is a solution. So $R$ with $\Phi_{R}$ carries every candidate to itself, and with (i) every candidate is kept by the maps. This is (iv). For (v): $D^{\sigma}$ has at $P_{\sigma(i)}$ the number that $D$ has at $P_{i}$, and the same number at $P_{4}$, hence the same $\epsilon$; by the computation of (ii), $\Phi_{R}$ carries the smooth sections for $D$ to those for $D^{\sigma}$, and those for $D^{\sigma}$ to those for $D$; it commutes with the lifts of the fold, and applied twice it is $\iota^{*}$. The formulas of (ii) and the statement (iii) hold as they stand, no step of their proofs using that the numbers of $D$ at $P_{2}$ and at $P_{3}$ are equal other than that for the smooth sections. So $\Phi_{R}$ carries the smooth states for $D$ one-to-one onto those for $D^{\sigma}$, and a solution of a candidate for $D$ to a solution of the candidate with the same eleven numbers for $D^{\sigma}$; the same begun at $D^{\sigma}$ carries a solution for $D^{\sigma}$ to a solution for $D$, and $\Phi_{R}$ applied twice is $\epsilon$ on a smooth state, so that every solution for $D^{\sigma}$ is the image of one for $D$. This is (v).
```

```tex
How the candidates are tested by Definition~\ref{def:A5eq}. The candidates are tested for the three divisors $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$ and $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:kept}(ii), for which every candidate is kept by the maps (Proposition~\ref{prop:kept}(iv)); they are not tested for $2P_{2}+P_{4}$ and $2P_{3}+P_{4}$, for which no candidate is said to be kept or struck. Clause~(i) of Definition~\ref{def:A5eq} is read as it is printed: a candidate meets it if there are two smooth states $\psi_{1}$, $\psi_{2}$ that are solutions, with $c_{1}\psi_{1}+c_{2}\psi_{2}=0$ for complex numbers $c_{1}$, $c_{2}$ only when $c_{1}=c_{2}=0$, and whose shadows are the same set. It is not asked that the two differ only in the internal piece: a solution and its product with a function of time or of space may be the two. A candidate meets clause~(ii) if there is a smooth state that is a solution and whose shadow after one step is not the same set as its shadow. A candidate that does not meet clause~(i) is \emph{struck by clause~(i)}, and one that does not meet clause~(ii) is \emph{struck by clause~(ii)}. That clause~(i) is read as it is printed, and not with the words of M1, Definition~2.1(iii), ``states that differ only in the internal piece'', is a reading and is not derived: it is the thirteenth choice of this paper. Its ground is the words of Definition~\ref{def:A5eq} as they stand, and not a clause of M1, Definition~3.2.
```

### M2-W208 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the candidates and the fifth axiom]\label{prop:fifth}
Let $D$ be one of the three divisors $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$, $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$, let the candidates be those of Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}, with their eleven numbers and their conditions (I) to (IV), and let them be tested as said above. A \emph{function of the time alone} is a function $g(p,s,r)=g_{0}(s)$ on $\widetilde{M}$ with $g_{0}$ smooth on $\R$ and of period $3L$. (i) If a smooth state $\psi$ is a solution of a candidate and $g$ is a function of the time alone, then $g\psi$ is a smooth state and a solution of the same candidate. (ii) If $a_{0}\neq0$ and one of $a_{1}$, $a_{2}$ is not zero, the only smooth state that is a solution is zero. (iii) Let $I$ be an open arc of the time circle of length less than $L$. There are a smooth section $f$ of the twist with $\epsilon\,f\circ\iota=f$ and a smooth section $v$ of the twist with $\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=-v$, both pulled back from $Y$, both with $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$ zero on every fibre, both zero at the times outside $I$ and not zero at some point of the fibre at each time of $I$. The function $f$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{0}=0$; $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$. The shadow of each is the set of the points $(s,x)$ with $s$ in $I$. (iv) A candidate with $a_{0}\neq0$ and one of $a_{1}$, $a_{2}$ not zero is struck by clause~(i) and by clause~(ii); it is counted as one with the candidate $\psi=0$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}(iv). Every other candidate, one with $a_{0}=0$ or with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$, meets the fifth axiom. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `select 140`; after M2-W207
- **Step:** T4b
- **Rests on:** M2-W207 (how the candidates are tested; the thirteenth choice); M2-W209, M2-W210, M2-W211 (its proof); M2-W165, M2-W163, M2-W164; M2-W187 (the candidates; (I) to (IV); part (iii)), M2-W191, M2-W192; M2-W185(ii), (iii), M2-W179, M2-W183, M2-W175; M2-W069 (the coordinates $z_{s}$); M1 v7.3, Proposition 4.4 (the corners by position); Minimality M2-13

### M2-W209 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i) and (ii). A function $g$ of the time alone is well defined on $\widetilde{M}$, the two names $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ of a point of $Y$ having times that differ by $3L$; it is smooth, it is pulled back from $Y$, it is constant along each fibre, and the fold, which leaves the time as it is, leaves it unchanged. For a smooth section $\psi$ of $S_{4}$, $g\psi$ is of type $(0,*)$, $w_{i}^{a_{i}}g\psi=g\,w_{i}^{a_{i}}\psi$ extends smoothly, and $\epsilon\,\iota^{*}(g\psi)=g\,\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$: so $g\psi$ is a smooth section of $S_{4}$, and it is a smooth state if $\psi$ is one. Its parts are $g\psi'$ and $g\psi''$. Since $g$ is pulled back from $Y$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(g\psi')=g\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi'$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}(g\psi')=g\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi'$, and likewise for $\psi''$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(ii)); since $g$ is constant along the fibres, $\delta(g\psi')=g\,\delta\psi'$ and $\delta(g\psi'')=g\,\delta\psi''$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(iii)). So $\mathrm{I}(g\psi)=g\,\mathrm{I}(\psi)$, and likewise for $\mathrm{II}$, $\mathrm{III}$ and $\mathrm{IV}$: if (I) to (IV) hold for $\psi$ they hold for $g\psi$. This is (i). For (ii): let $a_{0}\neq0$ and let $\psi$ be a smooth state that is a solution. By Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}(iii), $\psi'=0$. If $a_{1}\neq0$, then $\psi''=0$ by the same part. If $a_{1}=0$ and $a_{2}\neq0$: with $\psi'=0$ the condition (III) is $a_{2}\psi''+b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''+c_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''=0$, which is the condition (II) with the numbers $a_{2},b_{2},c_{2}$ in the place of $a_{1},b_{1},c_{1}$; the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}(iii) uses of (II) its form and that its first number is not zero, and it gives $\psi''=0$. In each case $\psi=0$. This is (ii).
```

### M2-W210 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii), the functions. At a time $s$ write $\omega=e^{-\kappa s}$ and $T=e^{\kappa s}$. Then $z_{s}(0,1)=\omega$ and $z_{s}(1,0)=iT$ (Definition~\ref{def:step}): the fibre at $s$ is the complex numbers modulo $\Z\omega+\Z iT$, and $z_{s}$ carries $P_{1},P_{2},P_{3},P_{4}$ to $0$, $iT/2$, $\omega/2$ and $c=(\omega+iT)/2$. Let
\[
F(z)\;=\;\sum_{n\in\Z}\frac{\pi}{\omega\,\sin\bigl(\pi(z-inT)/\omega\bigr)} .
\]
Since $|\sin(x+iy)|\geq\sinh|y|$ for real $x$ and $y$, the series, and the series of the derivatives of its terms in $z$ and in $s$ of every order, converge uniformly on every compact set of pairs $(z,s)$ on which no term has a pole: $F$ is smooth in $(z,s)$ and holomorphic in $z$ away from its poles. Replacing $n$ by $-n$ gives $F(-z)=-F(z)$; $\sin(u+\pi)=-\sin u$ gives $F(z+\omega)=-F(z)$; and replacing $n$ by $n+1$ gives $F(z+iT)=F(z)$. The poles of $F$ are the points $m\omega+inT$, $m$ and $n$ whole numbers, and near $0$, $F(z)=z^{-1}G(z)$ with $G$ smooth in $(z,s)$ and $G(0)=1$, the function $u/\sin u$ being smooth near $u=0$ with the value $1$ there and the terms with $n\neq0$ being smooth near $0$. At no time is $F$ zero at every point, since it has a pole; nor is its derivative $F'$ in $z$, $F$ being not constant. $F(z-\omega/2)$ and $F(z-c)$ are even in $z$: $F(-z-\omega/2)=-F(z+\omega/2)=F(z-\omega/2)$, and $F(-z-c)=-F(z+c)=-F(z-c+\omega+iT)=F(z-c)$; so $F'(z-c)$ is odd. Let
\[
K_{1}(z)=F(z)\,F(z-\omega/2),\qquad K_{2}(z)=F(z)\,F(z-c),\qquad K_{3}(z)=F(z-c)\,F'(z-c).
\]
Each is unchanged by $z\mapsto z+\omega$, two signs cancelling, and by $z\mapsto z+iT$, so that it is a function on the fibre; each is odd; each is holomorphic in $z$ away from the corners and smooth in $(z,s)$ there; and at no time is it zero at every point. $K_{1}$ has poles at $P_{1}$ and $P_{3}$ alone, of order at most one; $K_{2}$ at $P_{1}$ and $P_{4}$ alone, of order at most one; $K_{3}$ at $P_{4}$ alone, of order at most three: with $u=z-c$, $u^{3}F(u)F'(u)=G(u)\bigl(u\,G'(u)-G(u)\bigr)$. In each case the function times the power of $w_{i}$ of that order extends smoothly in $(p,s)$ across the corner circle. For $D=3P_{4}$, where $\epsilon=+1$, let $h'=1$ and $h''=K_{3}$; for $D=2P_{1}+P_{4}$, where $\epsilon=+1$, let $h'=1$ and $h''=K_{2}$; for $D=P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$, where $\epsilon=-1$, let $h'=K_{1}$ and $h''=1$. The fold being $z\mapsto-z$ on each fibre, in each case $\epsilon\,h'\circ\iota=h'$ and $\epsilon\,h''\circ\iota=-h''$, and the poles of $h'$ and of $h''$ are bounded by $D$.
```
- **Place:** `select 160`; after M2-W209; the second step of the proof
- **Step:** T4b
- **Rests on:** M2-W069 ($z_{s}=e^{-\kappa s}y+ie^{\kappa s}x$), M2-W175 ($w_{i}$; $\epsilon$; the fold on a fibre), M2-W179 ($z_{s}\circ\iota=-z_{s}$); M1 v7.3, Proposition 4.4 (`prop:threecycle`, l.434: $P_{1}=(0,0)$, $P_{2}=(\tfrac12,0)$, $P_{3}=(0,\tfrac12)$, $P_{4}=(\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$)
- **Mark:** CHECKED: `T4b_fifth.py` B1 (the three rules for $F$ and $zF(z)\to1$), B2 (parity, the two periods, the orders of the poles, not zero at sample points), B3 (the table of the three divisors), B4 ($\partial/\partial\bar z$ zero at sample points), **all numerical, at four times and a few points, with the series cut at $|n|\leq40$: they are checks and not proofs**; `M2_L16_fifth.lean`, `L16_table` (the parities and the bounds on the poles, taken as data); the rest by hand, in full in the statement. READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.434 (the corners). Hand steps, in full: $|\sin(x+iy)|^{2}=\sin^{2}x+\sinh^{2}y$; for $|n|T>|\mathrm{Im}\,z|$ the $n$-th term is at most $\pi/(\omega\sinh(\pi(|n|T-|\mathrm{Im}\,z|)/\omega))$, which falls off like $e^{-\pi|n|T/\omega}$; $w_{1}=z$, $w_{3}=z-\omega/2$ and $w_{4}=z-c$ near their corners, with $z=z_{s}(p)$; near $P_{3}$, $F(z-\omega/2)=w_{3}^{-1}G(w_{3})$ and $F(z)$ is smooth, $\omega/2$ being no pole of $F$; a product of two meromorphic functions neither of which is zero at every point is not zero at every point; $F'(u)=u^{-1}G'(u)-u^{-2}G(u)$; under $z\mapsto z+\omega$: $K_{1}$ and $K_{2}$ are products of two factors that each change sign, and $K_{3}$ of $F(z-c)$ and its derivative, which both change sign; $-c$ and $c$ differ by $\omega+iT$, and $F(z+\omega+iT)=-F(z)$. **Weak steps, taken as standard and read at no source:** that a series of smooth functions which converges uniformly on compact sets together with the series of its derivatives of every order has a smooth sum, differentiated term by term; that the derivatives of the terms in $s$ are bounded as the terms are (not written out). No external source is used: the function $\wp$ and its dependence on the periods (the mark of M2-W206) do not enter. The letters $\omega$, $T$, $c$, $F$, $G$, $K_{1}$, $K_{2}$, $K_{3}$, $h'$, $h''$ are the architect's; $T$ is also M1's name for the piece that carries time, and $c$ is the function of M2-W185(vi) in M2-W211, where the half-period is not used. The step brings in no reading
