```tex
(iii), the states, and (iv). Name the times of $I$ by the numbers of an interval $(s_{1},s_{2})$ with $s_{2}-s_{1}<L$, and let $\beta_{0}$ be a smooth function on $\R$ of period $3L$ that is not zero exactly at the times of $I$. With $h'$ and $h''$ as above, let $f(p,s)=\beta_{0}(s)\,h'(z_{s}(p))$ and $v(p,s)=\beta_{0}(s)\,h''(z_{s}(p))$ at the times of $I$, the functions $h'$ and $h''$ being taken for the time $s$, and let $f$ and $v$ be zero at the other times. They are functions on $Y$ less the corner circles, smooth there, and $w_{i}^{a_{i}}f$ and $w_{i}^{a_{i}}v$ extend smoothly across the corner circles, the poles being bounded by $D$: $f$ and $v$ are smooth sections of the twist, pulled back from $Y$. On every fibre $\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$ and $\partial v/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$; $\epsilon\,f\circ\iota=f$ and $\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=-v$; and at each time of $I$ neither is zero at every point of the fibre. So $f$, a form of degree zero, is a smooth state with the parts $\psi'=f$ and $\psi''=0$; since $f$ is pulled back from $Y$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}f=0$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}f=0$, and $\delta f=0$, so that $\mathrm{I}(f)=a_{0}f$ and $\mathrm{II}(f)$, $\mathrm{III}(f)$, $\mathrm{IV}(f)$ are zero: $f$ is a solution of every candidate with $a_{0}=0$. The form $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$ is a smooth section of $S_{4}$, and $\epsilon\,\iota^{*}(\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v)=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$: it is a smooth state with the parts $\psi'=0$ and $\psi''=v$, for which $\mathrm{I}$ is zero, $\mathrm{II}$ is $a_{1}v$, $\mathrm{III}$ is $a_{2}v$ and $\mathrm{IV}$ is $g_{1}\,\delta v=0$: it is a solution of every candidate with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$. Neither depends on the point of space or of the base, and $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ is zero at no point; so each is not zero at some point over $(s,x)$ exactly when $s$ is in $I$, and its shadow is the set of the points $(s,x)$ with $s$ in $I$. This is (iii). For (iv): let a candidate have $a_{0}=0$ or $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$, and let $\psi$ be its solution of (iii). The function $c(s)=\exp\bigl(\cos(2\pi s/L)\bigr)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(vi) is a function of the time alone with no zero, so that $c\psi$ is a solution by (i), with the same shadow as $\psi$. If $c_{1}\psi+c_{2}\,c\psi=0$, then $c_{1}+c_{2}\,c(s)=0$ at every time of $I$; $c$ takes two different values at two times of $I$, so $c_{1}=c_{2}=0$. The candidate meets clause~(i). The shadow of $\psi$ after one step is the set of the points $(s-L,x)$ with $s$ in $I$; $I$ has length less than $L$ and the time circle the length $3L$, so that $I$ moved by $-L$ has no time in common with $I$, and $I$ is not empty: the two sets are not the same, and the candidate meets clause~(ii). It meets the fifth axiom. Let a candidate have $a_{0}\neq0$ and one of $a_{1}$, $a_{2}$ not zero. By (ii) its one smooth solution is zero. Two solutions $\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}=0$ have $1\cdot\psi_{1}+0\cdot\psi_{2}=0$: the candidate is struck by clause~(i). The shadow of zero is the empty set, and so is its shadow after one step: the candidate is struck by clause~(ii). Its smooth solutions are those of the candidate $\psi=0$, with which it is counted as one (Definition~\ref{def:candidate}). This is (iv).
```

### M2-W214 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{definition}[the first axiom for an equation]\label{def:A1eq}
An equation for a state (Definition~\ref{def:family}) \emph{meets the first axiom} if it has a solution, other than zero, whose shadow is compact. The shadow of a smooth state is an open set of time and space, and time and space, the product of the time circle and $S^{3}$, are connected; so a shadow that is compact and not empty is the whole of time and space, and for smooth states the equation meets the first axiom exactly when it has a solution that is not zero at some point over each point $(s,x)$ of time and space. That the first axiom asks this of the equation is a choice and is not derived: it is the fourteenth choice of this paper. It is M1, Definition~2.3, which asks that time and space be compact, asked of the shadow of a solution, ``compact'' being how M1 reads ``close'' in the proof of Proposition~5.2. Clause~(b) of M1, Definition~3.2, is the ground of taking the shadow: it brings in no object that this paper does not have.
\end{definition}
```
- **Place:** `conditions 110`; after M2-W166; the first entry of card S08
- **Step:** S08 (inside T4b)
- **Rests on:** M2-W086 (`def:family`), M2-W163 (the shadow of a state), M2-W175 (a smooth state), M2-W052 (the unfolded geometry; time and space); M1 v7.3, Definition 2.3 (`def:A1`, l.178), the proof of Proposition 5.2 (l.512), Definition 3.2 clause (b) (l.275); the PI's rulings on card S08 (Decision Log P256, P262, P290); Minimality M2-14
- **Mark:** DECLARED: a reading and a numbered choice, the fourteenth of M2 (Minimality M2-14). The PI ruled that the first axiom asks "That something the equation brings be closed, as l.512 asks of the unit groups of the algebra" (P256, option 2 of P223), that which thing is "left to the list" (P262), and, the list being in, that it is the shadow of a solution ("A confirmed", P290); the print words confirmed by read-back (P291). That it is numbered the fourteenth is presentation. READ: `canon/M1/Paper_M1_Six_Axioms_v7_3.tex` l.178 ("A geometry satisfies A1 if $T$, $\Sigma$ and $F$ are compact, connected and without boundary, and the fundamental group of $M$ is non-trivial."), l.512 ("A1 requires every factor to close, and this is read as asking that the unit group of $\R\oplus\R u$ be compact for every $u\neq0$ of $\mathcal{A}$ with $u^{*}=-u$."), l.275 (clause (b)). CHECKED by hand, in full here: the set of the points of $\widetilde{M}$ over no corner circle at which a smooth state is not zero is open in $\widetilde{M}$, the points over the corner circles being a closed set and the state being continuous off them; the map of $\widetilde{M}$ to time and space, $(p,s,x,b)\mapsto(s,x)$, carries open sets to open sets; so the shadow is open. A set that is compact in time and space is closed there; a set that is open, closed and not empty in a connected space is the whole space. **Weak steps, taken as standard and read at no source:** that the projection of a bundle carries open sets to open sets; that a compact set of a Hausdorff space is closed; that the product of a circle and $S^{3}$ is connected. **The alternatives** (Minimality M2-14), as they were put to the PI at the PI's request (P290): the set of all the solutions of a candidate; the functions by which a solution may be multiplied; the maps that carry a candidate to itself; the eleven numbers; and that the candidates bring no such thing and the first axiom asks nothing of the equation beyond what is given (option 1 of P223). What the first two would strike was argued by hand to the PI and is scripted nowhere. **Not blind (CLAUDE.md §5), told to the PI before the ruling (P290):** the architect proposed the shadow after the result of the fifth-axiom test (M2-W208) and one case of this test (the constant function $1$) had been seen; the reading was fixed in row P290 before the rest of the test was run. **Identification steps** (CLAUDE.md §5): (1) the choice itself; (2) that "closed" is "compact", from M1 l.512; (3) that the thing the equation brings is the shadow of one solution and not of all. M1, Definition 2.3, also asks "connected and without boundary" and a loop that does not shrink: the statement takes "compact" alone, and for a smooth state the shadow that is compact and not empty is the whole of time and space, which is connected and has no boundary. Not shown and not claimed: that no other thing could serve; anything for a state that is not smooth
*(M2-W208 is replaced by M2-W219, P292; flagged: this entry is unchanged, and where its mark names M2-W208, M2-W219 is meant)*

### M2-W215 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the candidates and the first axiom]\label{prop:first}
Let $D$, the candidates and the functions $h'$ and $h''$ be as in Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth} and its proof. (i) At each time, $h'$ and $h''$ taken for the name $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ of a point of $Y$ are $h'$ and $h''$ taken for its name $(p,s+3L)$, or their negatives, with one sign for $h'$ and one for $h''$ at all times. (ii) There are a smooth section $f$ of the twist with $\epsilon\,f\circ\iota=f$ and a smooth section $v$ of the twist with $\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=-v$, both pulled back from $Y$, both with $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$ zero on every fibre, and neither zero at every point of the fibre at any time. The function $f$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{0}=0$; $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$; the shadow of each is the whole of time and space. (iii) Every candidate that meets the fifth axiom meets the first axiom, and a candidate that is struck by the fifth axiom does not: the first axiom strikes no candidate that the fifth axiom keeps. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `select 190`; after M2-W213

- **Rests on:** M2-W214 (`def:A1eq`; the fourteenth choice); M2-W216 (its proof); M2-W208 (`prop:fifth`), M2-W210 (the functions), M2-W211; M2-W039 and M2-W048 (the two names of a point of $Y$; $z_{s}\circ M_{0}^{3}=z_{s+3L}$); M2-W175, M2-W183, M2-W187; M1 v7.3, Proposition 4.4 (the proof, l.442: $M_{0}$ on the half-lattice); Minimality M2-14
- **Mark:** CHECKED: `papers/M2/checks/T4b_fifth.py`, D1 ($M_{0}^{3}$ and its inverse are the identity modulo 2; exact), D2 and D3 (the functions made from the basis of the other name agree; **numerical, at sample points, and not a proof**), and `MEFProof/Paper_M2/M2_L16_fifth.lean` (`L16_M03`, `L16_signs`); the proof is M2-W216. Confirmed by read-back (P291), in the words printed here. **More is checked than the statement says, and the PI is told in the closing list:** the two signs of part (i) are both $+1$ (M2-W216, by the integers of $M_{0}^{-3}$; script D3; `L16_signs`), so that "or their negatives" and the last clause of (i) say less than is shown; the statement is logged as the PI confirmed it. ### M2-W216 · retired
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i), (ii) and (iii). Let $\omega_{1}$, $\omega_{2}$ be a basis of the lattice of the fibre at a time with $\omega_{2}/\omega_{1}$ not real. A function $\Phi$ on the complex numbers that is holomorphic away from the lattice, has at each point of the lattice a pole of order at most one, has $z\,\Phi(z)\to1$ at $0$, and has $\Phi(z+\omega_{1})=-\Phi(z)$ and $\Phi(z+\omega_{2})=\Phi(z)$, is the only such function: the difference $\Delta$ of two has no pole, $|\Delta|$ is unchanged by the lattice and so is bounded, a bounded function that is holomorphic on the whole plane is constant, and $\Delta(z+\omega_{1})=-\Delta(z)$ then gives $\Delta=0$. The series of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth}, written with $\omega_{1}$ for $\omega$ and $\omega_{2}$ for $iT$, is such a function, by the argument given there. At a point of $Y$ with the names $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ the two coordinates agree, $z_{s}(M_{0}^{3}p)=z_{s+3L}(p)$, and the functions for the second name are made, in that coordinate, from the basis $\omega_{1}'=z_{s+3L}(M_{0}^{-3}(0,1))$, $\omega_{2}'=z_{s+3L}(M_{0}^{-3}(1,0))$ in the place of $\omega_{1}=z_{s+3L}(0,1)$, $\omega_{2}=z_{s+3L}(1,0)$. Since $M_{0}^{-3}(0,1)=(-8,13)$ and $M_{0}^{-3}(1,0)=(5,-8)$, $\omega_{1}'=\omega_{1}+2\lambda_{1}$ and $\omega_{2}'=\omega_{2}+2\lambda_{2}$ with $\lambda_{1}=6\omega_{1}-4\omega_{2}$ and $\lambda_{2}=-4\omega_{1}+2\omega_{2}$. For $\lambda=m\omega_{1}+n\omega_{2}$ in the lattice $F(z+\lambda)=(-1)^{m}F(z)$, so $F(z+2\lambda)=F(z)$: $F$ has the two rules of sign for the basis $\omega_{1}',\omega_{2}'$ also, and by what was said it is the function made from that basis. The half-periods of the second name are $\omega_{1}'/2=\omega_{1}/2+\lambda_{1}$ and $c'=c+\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}$, so that $F(z-\omega_{1}'/2)=(-1)^{6}F(z-\omega_{1}/2)$ and $F(z-c')=(-1)^{2}F(z-c)$: $K_{1}$, $K_{2}$ and $K_{3}$ made for the second name are $K_{1}$, $K_{2}$ and $K_{3}$ made for the first, and so are $h'$ and $h''$, the sign being $+1$ for each. This is (i). For (ii): let $f(p,s)=h'(z_{s}(p))$ and $v(p,s)=h''(z_{s}(p))$, the functions being taken for the time $s$, for every real $s$; by (i) they have the same value at the two names of a point of $Y$. As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth}, with the function $1$ in the place of $\beta_{0}$: $f$ and $v$ are smooth sections of the twist, pulled back from $Y$, with $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$ zero on every fibre, $\epsilon\,f\circ\iota=f$ and $\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=-v$, and at no time is either zero at every point of the fibre; $f$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{0}=0$, and $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$; neither depends on the point of space or of the base, so each is not zero at some point over each point $(s,x)$, and its shadow is the whole of time and space. This is (ii). For (iii): time and space, the product of a circle and $S^{3}$, are compact, so the shadow of the solution of (ii) is compact, and the solution is not zero: a candidate with $a_{0}=0$ or with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$, which are the candidates that meet the fifth axiom (Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth}(iv)), meets the first axiom. A candidate struck by the fifth axiom has no solution other than zero among the smooth states (Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth}(ii)), and does not meet it. This is (iii).
```

### M2-W218 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
How the candidates are tested by Definition~\ref{def:A5eq}. The candidates are tested for the three divisors $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$ and $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:kept}(ii), for which every candidate is kept by the maps (Proposition~\ref{prop:kept}(iv)); they are not tested for $2P_{2}+P_{4}$ and $2P_{3}+P_{4}$, for which no candidate is said to be kept or struck. A solution is tested as a smooth state. Clause~(i) of Definition~\ref{def:A5eq} is read as it is printed: a candidate meets it if there are two smooth states $\psi_{1}$, $\psi_{2}$ that are solutions, with $\mu_{1}\psi_{1}+\mu_{2}\psi_{2}=0$ for complex numbers $\mu_{1}$, $\mu_{2}$ only when $\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}=0$, and whose shadows are the same set. It is not asked that the two differ only in the internal piece: a solution and its product with a function of time or of space may be the two. A candidate meets clause~(ii) if there is a smooth state that is a solution and whose shadow after one step is not the same set as its shadow. A candidate that does not meet clause~(i) is \emph{struck by clause~(i)}, and one that does not meet clause~(ii) is \emph{struck by clause~(ii)}. A candidate that is struck by clause~(i) or by clause~(ii) is \emph{struck by the fifth axiom}, and one that meets both is \emph{kept by the fifth axiom}. That clause~(i) is read as it is printed, and not with the words of M1, Definition~2.1(iii), ``states that differ only in the internal piece'', is a reading and is not derived: it is the thirteenth choice of this paper. Its ground is the words of Definition~\ref{def:A5eq} as they stand, and not a clause of M1, Definition~3.2.
```

### M2-W219 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the candidates and the fifth axiom]\label{prop:fifth}
Let $D$ be one of the three divisors $3P_{4}$, $2P_{1}+P_{4}$, $P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$, let the candidates be those of Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}, with their eleven numbers and their conditions (I) to (IV), and let them be tested as said above. A \emph{function of the time alone} is a function $g(p,s,r)=\gamma(s)$ on $\widetilde{M}$ with $\gamma$ smooth on $\R$ and of period $3L$. (i) If a smooth state $\psi$ is a solution of a candidate and $g$ is a function of the time alone, then $g\psi$ is a smooth state and a solution of the same candidate. (ii) If $a_{0}\neq0$ and one of $a_{1}$, $a_{2}$ is not zero, the only smooth state that is a solution is zero. (iii) Let $U$ be an open arc of the time circle of length less than $L$. There are a smooth section $f$ of the twist with $\epsilon\,f\circ\iota=f$ and a smooth section $v$ of the twist with $\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=-v$, both pulled back from $Y$, both with $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$ zero on every fibre, both zero at the times outside $U$ and not zero at some point of the fibre at each time of $U$. The function $f$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{0}=0$; $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$. The shadow of each is the set of the points $(s,x)$ with $s$ in $U$. (iv) A candidate with $a_{0}\neq0$ and one of $a_{1}$, $a_{2}$ not zero is struck by clause~(i) and by clause~(ii); it is counted as one with the candidate $\psi=0$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}(iv). Every other candidate, one with $a_{0}=0$ or with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$, meets the fifth axiom. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```
- **Place:** `select 140`; in the place of M2-W208, which it replaces
- **Step:** T4b

### M2-W220 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i) and (ii). A function $g$ of the time alone is well defined on $\widetilde{M}$, the two names $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ of a point of $Y$ having times that differ by $3L$; it is smooth, it is pulled back from $Y$, it is constant along each fibre, and the fold, which leaves the time as it is, leaves it unchanged. For a smooth section $\psi$ of $S_{4}$, $g\psi$ is of type $(0,*)$, $w_{i}^{a_{i}}g\psi=g\,w_{i}^{a_{i}}\psi$ extends smoothly, and $\epsilon\,\iota^{*}(g\psi)=g\,\epsilon\,\iota^{*}\psi$: so $g\psi$ is a smooth section of $S_{4}$, and it is a smooth state if $\psi$ is one. Its parts are $g\psi'$ and $g\psi''$. Since $g$ is pulled back from $Y$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}(g\psi')=g\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi'$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}(g\psi')=g\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi'$, and likewise for $\psi''$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(ii)); since $g$ is constant along the fibres, $\delta(g\psi')=g\,\delta\psi'$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(iii)), and $\delta(g\psi'')=g\,\delta\psi''$ in the same way, $\delta$ on $\psi''$ being the same derivative of the coefficients (Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}). So $\mathrm{I}(g\psi)=g\,\mathrm{I}(\psi)$, and likewise for $\mathrm{II}$, $\mathrm{III}$ and $\mathrm{IV}$: if (I) to (IV) hold for $\psi$ they hold for $g\psi$. This is (i). For (ii): let $a_{0}\neq0$ and let $\psi$ be a smooth state that is a solution. By Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}(iii), $\psi'=0$. If $a_{1}\neq0$, then $\psi''=0$ by the same part. If $a_{1}=0$ and $a_{2}\neq0$: with $\psi'=0$ the condition (III) is $a_{2}\psi''+b_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}\psi''+c_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{B}\psi''=0$, which is the condition (II) with the numbers $a_{2},b_{2},c_{2}$ in the place of $a_{1},b_{1},c_{1}$; the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:candidates}(iii) uses of (II) its form and that its first number is not zero, and it gives $\psi''=0$. In each case $\psi=0$. This is (ii).
```

### M2-W221 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii), the functions. In the coordinates $(\theta_{\psi},\theta_{\omega})$ of M1, Appendix~B, the fibre is $\R^{2}/\Z^{2}$ and the corners are $P_{1}=(0,0)$, $P_{2}=(\tfrac12,0)$, $P_{3}=(0,\tfrac12)$ and $P_{4}=(\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$ (M1, Proposition~4.4). At a time $s$ let $\varpi_{1}=z_{s}(0,1)$, $\varpi_{2}=z_{s}(1,0)$ and $\varpi_{3}=(\varpi_{1}+\varpi_{2})/2$, the points $(0,1)$ and $(1,0)$ being written in those coordinates. The coordinate $z_{s}$ is linear and one-to-one from $\R^{2}$ to the complex numbers and depends smoothly on $s$ (Definition~\ref{def:step} and the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}); so $\varpi_{1}$ and $\varpi_{2}$ depend smoothly on $s$, $\varpi_{2}/\varpi_{1}$ is not real, the fibre at $s$ is the complex numbers modulo $\Z\varpi_{1}+\Z\varpi_{2}$, and $z_{s}$ carries $P_{1},P_{2},P_{3},P_{4}$ to $0$, $\varpi_{2}/2$, $\varpi_{1}/2$ and $\varpi_{3}$. Let
\[
\Xi(z)\;=\;\sum_{n\in\Z}\frac{\pi}{\varpi_{1}\,\sin\bigl(\pi(z-n\varpi_{2})/\varpi_{1}\bigr)} .
\]
For real $a$ and $b$, $|\sin(a+ib)|\geq\sinh|b|$, and the imaginary part of $\pi(z-n\varpi_{2})/\varpi_{1}$ is that of $\pi z/\varpi_{1}$ less $n\pi$ times the imaginary part of $\varpi_{2}/\varpi_{1}$, which is not zero; so the series, and the series of the derivatives of its terms in $z$ and in $s$ of every order, converge uniformly on every compact set of pairs $(z,s)$ on which no term has a pole: $\Xi$ is smooth in $(z,s)$ and holomorphic in $z$ away from its poles. Replacing $n$ by $-n$ gives $\Xi(-z)=-\Xi(z)$; $\sin(u+\pi)=-\sin u$ gives $\Xi(z+\varpi_{1})=-\Xi(z)$; and replacing $n$ by $n+1$ gives $\Xi(z+\varpi_{2})=\Xi(z)$. The poles of $\Xi$ are the points $m\varpi_{1}+n\varpi_{2}$, $m$ and $n$ whole numbers, and near $0$, $\Xi(z)=z^{-1}\Xi_{0}(z)$ with $\Xi_{0}$ smooth in $(z,s)$ and $\Xi_{0}(0)=1$, the function $u/\sin u$ being smooth near $u=0$ with the value $1$ there and the terms with $n\neq0$ being smooth near $0$. At no time is $\Xi$ zero at every point, since it has a pole; nor is its derivative $\partial_{z}\Xi$, $\Xi$ being not constant. $\Xi(z-\varpi_{1}/2)$ and $\Xi(z-\varpi_{3})$ are even in $z$: $\Xi(-z-\varpi_{1}/2)=-\Xi(z+\varpi_{1}/2)=\Xi(z-\varpi_{1}/2)$, and $\Xi(-z-\varpi_{3})=-\Xi(z+\varpi_{3})=-\Xi(z-\varpi_{3}+\varpi_{1}+\varpi_{2})=\Xi(z-\varpi_{3})$; so $\partial_{z}\Xi(z-\varpi_{3})$ is odd. Let
\[
K_{1}(z)=\Xi(z)\,\Xi(z-\varpi_{1}/2),\qquad K_{2}(z)=\Xi(z)\,\Xi(z-\varpi_{3}),\qquad K_{3}(z)=\Xi(z-\varpi_{3})\,\partial_{z}\Xi(z-\varpi_{3}).
\]
Each is unchanged by $z\mapsto z+\varpi_{1}$, two signs cancelling, and by $z\mapsto z+\varpi_{2}$, so that it is a function on the fibre; each is odd; each is holomorphic in $z$ away from the corners and smooth in $(z,s)$ there; and at no time is it zero at every point. $K_{1}$ has poles at $P_{1}$ and $P_{3}$ alone, of order at most one; $K_{2}$ at $P_{1}$ and $P_{4}$ alone, of order at most one; $K_{3}$ at $P_{4}$ alone, of order at most three: with $u=z-\varpi_{3}$, $u^{3}\,\Xi(u)\,\partial_{z}\Xi(u)=\Xi_{0}(u)\bigl(u\,\partial_{z}\Xi_{0}(u)-\Xi_{0}(u)\bigr)$. In each case the function times the power of $w_{i}$ of that order extends smoothly in $(p,s)$ across the corner circle. For $D=3P_{4}$, where $\epsilon=+1$, let $h'=1$ and $h''=K_{3}$; for $D=2P_{1}+P_{4}$, where $\epsilon=+1$, let $h'=1$ and $h''=K_{2}$; for $D=P_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}$, where $\epsilon=-1$, let $h'=K_{1}$ and $h''=1$. The fold being $z\mapsto-z$ on each fibre, in each case $\epsilon\,h'\circ\iota=h'$ and $\epsilon\,h''\circ\iota=-h''$, and the poles of $h'$ and of $h''$ are bounded by $D$.
```

### M2-W222 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(iii), the states, and (iv). Name the times of $U$ by the numbers of an interval $(s_{1},s_{2})$ with $s_{2}-s_{1}<L$, and let $\beta_{0}$ be a smooth function on $\R$ of period $3L$ that is not zero exactly at the times of $U$. With $h'$ and $h''$ as above, let $f(p,s)=\beta_{0}(s)\,h'(z_{s}(p))$ and $v(p,s)=\beta_{0}(s)\,h''(z_{s}(p))$ at the times of $U$, the functions $h'$ and $h''$ being taken for the time $s$, and let $f$ and $v$ be zero at the other times. They are functions on $Y$ less the corner circles, smooth there, and they meet the conditions of Definition~\ref{def:smoothstate} at the four corner circles, the poles being bounded by $D$: $f$ and $v$ are smooth sections of the twist, pulled back from $Y$. On every fibre $\partial f/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$ and $\partial v/\partial\bar z_{s}=0$; $\epsilon\,f\circ\iota=f$ and $\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=-v$; and at each time of $U$ neither is zero at every point of the fibre. So $f$, a form of degree zero, is a smooth state with the parts $\psi'=f$ and $\psi''=0$; since $f$ is pulled back from $Y$, $\mathrm{d}_{\Sigma}f=0$ and $\mathrm{d}_{B}f=0$, and $\delta f=0$, so that $\mathrm{I}(f)=a_{0}f$ and $\mathrm{II}(f)$, $\mathrm{III}(f)$, $\mathrm{IV}(f)$ are zero: $f$ is a solution of every candidate with $a_{0}=0$. The form $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$ is a smooth section of $S_{4}$, and, since $\iota^{*}\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$, $\epsilon\,\iota^{*}(\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v)=-\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$: it is a smooth state with the parts $\psi'=0$ and $\psi''=v$, for which $\mathrm{I}$ is zero, $\mathrm{II}$ is $a_{1}v$, $\mathrm{III}$ is $a_{2}v$ and $\mathrm{IV}$ is $g_{1}\,\delta v=0$: it is a solution of every candidate with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$. Neither depends on the point of space or of the base, and $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}$ is zero at no point; so each is not zero at some point over $(s,x)$ exactly when $s$ is in $U$, and its shadow is the set of the points $(s,x)$ with $s$ in $U$. This is (iii). For (iv): let a candidate have $a_{0}=0$ or $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$, and let $\psi$ be its solution of (iii). The function $c(s)=\exp\bigl(\cos(2\pi s/L)\bigr)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:operations}(vi) is a function of the time alone with no zero, so that $c\psi$ is a solution by (i), with the same shadow as $\psi$. If $\mu_{1}\psi+\mu_{2}\,c\psi=0$, then $\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}\,c(s)=0$ at every time of $U$; $c$ takes two different values at two times of $U$, so $\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}=0$. The candidate meets clause~(i). The shadow of $\psi$ after one step is the set of the points $(s-L,x)$ with $s$ in $U$; $U$ has length less than $L$ and the time circle the length $3L$, so that $U$ moved by $-L$ has no time in common with $U$, and $U$ is not empty: the two sets are not the same, and the candidate meets clause~(ii). It meets the fifth axiom. Let a candidate have $a_{0}\neq0$ and one of $a_{1}$, $a_{2}$ not zero. By (ii) its one smooth solution is zero. Two solutions $\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}=0$ have $1\cdot\psi_{1}+0\cdot\psi_{2}=0$: the candidate is struck by clause~(i). The shadow of zero is the empty set, and so is its shadow after one step: the candidate is struck by clause~(ii). Its smooth solutions are those of the candidate $\psi=0$, with which it is counted as one (Definition~\ref{def:candidate}). This is (iv).
```

### M2-W223 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
\begin{proposition}[the candidates and the first axiom]\label{prop:first}
Let $D$, the candidates and the functions $h'$ and $h''$ be as in Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth} and its proof. (i) At each time, $h'$ and $h''$ taken for the name $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ of a point of $Y$ are $h'$ and $h''$ taken for its name $(p,s+3L)$. (ii) There are a smooth section $f$ of the twist with $\epsilon\,f\circ\iota=f$ and a smooth section $v$ of the twist with $\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=-v$, both pulled back from $Y$, both with $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$ zero on every fibre, and neither zero at every point of the fibre at any time. The function $f$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{0}=0$; $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$; the shadow of each is the whole of time and space. (iii) Every candidate that is kept by the fifth axiom meets the first axiom; a candidate that is struck by the fifth axiom has no smooth state other than zero as a solution, and among the smooth states does not meet the first axiom. \rigD
\end{proposition}
```

### M2-W224 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
(i), (ii) and (iii). Let $\varpi_{1}$, $\varpi_{2}$ be a basis of the lattice of the fibre at a time, so that $\varpi_{2}/\varpi_{1}$ is not real, and let $\Xi$ be the series of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth} made from it. If a function $\Xi_{1}$ on the complex numbers is holomorphic away from the lattice, has at each point of the lattice a pole of order at most one, has $z\,\Xi_{1}(z)\to1$ at $0$, and has $\Xi_{1}(z+\varpi_{1})=-\Xi_{1}(z)$ and $\Xi_{1}(z+\varpi_{2})=\Xi_{1}(z)$, then $\Xi_{1}=\Xi$: $\Xi$ has these properties, by the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth}, which uses of the basis only that $\varpi_{2}/\varpi_{1}$ is not real; the two functions have the same residue at each point of the lattice, by the two rules of sign; so their difference $\Upsilon$ has no pole, $|\Upsilon|$ is unchanged by the lattice and so is bounded, a bounded function that is holomorphic on the whole plane is constant, and $\Upsilon(z+\varpi_{1})=-\Upsilon(z)$ then gives $\Upsilon=0$. At a point of $Y$ with the names $(p,s+3L)$ and $(M_{0}^{3}p,s)$ the two coordinates agree, $z_{s}(M_{0}^{3}p)=z_{s+3L}(p)$ (the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:time}). Let $\varpi_{1}=z_{s+3L}(0,1)$ and $\varpi_{2}=z_{s+3L}(1,0)$, from which the functions for the first name are made. The functions for the second name are made, in the same coordinate, from $\varpi_{1}'=z_{s}(0,1)=z_{s+3L}(M_{0}^{-3}(0,1))$ and $\varpi_{2}'=z_{s}(1,0)=z_{s+3L}(M_{0}^{-3}(1,0))$, with $\varpi_{3}'=(\varpi_{1}'+\varpi_{2}')/2$. Since $M_{0}^{3}=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}13&8\\8&5\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$ (Definition~\ref{def:time}), $M_{0}^{-3}(0,1)=(-8,13)$ and $M_{0}^{-3}(1,0)=(5,-8)$, so that $\varpi_{1}'=\varpi_{1}+2\ell_{1}$ and $\varpi_{2}'=\varpi_{2}+2\ell_{2}$ with $\ell_{1}=6\varpi_{1}-4\varpi_{2}$ and $\ell_{2}=-4\varpi_{1}+2\varpi_{2}$. For $\ell=m\varpi_{1}+n\varpi_{2}$ in the lattice $\Xi(z+\ell)=(-1)^{m}\Xi(z)$, so $\Xi(z+2\ell)=\Xi(z)$: $\Xi$ has $\Xi(z+\varpi_{1}')=-\Xi(z)$ and $\Xi(z+\varpi_{2}')=\Xi(z)$, and by what was said, for the basis $\varpi_{1}',\varpi_{2}'$ of the same lattice, it is the series made from that basis. The half-periods of the second name are $\varpi_{1}'/2=\varpi_{1}/2+\ell_{1}$ and $\varpi_{3}'=\varpi_{3}+\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}$, with $\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=2\varpi_{1}-2\varpi_{2}$, so that $\Xi(z-\varpi_{1}'/2)=(-1)^{6}\,\Xi(z-\varpi_{1}/2)$ and $\Xi(z-\varpi_{3}')=(-1)^{2}\,\Xi(z-\varpi_{3})$, and likewise for $\partial_{z}\Xi$: $K_{1}$, $K_{2}$ and $K_{3}$ made for the second name are $K_{1}$, $K_{2}$ and $K_{3}$ made for the first, and so are $h'$ and $h''$. This is (i). For (ii): let $f(p,s)=h'(z_{s}(p))$ and $v(p,s)=h''(z_{s}(p))$, the functions being taken for the time $s$, for every real $s$; by (i) they have the same value at the two names of a point of $Y$. As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth}, with the function $1$ in the place of $\beta_{0}$: $f$ and $v$ are smooth sections of the twist, pulled back from $Y$, with $\partial/\partial\bar z_{s}$ zero on every fibre, $\epsilon\,f\circ\iota=f$ and $\epsilon\,v\circ\iota=-v$, and at no time is either zero at every point of the fibre; $f$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{0}=0$, and $\mathrm{d}\bar z_{s}\wedge v$ is a smooth state and a solution of every candidate with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$; neither depends on the point of space or of the base, so each is not zero at some point over each point $(s,x)$, and its shadow is the whole of time and space. This is (ii). For (iii): time and space, the product of a circle and $S^{3}$, are compact, so the shadow of the solution of (ii) is compact, and the solution is not zero: a candidate with $a_{0}=0$ or with $a_{1}=a_{2}=0$, which are the candidates that are kept by the fifth axiom (Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth}(iv)), meets the first axiom. A candidate that is struck by the fifth axiom has no solution other than zero among the smooth states (Proposition~\ref{prop:fifth}(ii)), and among the smooth states it does not meet the first axiom. This is (iii).
```

### M2-W225 · active
- **Statement (as it will be printed):**
```tex
What Proposition~\ref{prop:first} does not say. It is for smooth states and for the three divisors named. The solutions of part~(ii) do not depend on the point of space or of the base; whether a candidate has other solutions whose shadow is compact is not examined. Nothing is said of what the first axiom asks beyond the shadow: the fourteenth choice takes the shadow and no other thing that the candidates bring. Which of the eleven numbers are free is not said here.
```
